Concave transforms of compactified S S -metrized divisors
Debam Biswas, Yulin Cai

Abstract
. We associate a concave transform to any compactified S S -metrized divisor on a quasi-projective variety over an adelic curve. Then we show a Hilbert-Samuel type formula for relatively nef compactified S S -metrized YZ \mathrm{YZ} -divisors.



1991 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 14G40; Secondary 11G50

1.
Introduction

1.1.
Okounkov bodies in algebraic geometry

The notion of Okounkov bodies is introduced by Okounkov
[
Oko96
]
to study the volume of any ample line bundle on a projective varietiy which is generalized by Lazarsfeld-Mustaţă
[
LM09
]
to arbitrary
big
line bundles. Let
X
be a
d
-dimension integral projective variety over a field
K
,
L
a big line bundle on
X
and
V_{\bullet}=\{V_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
a
graded linear series
of
L
i.e. a graded sub-algebra of
\{H^{0}(X,mL)\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
. If
V_{\bullet}

contains an ample series
(see
Definition

3.5.2
), we can associate a concave set
\Delta(V_{\bullet})\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
V_{\bullet}
. The crucial property is that

(1)

\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}{m^{d}/d!}={d!}\cdot{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(V_{\bullet})).}

where
\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta)
denotes the Lebesgue measure of any measurable subset
\Delta\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
The left-hand side of (
1
) is denoted by
\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})
. If
V_{m}=H^{0}(X,L^{\otimes m})
, we set
\mathrm{vol}(L)\coloneq\mathrm{vol}(\{H^{0}(X,mL)\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}})
, called the
volume
of
L
. We have the classical formula

(2)

\mathrm{vol}(L)=L^{d}

which relates volume with the auto-intersection number of
L
.

1.2.
Concave transforms in local case for projective varieties

Let
K
be a valued field complete with respect to a norm
{|\hskip 4.30554pt|}_{v}
,
X
a projective variety over
K
and
L
a big line bundle on
X
. We denote by
X^{\mathrm{an}}
the Berkovich analytification of
X
, see
[
Ber90
, §3.4, §3.5]
. Let
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\varphi}
be a continuous metric on
L
. Then for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m\varphi}\coloneq{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\varphi}^{\otimes m}
is a metric on
L^{\otimes m}
and we have the
sup-norm

{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m\varphi,\sup}
on
H^{0}(X,L^{\otimes m})
:

(3)

{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m\varphi,\sup}\coloneq\sup\limits_{x\in X^{\mathrm{an}}}\{\|s\|_{m\varphi}(x)\}.

When
K=\mathbb{C}
, for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we set

\mathcal{B}(m\varphi)\coloneq\{s\in H^{0}(X,L^{\otimes m})\mid\|s\|_{m\varphi,\sup}\leq 1\}\ \text{ and }\ d_{m}\coloneq\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(H^{0}(X,L^{\otimes m})).

Nyström
[
Nys14
]
associated a convex function
c[\varphi]\colon\Delta(L)^{\circ}\to\mathbb{R}
to
\varphi
. Given another metric
\psi
of
L
, he was able to show that

(4)

\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{m}}}(\mathcal{B}^{\infty}(m\psi))}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{m}}}(\mathcal{B}^{\infty}(m\varphi))}\right)=2(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(L)^{\circ}}(c[\varphi](\lambda)-c[\psi](\lambda))\,d\lambda

For any arbitrary complete valued field
K
, using the method developed in
[
BC11
]
, by considering a superaddtive function associated to
(L,\varphi)
in
[
CM15
, §4.2]
, Chen-Maclean constructed a concave transform
G_{(L,\varphi)}\colon\Delta(L)^{\circ}\to\mathbb{R}
in
[
CM15
, Remark 4.4]
. Given another metric
\psi
of
L
, by
[
CM15
, Theorem 4.3]
, we have that

(5)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}\log\left({{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m\psi,\det}}/{{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m\varphi,\det}}\right)=(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(L)^{\circ}}(G_{(L,\varphi)}(\lambda)-G_{(L,\psi)}(\lambda))\,d\lambda,

see also
[
Séd23
, §2.4]
. We denote by
\mathrm{vol}(L,\varphi,\psi)
the value of the left-hand side of (
5
). If the metrics
\varphi,\psi
are
semi-positive
in the sense of
[
Zha95
]
when
v
is non-trivial, and are uniform limits of
Fubini-Study metrics
when
v
is trivial, then from the Bedford–Taylor theory
[
BT76
]
in the archimedean case, and from
[
CD12
, §5.6]
(or
[
Cha06
, Proposition 2.7]
when
v
is non-trivial and
K
has a countable dense subfield) in the non-Archimedean case, for any
0\leq j\leq d
we can associate a mixed Monge-Ampére measure

c_{1}(L,\varphi)^{j}\wedge c_{1}(L,\psi)^{d-j}

on
X^{\mathrm{an}}
. The
relative Monge-Ampère energy
is defined as

E(\varphi,\psi)\coloneq\frac{1}{d+1}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{d}\int_{X^{\mathrm{an}}}\log\left({{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\psi}/{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\varphi}}\right)c_{1}(L,\varphi)^{j}\wedge c_{1}(L,\psi)^{d-j}.

Then we have the equality

(6)

\mathrm{vol}(L,\varphi,\psi)=(d+1)\cdot E(\varphi,\psi)

which was proved in
[
BB10
, Theorem A]
for the Archimedean case (notice that if
X
is not smooth, we can pass to its resolution of singularity) and in
[
BGM21
, Theorem 1.1]
for the non-Archimedean case (or
[
BE21
, Theorem 9.15]
if
L
is semi-ample). Notice that the above results in
[
BGM21
]
can be applied in the trivially valued case. The formulas (
4
), (
5
) are local analogues of (
1
), and (
6
) is a local analogue of (
2
).

1.3.
Concave transforms in Arakelov theory for projective varieties

The framework of the construction of concave transforms was given by Boucksom-Chen
[
BC11
]
which is also used in the local case above. Let
X
be a
d
-dimensional projective variety over a field
K
and
L
be a big line bundle on
X
. Let
V_{\bullet}=\{V_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
be a graded sub-algebra of
\{H^{0}(X,mL)\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}

containing an ample series
(see
[
BC11
, Definition 1.1]
) and
\mathcal{F}
be a
multiplicative
filtration (see
[
BC11
, Definition 1.3]
) of
V_{\bullet}
. Boucksom-Chen
[
BC11
]
associated a concave transform
G_{\mathcal{F}}\colon\Delta(V_{\bullet})^{\circ}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying certain measure convergence property if
\mathcal{F}
is furthermore
pointwise bounded below
and
linearly bounded above
in the sense of
[
BC11
, Definition 1.3]
. When
K
is a number field with the ring of adéles
\mathbb{A}_{K}
and
V_{\bullet}=\{V_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is equipped with a multiplicative norm
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m}
on each
V_{m}
such that
\overline{V_{m}}\coloneq(V_{m},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m})
is an
adelically normed space
in the sense of
[
BC11
, 2.1]
. Set

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{m}\coloneq\{s\in V_{m}\mid\|s\|_{m,\omega}\leq 1\text{ for any $\omega\in\Omega$}\},

(7)

\displaystyle\widehat{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits}_{K}(\widehat{V}_{m})\coloneq\frac{1}{[K\colon\mathbb{Q}]}\log\#\widehat{V}_{m},

\displaystyle\chi(\overline{V_{m}})\coloneq\frac{1}{[K:\mathbb{Q}]}\log\mathrm{vol}(\widehat{V}_{m}),

where
\mathrm{vol}(\cdot)
is the Haar measure of
V_{m,\mathbb{A}_{K}}\coloneq V_{m}\otimes_{K}\mathbb{A}_{K}
normalized by
\mathrm{vol}(V_{m,\mathbb{A}_{K}}/V_{m})=1
and
\widehat{V}_{m}
is viewed as a subset of
V_{m,\mathbb{A}_{K}}
.
Notice that the norms
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m}
give a multiplicative filtration of
V_{\bullet}
, namely the
filtration by minima

\mathcal{F}_{\min}
(see
[
BC11
, 2.5]
). Hence we can associate a concave transform
G_{\mathcal{F}_{\min}}\colon\Delta(V_{\bullet})^{\circ}\to\mathbb{R}
to
\overline{V_{\bullet}}=\{(V_{m},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
. If
\mathcal{F}_{\min}
is linearly bounded above, then the measure convergence property implies that

(8)

\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits}_{K}(\widehat{V}_{m})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}=(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(V_{\bullet})^{\circ}}\max\{0,G_{\mathcal{F}_{\min}}(\lambda)\}\,d\lambda.

If
\mathcal{F}_{\min}
is furthermore
linearly bounded below
(in particular,
\inf\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta(V_{\bullet})^{\circ}}G_{\mathcal{F}_{\min}}(\lambda)>-\infty
), then

(9)

\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\chi(\widehat{V}_{m})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}=(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(V_{\bullet})^{\circ}}G_{\mathcal{F}_{\min}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda.

These two formulas (
8
), (
9
) are the arithmetic version of (
1
),(
4
), respectively. In particular, if
L
is endowed with a
model metric

{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\varphi}=({\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\varphi,\omega})_{\omega\in M_{K}}
(
M_{K}
is the set of places of
K
) i.e.
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\varphi}
induced by a
metrized

line bundle
on a projective
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-model of
X
(see
[
Zha95
]
)
and
V_{m}=H^{0}(X,L^{\otimes m})
with
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m}
given by the sup-norms defined in (
3
), then the left-hand sides of (
8
), (
9
), denoted by
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{L})
and
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{L})
, are called the
arithmetic volume
and the
arithmetic
\chi
-volume
of
\overline{L}\coloneq(L,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\varphi})
, respectively. Moreover, if
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\varphi,\omega}
is induced by a
semi-positive
metrized line bundle on some projective
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-model of
X
with underlying line bundle ample (in this case, the corresponding filtration
\mathcal{F}_{\min}
satisfies all properties above), we have the arithmetic Hilbert-Samuel formula (see
[
AB95
, Théorèm Principal]
or
[
Mor14
, Theorem 5.36]
):

(10)

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{L})=\overline{L}^{d+1},

where
\overline{L}^{d+1}
is the arithmetic intersection number given by
[
GS90
]
.

In
[
CM20
]
, Chen-Moriwaki proposed a new framework of Arakelov theory. They consider projective varieties over an
adelic curve
which is much more general than the classical cases of number fields or function fields. An
adelic curve
consisted of a field
F
and absolute values on
F
which are parameterized by a measure space
(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
similar to the notion of
M
-fields considered by Gubler in
[
Gub97
]
. Throughout this paper, the adelic curve is assumed to be
proper
which means
that the following product formula holds: for any
a\in F^{\times}
,

\int_{\Omega}\log|a|_{\omega}\,\nu(d\omega)=0.

More examples of adelic curves are given in
[
CM20
, §3.2.4-6]
. If
K
is endowed with a structure of adelic curve
S=(K,\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
with
\Omega
is discrete or
K
countable, let
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\varphi}=({\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\varphi,\omega})_{\omega\in\Omega}
be a family of metrics on
L
which is
dominated
and
measurable
in the sense of
[
CM20
]
, then for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
H^{0}(X,L^{\otimes m})
together with the sup-norms
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m\varphi,\sup}
is an
adelic vector bundle
over
S
, so the
Arakelov degree

\widehat{\deg}(H^{0}(X,L^{\otimes m}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m\varphi,\sup})
and the
positive Arakelov degree

\widehat{\deg}_{+}(H^{0}(X,L^{\otimes m}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m\varphi,\sup})
are well-defined
[
CM20
, Definition 4.3.7, §4.3.4]
. Write
\overline{L}\coloneq(L,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\varphi})
. After generalizing
[
BC11
, Theorem A]
to the superadditive filtrations (see
[
CM20
, Theorem 6.3.20]
), Chen-Moriwaki
[
CM20
, Theorem 6.4.6]
associated a concave transform
G_{\overline{L}}\colon\Delta(L)^{\circ}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying

(11)

\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}_{+}(H^{0}(X,L^{\otimes m}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m\varphi,\sup})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}=(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(L)^{\circ}}\max\{0,G_{\overline{L}}(\lambda)\}\,d\lambda.

If
\inf\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta*(L)}G_{(L,\varphi)}(\lambda)<\infty
, then

(12)

\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}(H^{0}(X,L^{\otimes m}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m\varphi,\sup})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}=(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(L)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{L}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda.

As the classical case, the left-hand sides of (
11
), (
12
) are denoted by
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{L})
,
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{L})
, called the
arithmetic volume
, the
arithmetic
\chi
-volume
of
\overline{L}
, respectively. In their subsequent works
[
CM21
]

[
CM22
]
, if
\overline{L}
is
relatively ample
in the sense of
[
CM22
, Definition 6.3.2]
, they defined the arithmetic intersection number
(\overline{L}^{d+1}\mid X)_{S}
as integrals of local intersection numbers
[
CM21
, Definition 4.4.3]
and prove a Hilbert-Samuel type formula
[
CM22
, Theorem B]
:

(13)

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{L})=(\overline{L}^{d+1}\mid X)_{S}

generalizing (
10
) to the adelic curve case.

1.4.
Arakelov theory for line bundles with singular metrics

The formulas we presented in the local and global case are for continuous metrics of line bundles over projective varieties. In arithmetic geometry, there are singular metrics appearing naturally, e.g. the Petersson metric of the Hodge bundle on a compactification of the moduli space of principally polarized abelian varieties of dimension
g\geq 1
. In
[
BKK07
]
, Burgos-Kramer-Kühn developed a systematic approach to arithmetic intersection theory for singular metrics with log-log singularities along the boundary.

Another approach was given recently by Yuan-Zhang
[
YZ21
]
. Let
K
be a number field or the function field of a curve over a field,
U
a quasi-projective variety. Let
X
be a projective compactification of
U
such that
B=X\setminus U
is a Cartier divisor. We take a positive
arithmetic model divisor

\overline{\mathcal{B}}
for
B
, then
\overline{\mathcal{B}}
induces a
model metrized divisor

\overline{B}\coloneq(B,g_{B})
, called a
boundary divisor
of
U
. A
compactified metrized line bundle

\overline{L}
is given by a line bundle
L_{U}
on
U
and a sequence of line bundles
L_{j}
on projective compactifications
X_{j}
equipped with model metrics
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{j}
satisfying the following conditions:

•

L_{j}|_{U}=L_{U}
for any
j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
;

•

let
s
be a rational section of
L_{U}
, and
s_{j}
the extension of
s
to
L_{j}
, then for any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
, there is
j_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
such that for all
i,j\geq j_{0}
,

-\varepsilon B\leq{\rm div}(s_{i})-{\rm div}(s_{j})\leq\varepsilon B\quad\text{and}\quad-\varepsilon g_{B}\leq\log\|s_{j}\|_{j}-\log\|s_{i}\|_{i}\leq\varepsilon g_{B},

where the inequalities take place after pulling back to a joint projective compactification of
U
.

Yuan-Zhang then defined the arithmetic intersection number for
arithmetically nef
(called
nef
in
[
YZ21
]
) compactified line bundles. Using the energy approach, Burgos-Kramer
[
BK24
]
could extend the arithmetic intersection number to
relatively nef
compactified line bundles which allowed them to define the arithmetic intersection number of the line bundle of Siegel–Jacobi forms with canonical metrics, and their extension covers the arithmetic intersection number defined in
[
BKK07
]
.
In
[
CG24
]
, Gubler and the second author generalized the arithmetic intersection numbers of compactified metrized line bundles in case where
K
is any field endowed with a structure of adelic curve. They also used the energy approach of Burgos and Kramer to extend the arithmetic intersection number to more singular metrics.

Yuan-Zhang worked on the geometric case in
[
YZ21
]
at the same time, and defined similarly the
compactified line bundles
and corresponding intersection theory on a quasi-projective variety over an arbitrary field. Let
L
be a compactified line bundle on
U
. We can define the space of global sections
H^{0}(U,L)
and the volume
\mathrm{vol}(L)
of
L
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{vol}(L)\coloneq\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(H^{0}(U,L^{\otimes m}))}{m^{d}/d!}

which is in fact a limit, see
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.2.1 (1)]
. Using the continuity of
\mathrm{vol}(\cdot)

[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.2.1 (2)]
, if
L
is
nef
in the sense of
[
YZ21
]
, then we have a Hilbert-Samuel type formula
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.2.2 (1)]
:

\mathrm{vol}(L)=L^{d},

where
L^{d}
is the auto-intersection number of
L
given by
[
YZ21
, Proposition 4.1.1]
.

In
[
Bis23a
]
, the first author followed the method in
[
LM09
]
and defined the Okounkov body
\Delta(L)
associated to
L
with the property

\mathrm{vol}(L)=d!\cdot\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(L)).

For the arithmetic case, let
\overline{L}
be a compactified metrized line bundle on
U
with underlying compactified line bundle
L
. As the projective case, using (
7
), we can define the
arithmetic volume

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{L})
of
\overline{L}
, see
[
YZ21
, Definition 5.1.3]
. Using the continuity of
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\cdot)
in
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.2.1 (2)]
, if
L
is arithmetically nef, then we have the following
arithmetic Hilbert-Samuel formula
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.2.2 (1)]
:

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{L})=\overline{L}^{d+1},

where
\overline{L}^{d+1}
is the arithmetic intersection number given by
[
YZ21
, Proposition 4.1.1]
.

In fact, before Yuan-Zhang’s theory, Berman-Freixas
[
BF14
, Theorem 1.1]
established an arithmetic Hilbert Samuel formula for semi-positive metrics with log-log singularity. Instead of considering sup-norms, they consider the
L^{2}
-norms at archimedean places.

1.5.
Main results

The goal of this paper is to use the theory of concave transforms along the lines of
[
BC11
]
and
[
CM20
, Chapter 6]
to study the arithmetic volumes and arithmetic
\chi
-volumes of compactified metrized divisors on quasi-projective varieties.

Let
S=(K,\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
be a proper adelic curve such that
\mathcal{A}
is discrete or that
K
is countable, and let
U
be a
d
-dimensional normal quasi-projective variety over
K
. We further assume that
S
satisfies the tensorial minimal slope property for some
C\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
in
2.5.1
. Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)
be a
compactified
S
-metrized divisor
of
U
in the sense of
3.3.3
. In
Section

4
, we associate a concave transform
G_{\overline{D}}\colon\Delta(D)^{\circ}\to\mathbb{R}
to
\overline{D}
if
D
is big, where
\Delta(D)
be the Okounkov body of
D
. The first main result is given as follows:

Theorem A

(
Theorem

4.3.2
)
.

Let
\overline{V_{\bullet}}=\{\overline{V_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
be the graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles associated to
\overline{D}
defined in
Definition

4.2.2
. If
D
is big, then

(14)

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}={(d+1)!}\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}\max\{G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda),0\}\,d\lambda,

and

(15)

\displaystyle\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}\leq(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda

with equality if
\widehat{\mu}_{\min}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>-\infty
, where
d\lambda
is the standard Lebesgue measure on
\Delta(D)\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

The left-hand sides of (
14
), (
15
) are denoted by
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})
,
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})
, called the
arithmetic volume
, the
arithmetic
\chi
-volume
of
\overline{D}
, respectively (we will show in
Proposition

6.2.4
that the notion arithmetic volume coincide with the one defined in
[
YZ21
, Definition 5.1.3]
if
S
is given by a number field). We denote
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})\coloneq(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda
which plays an important role in this paper. We also give applications of the concave transform
G_{\overline{D}}
, for example, we show following fundamental inequality in
Theorem

4.5.3
:

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{\text{$V\subset U$ open}}\inf\limits_{x\in V(\overline{K})}h_{\overline{D}}(x)\geq\frac{\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})}{(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}(D)},

and generalize the height inequality
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.3.5 (3)]
to the adelic curve case in
Theorem

4.6.1
.

In
Section

5
and
Section

6
, we assume that
S
is given by a number field or the function field of a curve. We consider the set
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef},\mathrm{YZ}}
of
relatively nef compactified
\mathrm{YZ}
-divisors

\overline{E}
(see
6.1.2
) such that there is
arithmetically nef compactified
\mathrm{YZ}
-divisor
(see
6.1.2
) whose underlying compactified divisor is the same as the one of
\overline{E}
. The arithmetic intersection number
(\overline{E}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}
of an element
\overline{E}
in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef},\mathrm{YZ}}
is well-defined, see
[
CG24
, Theorem 11.3]
. We then obtain the following Hilbert-Samuel type formula.

Theorem B

(
Theorem

5.2.2
)
.

If
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef},\mathrm{YZ}}
and
D
is big. Then

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{num}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}.

If moreover,
\widehat{\mu}_{\min}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>-\infty
, then

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}.

An obvious consequence is that
(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}>0
implies that
\overline{D}
is
big
in the sense of
Definition

4.2.2
, see
Corollary

5.2.4
.
Another application of concave transforms is the following equidistribution theorem generalizing
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.4.3]
and
[
Bis23b
, Theroem (C)]
.

Theorem C

(
Theorem

6.5.3
)
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{cpt}}
with
D
big. Let
(x_{m})_{m\in I}\subset U(\overline{K})
be a generic net of points which is small with respect to
\overline{D}
. Then for any
c\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
such that
\overline{D}(c)
is big, for any
v\in\Omega
and for any
f_{v}\in C_{c}(U_{v}^{\mathrm{an}})
, we have that

(16)

\displaystyle\lim_{m\in I}\frac{1}{\#O(x_{m})}\sum_{\sigma\in\text{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)}f_{v}(\sigma(x_{m}))=\frac{\langle\overline{D}(c)^{d}\rangle\cdot(0,f_{v})}{\mathrm{vol}(D)}.

(here
O(x_{m})
is the Galois orbits of
x_{m}
and
\langle\overline{D}(c)^{d}\rangle\cdot(0,f_{v})
is the positive intersection number defined in
[
Bis23b
]
, see also
6.4.1
.)

Notice that we don’t require
\overline{D}
to be arithmetically integrable which is a rather strong condition, see
[
CG24
, Example 7.13]
.

Notation

For any scheme
X
, we denote by
\Div(X)
the group of Cartier divisors on
X
. An algebraic variety
X
over a field
k
is defined as a geometrically integral separated scheme of finite type over
k
, and we denote by
k(X)
its function field.

Let
S=(K,\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
be an adelic curve, see
Definition

2.1.1
. We denote by
\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
the space of integrable functions
\phi:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
. For any
\omega\in K
, we denote
{|\hskip 4.30554pt|}_{\omega}
the absolute value corresponding to
\omega
and
K_{\omega}
the completion of
K
with respect to
{|\hskip 4.30554pt|}_{\omega}
.

For any vector space
V
over
K
, we denote
V_{\omega}\coloneq V\otimes_{K}K_{\omega}
. Similarly, for any algebraic variety
X
over
K
and any (
\mathbb{Q}
-)Cartier divisor
D
on
X
, we denote by
X_{\omega},D_{\omega}
the base change of
X,D
respectively.
For a measurable subset
\Delta
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\Delta^{\circ}
denotes the topological interior of
\Delta
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta)
denotes the Lebesgue measure of
\Delta
.

2.
Adelic curves and adelic vector bundles

2.1.
Adelic Curves

We begin by introducing the notion of
adelic curves
in this subsection. Our main reference is
[
CM20
]
.

Definition 2.1.1

(
[
CM20
]
§ 3.1)
.

Let
K
be a field and
M_{K}
the set of all absolute values on
K
. An
adelic structure on
K
is a measure space
(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
equipped with a map
\phi:\Omega\rightarrow M_{K},\ \ \omega\mapsto|\cdot|_{\omega}
such that for any
a\in K^{\times}
, the function
\log|a|_{\omega}:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is measurable. The data
(K,(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu),\phi)
(or simply
(K,\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
) is called an
adelic curve
.
Furthermore, an adelic curve
(K,(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu),\phi)
is said to be
proper
if the
product formula
holds: for any
a\in K^{\times}
,

\int_{\Omega}\log|a|_{\omega}\,\nu(d\omega)=0.

Remark 2.1.2
.

Given an adelic curve
(K,(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu),\phi)
, we fix the following notation throughout the paper:

\Omega_{\infty}:=\{\omega\in\Omega\mid\text{$\phi(\omega)$ archimedean}\},

\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}:=\{\omega\in\Omega\mid\text{$\phi(\omega)$ non-archimedean}\},

\Omega_{0}:=\{\omega\in\Omega\mid\text{$\phi(\omega)$ trivial}\}\subset\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}.

2.2.
Adelic vector bundles

Throughout the rest of this section, we fix a proper adelic curve
S=(K,\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
.
Recall that given a function
\psi\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
, we define its
upper integral
as

\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=6.2611pt,depth=-5.8611pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=4.87221pt,depth=-4.47221pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}}\!\int_{\Omega}\psi(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)\coloneq\inf\left\{\int_{\Omega}\phi(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)\mid\phi\in{\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)},\ \phi\geq\psi\text{ almost everywhere on }\Omega\right\}

and its
lower integral
as

\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=-2.94444pt,depth=3.34444pt\hfil}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=-2.94444pt,depth=3.34444pt\hfil}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.1215pt{\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=-2.3611pt,depth=2.7611pt\hfil}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.82639pt{\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=-1.97221pt,depth=2.37221pt\hfil}\hss}}}\!\int_{\Omega}\psi(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)\coloneq\sup\left\{\int_{\Omega}\phi(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)\mid\phi\in{\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)},\ \phi\leq\psi\text{ almost everywhere on }\Omega\right\}.

We say such a function
\psi
is
upper dominated
(resp.
lower dominated
) if
\psi
has a finite upper integral (resp. lower integral).

2.2.1
.

By an
S
-normed vector space
over
K
, we mean a finite dimensional vector space
V
over
K
equipped with a family of norms
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}=({\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega})_{\omega\in\Omega}
denoted by
\overline{V}\coloneq(V,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|})=(V,({\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega})_{\omega\in\Omega})
where
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}
is a norm on
V_{\omega}\coloneq V\otimes_{K}K_{\omega}
. Recall that given an
S
-normed vector space
\overline{V}
over
K
, we consider the dual vector space
V^{\vee}
along with dual norms
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}^{\vee}
of
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}
at each
\omega\in\Omega
, see
[
CM20
, §1.1.5]
. This gives us an
S
-normed vector space which we call the
dual
S
-normed vector space
of
\overline{V}
over
K
and denote it by
\overline{V}^{\vee}\coloneq(V^{\vee},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}^{\vee})
.

Let
\overline{V}
be an
S
-normed vector space over
K
. We say that
\overline{V}
is an
adelic vector bundle
on
S
if the following conditions hold:

(1)

it is
measurable
: for any
s\in V
, the function

\omega{(\in\Omega)}\mapsto\|s\|_{\omega}

is measurable.

(2)

it is
dominated
: for any
s\in V
and
t\in V^{\vee}
both the functions

\omega{(\in\Omega)}\mapsto\log\|s\|_{\omega}\ \text{ and }\ \omega{(\in\Omega)}\mapsto\log\|t\|_{\omega}^{\vee}

are upper dominated.

2.2.2
.

Let
V
be a vector space over
K
and
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}=({\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega})_{\omega\in\Omega}
,
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}^{\prime}=({\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}^{\prime})_{\omega\in\Omega}
be families of norms on
V
. For any
\omega\in\Omega
, set

d_{\omega}({\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}^{\prime})\coloneq\sup\limits_{s\in V_{\omega}\setminus\{0\}}\left|\log\|s\|_{\omega}-\log\|s\|_{\mathbf{e},\omega}\right|

called the
local distance
of
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}
and
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}^{\prime}
at
\omega
. The
global distance
of
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}^{\prime}
is defined as

d({\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}^{\prime})\coloneq\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=6.2611pt,depth=-5.8611pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=4.87221pt,depth=-4.47221pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}}\!\int_{\Omega}d_{\omega}({\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}^{\prime})\,\nu(d\omega).

2.2.3
.

Let
\overline{V}=(V,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|})
be an adelic vector bundle on
S
. We can construct the following spaces.

•

The
determinant

\det(\overline{V})
of
\overline{V}
is given by fixing the determinant
\det(V)
of
V
to be the underlying vector space and the norm to be the determinant norm at each
\omega\in\Omega
(see
[
CM20
, Definition 1.1.65]
). It is proven in
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.1.32]
that
\det(\overline{V})
is an adelic vector bundle of dimension
1
over
K
.

•

For a vector subspace
W
of
V
, we can restrict the norms of
\overline{V}
at each
\omega\in\Omega
to obtain a normed family with underlying vector space
W
which is called the
restricted norm
. The subspace
W
with the restricted norm is an adelic vector bundle by
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.1.32 (2)]
.

•

For a quotient vector space of
G
of
V
, we can consider
G
along with the quotient norms at every
\omega\in\Omega
, see
[
CM20
, §1.1.3]
, to construct a family of norms, called the
quotient norm
, whose underlying space is
G
. The quotient space with the quotient norm is an adelic vector bundle by
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.1.32 (2)]
.

Using the notation above, we are able to define the following invariants of
\overline{V}
.

(1)

The
Arakelov degree
of
\overline{V}
is defined as

\widehat{\deg}(\overline{V})=\widehat{\deg}(\det(\overline{V}))\coloneq\int_{\Omega}-\log\|s\|_{\det,\omega}\,\nu(d\omega),

where
s
is any non-zero section of
\det(V)
and it is well-defined since
S
is proper and
\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}\det(V)=1
.

(2)

The
positive Arakelov degree
of
\overline{V}
is defined as

\widehat{\text{deg}}_{+}(\overline{V})\coloneq\sup_{\overline{W}}\{\widehat{\text{deg}}(\overline{W})\},

where
\overline{W}
varies over all subspaces of
\overline{V}
equipped with restriction norms.

(3)

The
slope
of
\overline{V}
is defined as

\widehat{\mu}(\overline{V})\coloneq\frac{\widehat{\deg}(\overline{V})}{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V)}.

(4)

The
maximal slope
and
minimal slope
of
\overline{V}
are defined as

\widehat{\mu}_{\max}(\overline{V})\coloneq\sup_{\overline{W}}\{\widehat{\mu}(\overline{W})\}\ \text{and}\ \widehat{\mu}_{\min}(\overline{V})\coloneq\inf_{\overline{G}}\{\widehat{\mu}(\overline{G})\},

respectively,
where
\overline{W}
varies over all subspaces of
\overline{V}
with the restricted norms and
\overline{G}
varies over all quotient spaces of
\overline{V}
with quotient norms.

Let
\overline{M}=(M,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{M})
,
\overline{N}=(N,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{N})
be two adelic vector bundles, and
f\colon M\to N
a
K
-linear map. For any
\omega\in\Omega
, there is an induced map of normed vector spaces

f_{\omega}\colon(M_{\omega},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{{M},\omega})\to(N_{\omega},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{{N},\omega})

and we can consider its
operator norm

\|f\|_{\omega}\coloneq\|f_{\omega}\|
. Then we define the
height
of
f
as

h(f)\coloneq\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=-2.94444pt,depth=3.34444pt\hfil}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=-2.94444pt,depth=3.34444pt\hfil}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.1215pt{\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=-2.3611pt,depth=2.7611pt\hfil}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.82639pt{\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=-1.97221pt,depth=2.37221pt\hfil}\hss}}}\!\int_{\Omega}\log\|f\|_{\omega}\,\nu(d\omega).

2.3.
Purification

For our later application in the case of the classical global fields, we consider pure norms in order to relate our arithmetic volumes with the classical ones.

2.3.1
.

Let
\overline{V}=(V,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|})
be an
S
-normed vector space. For any
\omega\in\Omega\setminus\Omega_{0}
, we denote

\widehat{V}_{\omega}\coloneq\{s\in V_{\omega}\mid\|s\|_{\omega}\leq 1\}.

The
purification
of
\overline{V}
, denoted by
\overline{V}_{\mathrm{pur}}
, is the same underlying vector space
V
equipped with a family of norms
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur}}\coloneq\{{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}\}_{\omega\in\Omega}
defined as follows:

•

for any
\omega\in\Omega\setminus(\Omega_{0}\cup\Omega_{\infty})
and
0\neq s\in V_{\omega}
,

\|s\|_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}\coloneq\inf\{|\alpha|\mid\alpha\in K_{\omega}^{\times},\ s\in\alpha\widehat{V}_{\omega}\};

•

for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\infty}
and
0\neq s\in V_{\omega}
, we set

\|s\|_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}=\|s\|_{\omega};

•

for any
\omega\in\Omega_{0}
and
0\not=s\in V_{\omega}
,
\|s\|_{\omega}=1
.

Notice that
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}\leq{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}
for any
\omega\in\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\infty}
. Moreover it is not always true that
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}={\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}
in the case when
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
which leads to the following definition of purity.

Definition 2.3.2
.

For
\omega\in\Omega
, we say that
\overline{V}
is
pure at
\omega
if
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}={\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}
. We say that
\overline{V}
is
pure
if it is pure at each
\omega\in\Omega
.

Remark 2.3.3
.

Let
V=(V,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|})
be an
S
-normed vector space such that
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}
is ultrametric for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
. By
[
CM20
, Proposition 1.1.30 (3), Proposition 1.1.32 (2)]
,
\overline{V}
is pure at
\omega\in\Omega
if and only if
(V_{\omega},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega})
is pure in the sense of
[
CM20
, Definition 1.1.29]
. In particular, if
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
gives a discrete absolute value
{|\hskip 4.30554pt|}_{\omega}
on
K
, then the following statements are equivalent:

•

\overline{V}
is pure at
\omega
;

•

\|V\|_{\omega}=|K|_{\omega}
, see also
[
BMPS16
, Proposition 2.9]
;

•

{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}
is induced by some
lattice

\mathcal{V}_{\omega}
of
V_{\omega}
, see
[
CM20
, Definition 1.1.29, Proposition 1.1.30 (2)]
.

Here, by a lattice of
V_{\omega}
, we mean a sub-
K_{\omega}^{\circ}
-module
\mathcal{V}_{\omega}
of
V_{\omega}
generating
V_{\omega}
as a vector space over
K_{\omega}
with
\mathcal{V}_{\omega}
bounded in
V_{\omega}
for some norm of
V_{\omega}
, see
[
CM20
, Definition 1.1.23]
.

For further application, we give the following lemma.

Lemma 2.3.4
.

Let
\overline{V}
be an
S
-normed vector bundle. If
\overline{V}
is dominated, then for any
s^{\prime}\in V^{\vee}
, the function
\omega(\in\Omega)\mapsto\log\|s^{\prime}\|^{\vee}_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}
is upper dominated where
(V^{\vee},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur}}^{\vee})
is the dual of
\overline{V}_{\mathrm{pur}}
.

Proof.

Since
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}\leq{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}
, the identity on
V
induces a morphism of normed vector spaces

f_{\omega}\colon(V_{\omega},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur},\omega})\rightarrow(V_{\omega},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega})

for any
\omega\in\Omega
and clearly the operator norm
\|f\|_{\omega}\leq 1
. Then passing to the dual and using
[
CM20
, Proposition 1.1.22]
, for any
s^{\prime}\in V^{\vee}
and
\omega\in\Omega

\|s^{\prime}\|^{\vee}_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}\leq\|s^{\prime}\|^{\vee}_{\omega}

Since
\overline{V}
is dominated, we have that
\log\|s^{\prime}\|_{\omega}^{\vee}
is upper dominated, then so is
\log\|s^{\prime}\|^{\vee}_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}
by the above inequality using
[
CM20
, Proposition A.4.2 (2)]
.
∎

2.4.
Harder-Narasimhan Filtration

2.4.1
.

Let
\overline{V}
be an adelic vector bundle on
S
. The
Harder-Narasimhan filtration
of
V
is an
\mathbb{R}
-indexed non-increasing filtration
\mathcal{H}
on
V
defined as

\mathcal{H}^{t}(V)\coloneq\mathrm{Vect}_{K}(\{W\subseteq V\mid\widehat{\mu}_{\min}(\overline{W})\geq t\})

for
t\in\mathbb{R}
, where
\mathrm{Vect}_{K}(\cdot)
denotes the vector space generated over
K
by the subset, see also
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.3.46]
.
The
jumping numbers
of the Harder-Narasimhan filtration is defined as usual by

\widehat{\mu}_{i}(\overline{V})\coloneq\sup\{t\in\mathbb{R}\mid\text{dim}_{K}(\mathcal{H}^{t}(V))\geq i\}

for every integer
i\in\mathbb{N}
. By
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.3.46]
, the last slope of
\overline{V}
is exactly the minimal slope
\widehat{\mu}_{\min}(\overline{V})
defined in
2.2.3

(4)
. See
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.3.50, Proposition 4.3.51, Corollary 4.3.52]
for the relation between the jumping numbers and the Arakelov degree of
\overline{V}
.

2.5.
Graded algebra of adelic vector bundles

In this subsection, we introduce graded algebras where each of the graded pieces are adelic vector bundles. Later on we will introduce arithmetic volumes as the rate of asymptotic growth of positive degrees of such algebras. In order to have a satisfying theory of filtration, we need to introduce a technical condition on our base adelic curve. Note that given two adelic vector bundles
\overline{E}=(E,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{E})
and
\overline{F}=(F,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{F})
over an adelic curve
S
, we can define their
(\varepsilon,\pi)
-tensor product
by setting the norm at every
\omega\in\Omega
to be the
(\varepsilon,\pi)
-tensor product
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{{E},\omega}\otimes_{\varepsilon,\pi}{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{{F},\omega}
(see
[
CM20
, Chapter 4]
for more details). We denote this adelic vector bundle by
\overline{E}\otimes_{\varepsilon,\pi}\overline{F}
.

2.5.1
[
CM20
]
Definition 4.3.73, Definition 6.3.23.

Let
C\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. We say that
S
has

•

the
tensorial minimal slope property of level
\geq C_{0}
if for any two adelic vector bundles
\overline{E}
and
\overline{F}
on
S
, we have

\widehat{\mu}_{\min}(\overline{E}\otimes_{\varepsilon,\pi}\overline{F})\geq\widehat{\mu}_{\min}(\overline{E})+\widehat{\mu}_{\min}(\overline{F})-C_{0}\log(\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E)\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(F));

•

the
strong tensorial minimal slope property of level
\geq C_{0}
if for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
and any family
\{\overline{E_{i}}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
of adelic vector bundles on
S
, we have

\widehat{\mu}_{\min}(\overline{E_{1}}\otimes_{\varepsilon,\pi}\cdots\otimes_{\varepsilon,\pi}\overline{E_{n}})\geq\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\mu}_{\min}(\overline{E_{i}})-C_{0}\log(\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E_{i}));

•

the
Minkowski property of level
\geq C_{0}
if for any adelic vector bundles
\overline{E}
on
S
, we have

{\nu}_{1}(\overline{E})\geq\widehat{\mu}_{\max}(\overline{E})-C_{0}\log(\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E)),

where

\nu_{1}(\overline{E})\coloneq\sup\left\{t\in\mathbb{R}\mid\exists 0\not=s\in E\text{ such that }\int_{\Omega}-\log\|s\|_{\omega}\,\nu(d\omega)\geq t\right\}

is the
first
(
logarithmic
)
minimal
of
\overline{E}
,
see
[
CM20
, Definition 4.3.69]
for the more general definition.

Note that Chen-Moriwaki showed in
[
CM20
, Corollary 5.6.2]
that
S
has the tensorial minimal slope property of level
\geq\frac{3}{2}\nu(\Omega_{\infty})
when the underlying field
K
is perfect. In particular number fields and function fields of characteristic
0
are included in this formalism. When
S
is given by a regular projective curve over some field
k
, then
S
has the Minkowski property of level
\geq C_{0}
for some
C_{0}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, see
[
CM20
, Remark 4.3.74]
, which implies that
S
has the tensorial minimal slope property of level
\geq C_{0}
by
[
CM20
, Corollary 4.3.76]
.

Definition 2.5.2

(
[
CM20
]
Definition 6.3.24)
.

Let
R\coloneq\mathrm{Frac}(\overline{K}[[z_{1},\ldots z_{d}]])
be the field of formal power series. We call a
graded
K
-algebra of
S
-normed vector spaces
(
with respect to
R
) to be any family
\overline{E_{\bullet}}\coloneq\{\overline{E_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}=\{(E_{m},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
of
S
-normed vector spaces satisfying

(3)

\bigoplus\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}E_{\overline{K},m}T^{m}
is contained in a
\overline{K}
-subalgebra of finite type inside
R[T]
, where
E_{\overline{K},m}\coloneq E_{m}\otimes_{K}\overline{K}
;

(4)

for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m,\omega}
is ultrametric for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
;

(5)

for any
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
,
s_{1}\in E_{m}
,
s_{2}\in E_{n}
and
\omega\in\Omega
, we have

\|s_{1}\cdot s_{2}\|_{m_{1}+m_{2},\omega}\leq\|s_{1}\|_{m_{1},\omega}\cdot\|s_{2}\|_{m_{2},\omega}

A
graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles
is a graded
K
-algebra of
S
-normed vector spaces
\overline{E_{\bullet}}
such that each
\overline{E_{m}}
is an adelic bundle bundle.

Remark 2.5.3
.

Our definition here is slightly more general than
[
CM20
, Definition 6.3.24]
, with this definition, we are able to remove the assumption that
X
has a rational regular point
p\in X(K)
in
[
CM20
, Theorem 6.4.6]
as pointed out in
[
CM20
, Remark 6.3.29]
. In this paper, we will prove a similar result for quasi-projective varieties, but without this assumption, see
Theorem

4.3.2
.

Remark 2.5.4
.

Recall from
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.3.18]
that for any graded
K
-algebra
E_{\bullet}=\{E_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
\bigoplus\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}E_{\overline{K},m}T^{m}
is
\mathrm{Frac}(\overline{K}[[z_{1},\ldots z_{d}]])
where
E_{\overline{K},m}\coloneq E_{m}\otimes_{K}\overline{K}
, there is a minimal non-negative integer
0\leq\kappa\leq d
such that
\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E_{m})=\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E_{m,\overline{K}})=O(m^{\kappa})
,
i.e. there is
C\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that
|\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E_{m})/m^{\kappa}|<C
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, see
[
CM20
, Definition 6.3.9, Proposition 6.3.18]
,
and we call this integer the
Kodaira dimension
of
E_{\bullet}
.

We next introduce the
arithmetic volume
of a graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles.

Definition 2.5.5
.

Let
\overline{E_{\bullet}}
be a graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles with respect to
R\coloneq\mathrm{Frac}(\overline{K}[[z_{1},\ldots z_{d}]])
. We define the
arithmetic volume
and
arithmetic
\chi
-volume
of
\overline{E_{\bullet}}
as

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})\coloneq\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{E_{m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}

and

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})\coloneq\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}(\overline{E_{m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!},

respectively.

Definition 2.5.6
.

Let
\overline{E_{\bullet}}
be a graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles.
We define the
asymptotic maximal slope
and
asymptotic minimal slope
of
\overline{E_{\bullet}}
as

\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})\coloneq\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\mu}_{\max}(\overline{E_{m}})}{m}

and

\widehat{\mu}_{\min}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})\coloneq{\liminf\limits_{m\to\infty}}\frac{\widehat{\mu}_{\min}(\overline{E_{m}})}{m},

respectively.

Remark 2.5.7
.

Note that these quantities are not necessarily bounded. However we will show that for compactified divisors the asymptotic maximal slope is always finite and it turns out that under suitable positivity assumptions on the geometric divisor, the asymptotic minimal slope is also bounded.

2.6.
Over global fields

We close this section by considering the adelic vector bundles over a global field.

2.6.1
.

Let
C
be the spectrum of the ring of integers of a number field
K
or a smooth projective curve over some field
k
. Let
K
be the function field of
C
. We can define an adelic curve, denoted by
S
, as follows. Set
\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
the set of closed points on
C
and

\displaystyle\Omega_{\infty}\coloneq\begin{cases}\text{Hom}(K,\mathbb{C}),&\text{ if $K$ is a number field;}\\
\emptyset&\text{ if $K$ is the function field of a smooth curve over $k$.}\end{cases}

We equip
\Omega\coloneq\Omega_{\infty}\coprod\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
with the discrete
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{A}
. Then every
\omega\in\Omega
determines an absolute value on
K
in an obvious way. Let
\nu
be the measure on
(\Omega,\mathcal{A})
such that for any
\omega\in\Omega
,

\nu(\omega)=\nu(\{\omega\})\coloneq\begin{cases}\frac{[K_{\omega}\colon\mathbb{Q}_{\omega}]}{[K\colon\mathbb{Q}]}&\text{ if $K$ is a number field;}\\
[k(\omega)\colon k]&\text{ if $K$ is the function field of a smooth curve over $k$.}\end{cases}

Then
S
is proper.

In the following, we fix
C
, the spectrum of the ring of integers of a number field
K
or a smooth projective curve over some field
k
with function field
K
, and let
S=(K,\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
be the adelic curve associated to
C
given in
2.6.1
.

Classically, the
coherent

S
-normed vector spaces over
K
as defined in
Definition

2.6.3
are frequently studied. For a graded
K
-algebra of coherent adelic vector bundles
\overline{E_{\bullet}}
, the arithmetic volume
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})
can be defined in two ways, either using positive degree as we did in
Definition

2.5.5
or using small sections in
Definition

2.6.10
. We will show these two definitions of arithmetic volume coincide if
\overline{E_{\bullet}}
is
asymptotically pure
.

Recall from
[
CM20
, §1.2.3]
that for an
S
-normed space
\overline{V}=(V,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|})
, a basis
\mathbf{e}=\{e_{1},\cdots,e_{r}\}
of
V
is a
orthonormal basis
of
V_{\omega}
if

•

\|e_{i}\|_{\omega}=1
for any
i=1,\cdots,r
;

•

\|\lambda_{1}e_{1}+\cdots+\lambda_{r}e_{r}\|_{\omega}\geq\max\limits_{1\leq i\leq r}\{|\lambda_{i}|_{\omega}\}
for any
(\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{r})\in K^{r}
.

In particular, if
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
and
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}
is ultrametric,
then the second condition is equivalent to that
\|\lambda_{1}e_{1}+\cdots+\lambda_{r}e_{r}\|_{\omega}=\max\limits_{1\leq i\leq r}\{|\lambda_{i}|_{\omega}\}
for any
(\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{r})\in K^{r}
.

Example 2.6.2

(
[
CM20
]
Example 4.1.5)
.

Let
V
be a vector space over
K
and
\mathbf{e}=\{e_{1},\cdots,e_{r}\}
a basis of
V
. We can associate
\mathbf{e}
an
S
-norm
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathbf{e}}=\{{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathbf{e},\omega}\}_{\omega\in\Omega}
as follows: for any
s=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}e_{i}\in V_{\omega}=V\otimes_{K}K_{\omega}
, we set

\displaystyle\|s\|_{\mathbf{e},\omega}\coloneq\begin{cases}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r}|\lambda_{i}|_{\omega}&\text{ if $\omega\in\Omega_{\infty}$;}\\
\max\limits_{1\leq i\leq r}\{|\lambda_{i}|_{\omega}\}&\text{ if $\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}$.}\end{cases}

Then by
[
CM20
, Example 4.1.5]
, the basis
\mathbf{e}
as above gives a structure of adelic vector bundle which we denote by
\overline{V}_{\mathbf{e}}
. Moreover,
\overline{V}_{\mathbf{e}}
is pure by
[
BMPS16
, Proposition 2.9]
.

Definition 2.6.3
.

Let
\overline{V}=(V,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|})
be an
S
-normed vector space such that
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}
is ultrametric for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
. We say that
\overline{V}

(5)

(
[
BMPS16
, Definition 2.10]
) is
generically trivial
if there is a basis
\mathbf{e}\coloneq\{e_{1},\cdots,e_{r}\}
of
V
such that
\mathbf{e}
forms an orthonormal basis of
V_{\omega}
over
K_{\omega}
for all but finitely many
\omega\in\Omega
;

(6)

(
[
BMPS16
, Definition 4.4.4]
) is
coherent
if for any
s\in V
, we have that
\|s\|_{\omega}\leq 1
for all but finitely many
\omega\in\Omega
.

The generically trivial
S
-normed vector spaces and adelic vector bundles are closely related.

Lemma 2.6.4
.

Let
\overline{V}=(V,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|})
be an
S
-normed vector space such that
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}
is ultrametric for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
.

(7)

We have that
\overline{V}
is generically trivial if and only if there is a basis
\mathbf{e}
of
V
such that
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}={\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathbf{e},\omega}
for all but finitely many
\omega\in\Omega
. In particular, if
\overline{V}
is a generically trivial, then it is a coherent adelic vector bundle.

(8)

If
\overline{V}
is pure at all but finitely many
\omega\in\Omega
, then
\overline{V}
is an adelic vector bundle if and only if
\overline{V}
is generically trivial. In particular, in this case,
\overline{V}
is coherent by
(7)
.

(9)

If
\overline{V}
is a coherent adelic vector bundle, then the purification
\overline{V}_{\mathrm{pur}}
is a pure generically trivial adelic vector bundle.

Proof.

(7)
The first statement is from the definition. If
\overline{V}
is a generically trivial, by
[
CM20
, Corollary 4.1.10]
, then
\overline{V}
is an adelic vector bundle. On the other hand, notice that
\overline{V}_{\mathbf{e}}
is coherent for any basis
\mathbf{e}
, so
\overline{V}
is coherent by the first statement.

(8)
If
\overline{V}
is generically trivial, then it is an adelic vector bundle by
(7)
. For the converse statement, assume that
\overline{V}
is an adelic vector bundle. Let
\mathbf{e}
be a basis of
V
, and
\overline{V}_{\mathbf{e}}=(V,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathbf{e}})
the associated adelic vector bundle. Since
\overline{V}
and
\overline{V}_{\mathbf{e}}
are adelic vector bundles, by
[
CM20
, Corollary 4.1.8]
we have that

d({\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathbf{e}})=\sum\limits_{\omega\in\Omega}\sup\limits_{s\in V_{\omega}\setminus\{0\}}\left|\log\|s\|_{\omega}-\log\|s\|_{\mathbf{e},\omega}\right|<\infty

Now observing that all pure norms have discrete value groups in the non-archimedean places by
[
BMPS16
, Proposition 2.9]
, we deduce that
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}
and
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathbf{e},\omega}
must agree at all but finitely many
\omega\in\Omega
. This implies that
\overline{V}
is generically trivial by
(7)
.

(9)
Let
s\in V
. By the definition of the purification, for
\omega\in\Omega
, if
\|s\|_{\omega}\leq 1
, then
\|s\|_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}\leq 1
. Since
\overline{V}
is coherent, we have that
\|s\|_{\omega}\leq 1
for almost all
\omega\in\Omega
. So
\|s\|_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}\leq 1
for almost all
\omega\in\Omega
. Hence

\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=6.2611pt,depth=-5.8611pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=4.87221pt,depth=-4.47221pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}}\!\int_{\Omega}\log\|s\|_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}\,\nu(d\omega)<\infty.

Now noting that the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{A}
is discrete, by
Lemma

2.3.4
we can easily conclude that
\overline{V}_{\mathrm{pur}}
is an adelic vector bundle. Moreover,
\overline{V}_{\mathrm{pur}}
is generically trivial by
(8)
.
∎

Definition 2.6.5
.

Let
\overline{V}=(V,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|})
be an
S
-norm vector space such that
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}
is ultrametric for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
. For any
\omega\in\Omega
, notice that
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}\leq{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}
. We set

\sigma_{\omega}(\overline{V})\coloneq d_{\omega}({\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur}})=\sup\limits_{s\in V_{\omega}\setminus\{0\}}(\log\|s\|_{\omega}-\log\|s\|_{\mathrm{pur},\omega})

The
impurity
of
\overline{V}
is defined as

{\sigma}(\overline{V})\coloneq d({\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur}})=\sum\limits_{\omega\in\Omega}\sigma_{\omega}(\overline{V})\in[0,+\infty].

From the definition,
\overline{V}
is pure if and only
\sigma(\overline{V})=0
.
By
Lemma

2.6.4

(9)
and
[
CM20
, Corollary 4.1.8]
, if
\overline{V}
is a coherent adelic vector bundle, then
{\sigma}(\overline{V})<+\infty
.

Remark 2.6.6
.

Keep the notation in
Definition

2.6.5
. If
K
is a number field and
\overline{V}
is coherent, then the impurity
\sigma(\overline{V})
is exactly the one defined in
[
CM20
, §4.4.3]
. We explain the reason as follows. Set

\mathcal{V}\coloneq\{s\in V\mid\|s\|_{\omega}\leq 1\text{ for all $\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}$}\}.

Since
\overline{V}
is coherent, by
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.4.2 (2), Remark 4.4.1 (2)]
, for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
, we have that

\mathcal{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}K_{\omega}^{\circ}=\{x\in V_{\omega}\mid\|s\|_{\omega}\leq 1\}.

By definition of purification, the norm
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}
is exactly the one arising from
\mathcal{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}K_{\omega}^{\circ}
in
[
CM20
, Definition 1.1.23]
. This implies our claim by definitions of impurity.

As
[
BMPS16
, Definition 2.14, Definition 2.18]
, we have the following definition.

Definition 2.6.7
.

Let
\overline{V}=(V,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|})
be a coherent
S
-normed vector space. We define the space of its
small sections
as

\widehat{V}\coloneq\{s\in V\mid\|s\|_{\omega}\leq 1\ \text{for all}\ \omega\in\Omega\}

and define

\widehat{h}^{0}(\overline{V})\coloneq\begin{cases}\log(\#\widehat{V})&\text{if $K$ is a number field}\\
\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{k}(\widehat{V})&\text{if $K$ is the function field of a curve of a field $k$}\end{cases}

We further assume that
\overline{V}
is generically trivial.

•

Assume that
K
is a function field of curve over a field
k
. Let
\mathbf{b}
be a basis of
V
over
K
. For any
\omega\in\Omega
, we choose a basis
\mathbf{b}_{\omega}
of
\widehat{V}_{\omega}\coloneq\{s\in V_{\omega}\mid\|s\|_{\omega}\leq 1\}
over
K_{\omega}^{\circ}
. The
Euler characteristic
of
\overline{V}
is defined as

(17)

\displaystyle\chi(\overline{V})\coloneq\sum\limits_{\omega\in\Omega}\nu(\omega)\log|\det(\mathbf{b}_{\omega}/\mathbf{b})|_{\omega},

where
\mathbf{b}_{\omega}/\mathbf{b}
denotes the matrix of
\mathbf{b}_{\omega}
with respect to the basis
\mathbf{b}
. This quantity does not depend on the choice of bases.

•

Assume that
K
is a number field. Let
\mathbb{A}_{K}
be its ring of adeles and
V_{\mathbb{A}_{K}}\coloneq V\otimes_{K}\mathbb{A}_{K}
. The
Euler characteristic
of
\overline{V}
is defined as

(18)

\displaystyle\chi(\overline{V})\coloneq\frac{1}{[K:\mathbb{Q}]}\log\mathrm{vol}(\widehat{V}),

where
\widehat{V}\subseteq V_{\mathbb{A}_{K}}
is viewed as the adelic unit ball with respect to the norms
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}
and
\mathrm{vol}(\cdot)
denotes the Haar measure on
V_{\mathbb{A}_{K}}
normalized by

\mathrm{vol}(V_{\mathbb{A}_{K}}/V)=1
.

Remark 2.6.8
.

Let
\overline{V}
be a generically trivial adelic vector bundle. If
K
is a function field, by
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.3.18]
, we have that
\widehat{\deg}(\overline{V})=\chi(\overline{V})
.
If
K
is a number field, the expression (
18
) for
\chi(\overline{V})
can have a similar formula as (
17
), see
[
BMPS16
, Remark 2.20]
. Unlike the function field case,
\widehat{\deg}(\overline{V})\not=\chi(\overline{V})
in general. However, we have that

\widehat{\deg}(\overline{V})=\chi(\overline{V})+O(\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V)\log(\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V)))

see
[
BC11
, (3.5)]
.

Remark 2.6.9
.

Let
\overline{V}=(V,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|})
be a coherent adelic vector bundle. By the definition of purification, for any
s\in V
and
\omega\in\Omega
, we have that
\|s\|_{\omega}\leq 1
if and only if
\|s\|_{\mathrm{pur},\omega}\leq 1
. Hence

\widehat{h}^{0}(\overline{V})=\widehat{h}^{0}(\overline{V}_{\mathrm{pur}}).

If furthermore,
\overline{V}
is generically trivial, by
[
BMPS16
, Proposition 2.23]
, then

\chi(\overline{V})=\chi(\overline{V}_{\mathrm{pur}}).

Next, we consider the classical arithmetic volume of a graded
K
-algebra of coherent
S
-normed vector spaces.

Definition 2.6.10
.

Let
\overline{E_{\bullet}}
be a graded
K
-algebra of coherent
S
-normed vector spaces (i.e.
\overline{E_{m}}
is coherent for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
).
We define the
classical arithmetic volume

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})\coloneq\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{h}^{0}(\overline{E_{m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!},

where
d
is the Kodaira dimension of the graded
K
-algebra
E_{\bullet}
.

We further assume that
\overline{E_{m}}
is generically trivial for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
. We define the
classical arithmetic
\chi
-volume

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})\coloneq\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{\chi(\overline{E_{m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}.

Definition 2.6.11

(
[
CM20
]
Definition 7.5.1)
.

Let
\overline{E_{\bullet}}
be a graded
K
-algebra of coherent
S
-normed vector spaces. We say that
\overline{E_{\bullet}}
is
asymptotically pure
if

\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\sigma(\overline{E_{m}})}{m}=0.

Proposition 2.6.12
.

Let
\overline{E_{\bullet}}
be a graded
K
-algebra of coherent (resp. generically trivial) adelic vector bundles. One has the following inequality:

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})\text{ (resp. $\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})$)},

the equality holds if
\overline{E_{\bullet}}
is asymptotically pure.

Proof.

We first consider the arithmetic volume. Let
\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},m}}=(E_{m},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur},m})
be the purification of
\overline{E_{m}}=(E_{m},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m})
. Since each
\overline{E_{m}}
is a coherent adelic vector bundle, by
Lemma

2.6.4

(9)
,
\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},\bullet}}\coloneq\bigoplus\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},m}}
is a graded
K
-algebra of pure generically trivial adelic vector spaces. By
Remark

2.6.8
,
\widehat{h}^{0}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},m}})=\widehat{h}^{0}(\overline{E_{m}})
and hence
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},\bullet}})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})
. On the other hand, by
[
BMPS16
, Proposition 2.13]
,
\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},m}}
is the adelic vector bundle associated to some Hermitian vector bundle
\overline{\mathcal{E}_{m}}
on
C
if
K
is a number field or to some vector bundle
\mathcal{E}_{m}
on
C
if
K
is the function field of a curve. By
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.3.23, Proposition 4.3.24]
, we have that

(19)

\displaystyle|\widehat{h}^{0}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},m}})-\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},m}})|\leq C_{1}\cdot\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E_{m})\log(\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E_{m}))

for some constant
C_{1}>0
dependent only on
K
. Combine (
19
) and the fact that
\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E_{m})}{m^{d}}
is bounded when
m\to\infty
, we have that
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},\bullet}})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},\bullet}})
. It remains to compare
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},\bullet}})
and
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})
. Notice that

(20)

e^{-\sigma_{\omega}(\overline{E_{m}})}{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur},m,\omega}\leq{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m,\omega}\leq{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur},m,\omega}

for any
\omega\in\Omega
. By
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.3.21]
, we have that

\displaystyle\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},m}})\leq\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{E_{m}})

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\deg}_{+}(E_{m},(e^{-\sigma_{\omega}(\overline{E_{m}})}{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathrm{pur},m,\omega})_{\omega\in\Omega})

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},m}})+\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E_{m})\sigma(\overline{E_{m}}).

Hence
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},\bullet}})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})
and

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},m}})+\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E_{m})\sigma(\overline{E_{m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},\bullet}})+(d+1)!\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E_{m})\sigma(\overline{E_{m}})}{m^{d+1}}.

Again, notice that
\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E_{m})}{m^{d}}
is bounded when
m\to\infty
, if
\overline{E_{\bullet}}
is asymptotically pure, i.e.
\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\sigma(\overline{E_{m}})}{m}=0
, then
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},\bullet}})
. This completes the proof for arithmetic volume.

For the arithmetic
\chi
-volume, the proof is similar. Assume that each
\overline{E_{m}}
is generically trivial, we keep the notation as before. By
[
BMPS16
, Proposition 2.23]
, we have that
\chi(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},m}})=\chi(\overline{E_{m}})
and hence
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},\bullet}})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})
. By
Remark

2.6.8
, we have that

(21)

\displaystyle|\chi(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},m}})-\widehat{\deg}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},m}})|\leq C_{2}\cdot\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E_{m})\log(\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E_{m}))

for some constant
C_{2}>0
dependent only on
K
. This implies that
\mathrm{vol}_{\chi}(E_{\mathrm{pur},\bullet})=\mathrm{vol}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(E_{\mathrm{pur},\bullet})
. It remains to compare
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},\bullet}})
and
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})
. By (
20
), we have

\widehat{\deg}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},m}})\leq\widehat{\deg}(\overline{E_{m}})\leq\widehat{\deg}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},m}})+\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(E_{m})\sigma(\overline{E_{m}}).

As above, this implies that
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{E_{\mathrm{pur},\bullet}})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})
and the equality holds if
\overline{E_{\bullet}}
is asymptotically pure, which completes the proof.
∎

3.
Review of compactified
S
-metrized divisors

In this section, we recall the notion of compactified
S
-metrized divisors on quasi-projective varieties over adelic curves defined in
[
CG24
]
. Recall that given a
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space
M
, and a subset
N
, the the closure
\overline{N}
of
N
with respect to the
finite subspace topology
is given by
\overline{N}=\varinjlim\limits_{E}\overline{(N\cap E)}
, where
E
runs through all finite dimensional subspaces of
M
and
\overline{(N\cap E)}
is the closure of
N\cap E
in
E
with respect to the canonical euclidean topology.

3.1.
Compactified (geometric) divisors

3.1.1
.

Let
X
be a projective variety over a field
K
. We set
N_{\mathrm{gm}}(X)
the set of nef divisors in
\Div(X)
, and
N_{\mathrm{gm},\mathbb{Q}}(X)
the cone in
\Div_{\mathbb{Q}}(X)\coloneq\Div(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
generated by
N_{\mathrm{gm}}(X)
. For a quasi-projective variety
U
over
K
, a (
geometric
)
boundary divisor
of
U
is a pair
({X}_{0},B)
consisting of a projective
K
-model
U\hookrightarrow X_{0}
and an effective divisor
B\in{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}({X}_{0})
such that
|B|={X}_{0}\setminus U
. We set

(22)

\Div_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}\coloneq\varinjlim_{X}\Div_{\mathbb{Q}}(X),\ \ N_{\mathrm{gm},\mathbb{Q}}(U)\coloneq\varinjlim_{X}N_{\mathrm{gm},\mathbb{Q}}(X),

where
X
ranges over all projective
K
-models of
U
. Let
(X_{0},B)
be a boundary divisor of
U
. The (
B
-
)
boundary topology
on
\Div_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
is defined such that a basis of neighborhoods of a divisor
D
is given by

\displaystyle B(r,D)\coloneq\{E\in{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)\mid-rB\leq{E}-{D}\leq rB\},\ \ r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}.

Notice that the boundary topology is independent of the choice of
(X_{0},B)
. We write
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
(resp.
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{snef}}
) the completion of
\Div_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
(resp.
{N}_{\mathrm{gm},\mathbb{Q}}(U)
) with respect to the boundary topology and
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{int}}\coloneq\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{snef}}-\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{snef}}
. Moreover, write
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{nef}}
for the closure of
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{snef}}
in
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{int}}
with respect to the finite subspace topology. An element in
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
is called a
compactified
(
geometric
)
divisor
, we have a natural map

\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}\to\Div_{\mathbb{Q}}(U),\ D\mapsto D|_{U}.

For
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, we say
D
is
strongly nef
(resp.
nef
,
integrable
) if
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{snef}}
(resp.
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{nef}}
,
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{int}}
).
We have a symmetric multilinear map

\underbrace{\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{int}}\times\cdots\times\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{int}}}_{d\text{-times}}\to\mathbb{R},\ \ ({D_{1}},\cdots,{D_{d}})\mapsto{D_{1}}\cdots{D_{d}},

see
[
YZ21
, Proposition 4.1.1]
. For simplicity, for
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{int}}
we denote
\deg_{D}(U)\coloneq D^{d}
.

Remark 3.1.2
.

In
[
CG24
, Definition 3.4]
, the space
\Div_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
defined to be
\varinjlim_{X}\Div_{\mathbb{Q}}(X)
, where
X
ranges over all proper
K
-models of
U
(although
U
is quasi-projective), so the completion
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
in
[
CG24
]
may be larger than the one defined above. In the local and global cases below, the same situation happens. The reason why we only consider projective
K
-models is that the ampleness condition in
Definition

3.5.2
is crucial in our paper and that the results in
[
YZ21
]
are induced from the ones in projective case.

3.1.3
.

Let
U
be a
d
-dimensional normal quasi-projective variety over a field
K
and
{D}\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. Notice that an element
f
in the function field
K(U)
of
U
can determine a compactified divisor denoted by
\mathrm{div}(f)
. We can introduce the space of
global sections
of
\overline{D}
as

H^{0}(U,{D})\coloneq\{f\in K(U)^{\times}\mid\mathrm{div}(f)+{D}\geq 0\}\cup\{0\}

which is a
K
-vector space since
U
is normal by
[
CM20
, Proposition 2.4.13 (6)]
(on projective
K
-models of
U
). The set
H^{0}(U,{D})
is not necessarily stable under addition if
U
is not normal, see
[
CM20
, Example 2.4.15]
.
We have a natural injective map
H^{0}(U,D)\hookrightarrow H^{0}(U,D|_{U})
. If
D
is induced by a divisor on some projective model
X
of
U
which still denoted by
D
, then
H^{0}(X,D)\subset H^{0}(U,D)
, the equality holds if
X
is integrally closed in
U
by
[
YZ21
, Lemma 2.3.7]
. We have the following properties:

(6)

(
[
YZ21
, Lemma 5.1.6, Theorem 5.2.1 (1)]
) The
K
-vector space
H^{0}(U,{D})
has finite dimension and the limit

\mathrm{vol}(D)\coloneq\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(H^{0}(U,m{D}))}{m^{d}/d!}

exists;

(7)

(
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.2.1 (2), Theorem 5.2.9]
) If
D_{n}
converges to
D
in
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
with respect to the boundary topology, then
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\mathrm{vol}(D_{n})=\mathrm{vol}(D)
.

We call
\mathrm{vol}(D)
the
volume
of
D
. We say that
{D}\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
is
big
if
{\mathrm{vol}}(D)>0
. We refer to
[
YZ21
, §5.1–5.2]
for details and various properties of volumes.

3.2.
Local theory: mixed Monge-Ampère measures

In this subsection, we fix a field
K
with an absolute value
{|\hskip 4.30554pt|}_{v}
and a
d
-dimensional quasi-projective variety
U
over
K
. We denote by
U^{\mathrm{an}}=U_{v}^{\mathrm{an}}
the Berkovich analytification of
U
with respect to the place
v
, see
[
Ber90
, §3.4–3.5]
.

3.2.1
.

For a
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier divisor
D\in\Div_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)\coloneq\Div(U)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
,
D
can be written by local equations
f\in\mathcal{O}_{U}(V)^{\times}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
. Then we get a well-defined continuous real function
\log|f|
on
V^{\mathrm{an}}
for such open subsets
V
. A
Green function
for
D
is a continuous real function
g_{D}
such that on
(U\setminus|D|)^{\mathrm{an}}
such that for any local equation
f
of
D
on an open subset
V
of
U
, we have that
g_{D}+\log|f|
is a continuous function on
V^{\mathrm{an}}
.

We denote by
\widehat{\Div}(U)
(resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)
) the group of pairs
(D,g)
with
D\in\Div(U)
(resp.
D\in\Div_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)
) and
g
a Green function for
D
. Obviously, we have that
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)\simeq\widehat{\Div}(U)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
.

3.2.2
.

For a projective variety
X
over
K
, we denote
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}
the group of
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier divisors with
model
Green functions,
N_{\mathrm{mo},\mathbb{Q}}(X)
(resp.
N_{\mathrm{tFS},\mathbb{Q}}(X)
) the cone in
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(X)
generated by the Cartier divisors with
semi-positive
model Green functions (resp.
\mathrm{tFS}
-Green functions) and
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{tFS}}\coloneq N_{\mathrm{tFS},\mathbb{Q}}(X)-N_{\mathrm{tFS},\mathbb{Q}}(X)
.

3.2.3
.

Let
U
be a quasi-projective variety over
K
. Assume that
v
is non-trivial. A
boundary divisor
of
U
is a pair
({X}_{0},\overline{B})
consisting of a projective
K
-model
U\hookrightarrow X_{0}
and an effective divisor
\overline{B}=(B,g_{B})\in\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}({X}_{0})_{\mathrm{mo}}
such that
|B|={X}_{0}\setminus U
. We set

(23)

\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}\coloneq\varinjlim_{X}\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}},\ \ N_{\mathrm{mo},\mathbb{Q}}(U)\coloneq\varinjlim_{X}N_{\mathrm{mo},\mathbb{Q}}(X)

where
X
ranges over all projective
K
-models of
U
. Then a boundary divisor
(X_{0},B)
gives a
boundary topology
on
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
as
3.1.1
which is independent of the choice of
(X_{0},B)
. We denote by
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
(resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{snef}}
) the completion of
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
(resp.
N_{\mathrm{mo},\mathbb{Q}}(U)
) with respect to the boundary topology and
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{int}}\coloneq\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{snef}}-\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{snef}}
. Moreover, we denote by
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{nef}}
the closure of
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{snef}}
in
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{int}}
with respect to the finite subspace topology.
When
v
is trivial, we can do a similar procedure on
(\widehat{\Div}_{\mathrm{tFS},\mathbb{Q}},N_{\mathrm{tFS}})
, the corresponding spaces are also denoted similarly. An element
\overline{D}
in
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)
is called a
compactified metrized divisor
on
U
, it is called
strongly nef
(resp.
nef
) if
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{snef}}
(resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{nef}}
). We have a homomorphism

\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}

and an injective homomorphism

\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U),

see
[
CG24
, 4.20]
. So an element in
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
can be uniquely written as
(D,g)
with
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
and
g
a Green function for
D|_{U}
.

There is a unique way to associate to any
d
-dimension quasi-projective
U
over
K
and to a family
\overline{D_{1}},\dots,\overline{D_{d}}\in\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{nef}}
a positive Radon measure
c_{1}(\overline{D_{1}})\wedge\cdots\wedge c_{1}(\overline{D_{d}})
on
U^{\mathrm{an}}
satisfying multilinearity, symmetry, continuity (respect to boundary topology) and other properties, see
[
CG24
, Proposition 4.45]
.

Remark 3.2.4
.

If we have finitely many nef compactified metrized divisors
\overline{D_{j}}=(D_{j},g_{j})
for
j=0,\dots,k
, then there is an open subset
U^{\prime}\subset U
such that the restriction of
\overline{D_{j}}
on
U^{\prime}
is strongly nef, see
[
CG24
, Remark 4.34]
.

3.3.
Global theory: Compactified
S
-metrized divisors

In the rest of this section, we fix an adelic curve
S=(K,\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
satisfying the following conditions:

(a)

\nu(\Omega_{\infty})<\infty
;

(b)

the set
\nu(\mathcal{A})\not\subset\{0,+\infty\}
;

(c)

either the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{A}
is discrete, or that
K
is countable.

We also fix a
d
-dimensional quasi-projective variety
U
over
K
and set
U_{\overline{K}}\coloneq U\times_{\mathrm{Spec}(K)}\mathrm{Spec}(\overline{K})
.

3.3.1
.

An
S
-Green function
for a divisor
D\in\Div_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)
is a family of Green functions
g_{D,\omega}
for the base change
D_{\omega}
of
D
to
U_{\omega}
with
\omega
running over
\Omega
. We denote
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)
the group of pairs
(D,g_{D})
with
D
a
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier divisor and
g_{D}
an
S
-measurable
,
locally
S
-bounded

S
-Green function for
D
, see
[
CG24
, Definition 6.3, Definition 6.9]
.
Given
\overline{D}\coloneq(D,g_{D})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)
and
x\in U(\overline{K})
, we denote its
height function
as

h_{\overline{D}}(x)\coloneq\int_{\Omega_{\overline{K}}}g_{\omega}(x)\ \nu_{\overline{K}}(d\omega),

where
S_{\overline{K}}=(\overline{K},\Omega_{\overline{K}},\mathcal{A}_{\overline{K}},\nu_{\overline{K}})
denotes the canonical extension of the adelic curve structure on
K
to the algebraic closure
\overline{K}
as explained in
[
CM20
, §3.4.2]
.

If
U=X
is projective, a pair
(D,g_{D})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
if and only if
(D,g_{D})
is an
adelic
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
in the sense of Chen and Moriwaki,
[
CM20
, §6.2.3]
, see
[
CG24
, Remark 6.21]
. Note that in this case, it also ocincides with the definition of height function given in
[
CM20
, Definition 6.2.1]
.

3.3.2
.

Let
X
be a
d
-dimensional projective variety over
K
.
We consider the submonoid

N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
of
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
consisting of
(D,g_{D})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
with
g_{D,\omega}
a uniform limit of semipositive model (resp. tFS-)Green functions for
D_{\omega}
on
X_{\omega}^{\mathrm{an}}
if
\omega\in\Omega\setminus\Omega_{0}
(resp.
\omega\in\Omega_{0}
). Write
M_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)\coloneq N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)-N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
which is an ordered
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space:
(D,g_{D})\geq 0
if and only if
D\geq 0
and
g_{D,\omega}\geq 0
for any
\omega\in\Omega
. We have a symmetric multilinear map

(24)

\displaystyle M_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)^{d+1}\to\mathbb{R},\ \ (D_{0},\cdots,D_{d})\mapsto(D_{0}\cdots D_{d}\mid X)_{S}.

For any
\overline{D}\in M_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
and integral closed subscheme
Z
of
X
, set
h_{\overline{D}}(Z)\coloneq(\overline{D}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits(Z)+1}\mid Z)_{S}
(the arithmetic intersection number is well-defined without assuming that
X
is geometrically integral, see
[
CM21
]
). Notice that the restriction of
\overline{D}
on
Z
may not be defined, but the restriction of the corresponding
S
-metrized
(
\mathbb{Q}
-
)
line bundle
is defined (see
[
CG24
, §7.2]
), so
h_{\overline{D}}(Z)
is well-defined.

We say that
\overline{D}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
is
S
-ample
if
D
is ample and if
there is
\varepsilon>0
such that for every integral closed subscheme
Z
of
X
, we have

h_{\overline{D}}(Z)\geq\varepsilon\deg_{D}(Z)(\operatorname{dim}\nolimits(Z)+1).

The
S
-ample divisors form a cone
N^{+}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
of
M_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
. We say that
\overline{D}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
is
S
-nef
if
\overline{D}
is in the closure of
N^{+}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
in
M_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
with respect to the finite subspace topology. The
S
-nef divisors form a subcone
N^{\prime}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
of
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
which is preserved under pull-backs and tensor products.

3.3.3
.

Set

\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}\coloneq\varinjlim_{X}\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X),\ \ N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)\coloneq\varinjlim_{X}N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X),\ \ N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(U)\coloneq\varinjlim_{X}N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(X),

where
X
runs through all projective
K
-model of
U
.

A
weak boundary divisor
(resp. a
boundary divisor
) of
U
is a pair
({X}_{0},\overline{B})
consisting of a projective
K
-model
U\hookrightarrow{X}_{0}
over
K
and

\overline{B}\in M_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\geq 0}
such that
|B|\subset{X}_{0}\setminus U
(resp.
|B|={X}_{0}\setminus U
).
The weak boundary divisors form a directed subset
T
of
M_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\geq 0}
. As in
3.1.1
, a weak boundary divisor
(X_{0},\overline{B})
gives a topology, called
\overline{B}
-boundary topology
, on
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}
.
We set
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}^{d_{\overline{B}}}
(resp.
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{d_{\overline{B}}}
, resp.
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(U)^{d_{\overline{B}}}
) as the completion of
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}
(resp.
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)
, resp.
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(U)
) with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology. The space

\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}\coloneq\varinjlim_{\overline{B}\in T}\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}^{d_{\overline{B}}}

is called the space of
compactified
S
-metrized
(
\mathbb{Q}
-
)
divisors
of
U
. We also set

\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}\coloneq\varinjlim_{\overline{B}\in T}N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{d_{\overline{B}}},\ \ \widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}snef}}\coloneq\varinjlim_{\overline{B}\in T}N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(U)^{d_{\overline{B}}},

\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}int}}\coloneq\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}-\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}},

\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}int}}\coloneq\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}snef}}-\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}snef}},

and denote by
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}

(resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
) the closure of
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}
(resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}snef}}
) in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}int}}
(resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}int}}
) with respect to the finite subspace topology. A compactified
S
-metrized divisors
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
is called
relatively integrable
(resp.
strongly relatively nef
, resp.
relatively nef
, resp.
arithmetically integrable
, resp.
strongly arithmetically nef
, resp.
arithmetically nef
) if
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}int}}
(resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}
, resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}
, resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}int}}
, resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
, resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
).

We have injective homomorphisms

(25)

\displaystyle\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}int}}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}int}}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)

(see
[
CG24
, Proposition 7.4]
for the injectivity) and a forgetting homomorphism

\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}\to\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}.

By the injectivity of (
25
), throughout this paper, we write an element
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
as
(D,g)
with
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
and
g
an
S
-measurable, locally
S
-bounded
S
