-Green function for
D|_{U}
. For a compactified divisor
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, an
S
-Green function for
D
is an
S
-Green function
g
for
D|_{U}
such that
(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
.
Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
and
x\in U(\overline{K}
), we write
h_{\overline{D}}(x)\coloneq\int_{\Omega}g_{\omega}(x)\,\nu(d\omega).

Remark 3.3.4
.

If
U=X
is projective, by
[
CG24
, Proposition 7.10]
, we have that

\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{CM}}=\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{cpt}}=\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}int}}=\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}int}},

\text{$\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}=N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)$ \ \ and \ \ $\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}=N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(X)$}.

3.4.
Extension of Yuan-Zhang’s arithmetic intersection number

We have a symmetric bilinear map

\underbrace{\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}int}}\times\cdots\times\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}int}}}_{(d+1)\text{-times}}\to\mathbb{R},\ \ (\overline{D_{0}},\dots,\overline{D_{d}})\mapsto(\overline{D_{0}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}}\mid U)_{S},

see
[
CG24
, Theorem 7.22]
.
We want to extend this map to relatively nef case, see
[
BK24
, Definition 4.6]
for archimedean case and
[
CG24
, Theorem 11.3]
for general case.

3.4.1
.

Let
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
and
g_{1},g_{2}
measurable
S
-Green functions for
D
, see
3.3.2
. We say that
g_{1}
is
more singular than
g_{2}
,
denoted by
[g_{1}]\leq[g_{2}]
, if there is a
\nu
-integrable function
C\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
such that
g_{1,\omega}\leq g_{2,\omega}+C(\omega)
for any
\omega\in\Omega
. We say that
g_{1}
,
g_{2}

have equivalent singularities
, denoted by
[g_{1}]=[g_{2}]
, if
[g_{2}]\leq[g_{1}]
and
[g_{1}]\leq[g_{2}]
.

Denote by
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
the monoid of relatively nef compactified
S
-metrized divisors
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}
satisfying the following property: there is an
S
-Green function
g^{\prime}
for
{D}
such that
(D,g^{\prime})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}.

3.4.2
.

Let
\overline{D_{j}}=({D}_{j},g_{j})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}
for
j=0,\dots,d
. Assume that there are
\overline{D_{j}^{\prime}}=({D}_{j},g_{j}^{\prime})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
with
[g_{j}]\leq[g_{j}^{\prime}]
for
j=0,\dots,d
(in particular,
\overline{D_{j}}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
). For any
\omega\in\Omega
, we set

E(\mathbf{g}_{\omega}^{\prime},\mathbf{g}_{\omega})\coloneq\sum\limits_{j=0}^{d}\int_{U_{\omega}^{\mathrm{an}}}(g_{j,\omega}-g_{j,\omega}^{\prime})\,c_{1}(\overline{D_{0,\omega}})\wedge\cdots\wedge c_{1}(\overline{D_{j-1,\omega}})\wedge c_{1}(\overline{D_{j+1,\omega}^{\prime}})\wedge\cdots\wedge c_{1}(\overline{D_{d,\omega}^{\prime}}),

E(\mathbf{g}^{\prime},\mathbf{g})\coloneq\int_{\Omega}E(\mathbf{g}_{\omega}^{\prime},\mathbf{g}_{\omega})\,\nu(d\omega)

and define

\displaystyle(\overline{D_{0}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}}\mid U)_{S}\coloneq(\overline{D_{0}^{\prime}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}^{\prime}}\mid U)_{S}+E(\mathbf{g}^{\prime},\mathbf{g}).

By
[
CG24
, Lemma 10.7]
, the map
\omega\mapsto E(\mathbf{g}_{\omega}^{\prime},\mathbf{g}_{\omega})
is
\nu
-integrable. The value
(\overline{D_{0}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}}\mid U)_{S}
is in
\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}
and it is finite if
[g_{j}]=[g_{j}^{\prime}]
for any
j
. It is shown in
[
CG24
, Proposition 10.10, Theorem 11.2]
that
(\overline{D_{0}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}}\mid U)_{S}
is independent of the choices of
\overline{D_{j}^{\prime}}
. Notice that every
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
satisfies the assumption based on the following fact: if
\overline{D^{\prime}}=(D,g^{\prime})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
, then
h\coloneq\max\{g,g^{\prime}\}
is an
S
-Green function for
{D}
with
({D},h)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
and
[g]\leq[h]
. Hence, we have a symmetric mulitlinear map

\underbrace{\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}\times\cdots\times\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}}_{(d+1)\text{-times}}\to\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\},\ \ (\overline{D_{0}},\dots,\overline{D_{d}})\mapsto(\overline{D_{0}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}}\mid U)_{S}.

3.5.
Graded linear series

3.5.1
.

An
admissible flag
of
U_{\overline{K}}
is a flag

Y_{\bullet}\colon\{\text{pt}\}=Y_{d}\subset Y_{d-1}\subset\cdots\subset Y_{0}=U_{\overline{K}}

of irreducible subvarieties of
U_{\overline{K}}
such that
\mathrm{codim}(Y_{i})=i
in
U_{\overline{K}}
and that the closed point
Y_{d}
is regular in each
Y_{i}
. Giving an admissible flag
Y_{\bullet}
is equivalent to choosing a closed regular point
p\coloneq Y_{d}
in
U_{\overline{K}}
and a system of parameters
\{z_{1},\cdots,z_{d}\}
such that
\widehat{\mathcal{O}_{U_{\overline{K}},p}}=\overline{K}[[z_{1},\cdots,z_{d}]]
, where
\kappa(p)
is the residue field of
p
, and
\widehat{\mathcal{O}_{U_{\overline{K}},p}}
is the Krull completion of the local ring
\mathcal{O}_{U_{\overline{K}},p}
with respect to its maximal ideal. Hence we have a valuation
v_{Y}
on
\widehat{\mathcal{O}_{U_{\overline{K}},p}}
as follows: for any
f=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}}a_{\alpha}z^{\alpha}\in\widehat{\mathcal{O}_{U_{\overline{K}},p}}=\overline{K}[[z_{1},\cdots,z_{d}]]
,

v_{Y}(f)\coloneq\min\{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\mid a_{\alpha}\not=0\},

where the minimum is taken with respect to the lexicographic order on the variables
z_{1},\cdots,z_{d}
.

In the rest of this section, we assume that
U
is normal and fix an admissible flag
Y_{\bullet}
of
U_{\overline{K}}
. Then we have a valuation function
v_{Y}
on
H^{0}(U,D)\otimes_{K}\overline{K}
for any
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
.

Next we introduce the notion of a graded linear series containing an ample series. It can be thought of as a generalization of the bigness property of
D
to arbitrary graded linear sub-series of
D
.

Definition 3.5.2
.

A
graded linear series
of
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
is a graded sub-algebra
V_{\bullet}=\{V_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\{H^{0}(U,mD)\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
with
\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})<\infty
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
We say a graded linear series
V_{\bullet}
of
D

contains an ample series
if the following two condition holds:

(h)

V_{m}\neq\{0\}
for large enough
m
;

(i)

there is a positive integer
n
, a projective model
X
of
U
and an ample divisor
A
on it such that
nD\geq A
and the canonical injection
H^{0}(U,mA)\xhookrightarrow{\otimes s^{\otimes m}}H^{0}(U,mnD)
factors through
V_{mn}
, i.e.

H^{0}(U,mA)\xhookrightarrow{\otimes s^{\otimes m}}V_{mn}\xhookrightarrow{}{H^{0}}(U,mnD),

for some non-zero
s\in H^{0}(U,nD-A)
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

3.5.3
.

Let
V_{\bullet}=\{V_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
be a graded linear series of
D
. As
3.1.3

(6)
, we can define the
algebraic volume
of
V_{\bullet}
as

\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})\coloneq\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}{m^{d}/d!}.

Let
D_{\overline{K}}
be the pull-back of
D
to
U_{\overline{K}}
. Then the valuation
v_{Y}
is defined on
H^{0}(U_{\overline{K}},D_{\overline{K}})=H^{0}(U,D)\otimes_{K}\overline{K}
. We denote set the semi-group

\Gamma(V_{\bullet})\coloneq\left\{(m,\gamma)\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}\mid\gamma=v_{Y}(s)\text{ for some $s\in V_{m}\otimes_{K}\overline{K}$}\right\}

and
\Gamma(V_{\bullet})_{m}\coloneq\Gamma(V_{\bullet})\cap(\{m\}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d})
. The associated
Okounkov body of
V_{\bullet}
is defined as

\Delta(V_{\bullet})\coloneq\text{ the closure of $\bigcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}\frac{1}{m}\Gamma(V_{\bullet})_{m}$ in $\{1\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}$ }.

We set
\Gamma(D)\coloneq\Gamma\left(\{H^{0}(U,mD)\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\right)
and
\Delta(D)\coloneq\Delta\left(\{H^{0}(U,mD)\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\right)
.

On the other hand, let
\Gamma(V_{\bullet})_{\mathbb{Z}}
be the subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}
generated by
\Gamma(V_{\bullet})
, and
\Gamma(V_{\bullet})_{\mathbb{Z},0}\coloneq\Gamma(V_{\bullet})_{\mathbb{Z}}\cap(\{0\}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d})
. Notice that
V_{\overline{K},\bullet}
is a
\overline{K}
-subalgebra of
\overline{K}[[z_{1},\cdots,z_{d}]]
, we have the Kodaira dimension
\kappa=\operatorname{dim}\nolimits(\Delta(V_{\bullet}))\leq d
of
V_{\bullet}
, see
Remark

2.5.4
. Then
\kappa=\mathrm{rk}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\Gamma(V_{\bullet})_{\mathbb{Z},0})
by
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.3.6 (1)]
. Moreover, by
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.3.18]
,

\lim\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet}),m\to\infty}\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}{m^{\kappa}}=\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{\kappa}}(\Delta(V_{\bullet}))>0,

where
\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet})\coloneq\{m\in\mathbb{N}\mid\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})\not=0\}
.
In particular, for
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
,
D
is big if and only if the Kodaira dimension of
\{H^{0}(U,mD)\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is
d
.

We state our next lemma which will be repeatedly used throughout this paper.

Lemma 3.5.4
.

Let
V_{\bullet}=\{V_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
be a graded linear series of
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. Suppose
V_{\bullet}
contains an ample series. Then
\Gamma(V_{\bullet})_{\mathbb{Z}}=\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}
. In particular, we have

(26)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})}=\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}{m^{d}/d!}={d!}\cdot{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(V_{\bullet}))}={d!}\cdot{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(V_{\bullet})^{\circ})}

where
S^{\circ}
denotes the topological interior of any subset
S\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Proof.

We choose a projective
K
-model
X
of
U
and an ample divisor
A
as in
Definition

3.5.2
which can be furthermore chosen to be very ample. As argued in the proof of
[
LM09
, Lemma 2.2]
, there exist sections
s_{0},s_{1},\cdots,s_{d}
in
H^{0}(X,A)
, hence in
H^{0}(U,A)
, with
v_{Y}(s_{0})=0
and
v_{Y}(s_{i})=e_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq d
, where
\{e_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq d}
is the standard basis of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
0
is the zero vector. Choose
m
such that there is a sequence of inclusions

H^{0}(U,A)\xhookrightarrow{\otimes t_{0}}V_{m}\xhookrightarrow{}H^{0}(U,mD)

for a non-zero
t_{0}\in H^{0}(U,mD-A)
. Write
f_{0}\coloneq v_{Y}(t_{0})
. Since
V_{m}\neq\{0\}
for large enough
m
, we can choose
m
above such that there is a non-zero section
t_{1}\in V_{m+1}
. Write
f_{1}\coloneq v(t_{1})
. Then clearly the vectors
(m,f_{0})
,
(m,f_{0}+e_{i})
and
(m+1,f_{1})
are all in
\Gamma(V_{\bullet})
. Hence
\Gamma(V_{\bullet})_{\mathbb{Z}}=\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}
,
\Gamma(V_{\bullet})_{\mathbb{Z},0}\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. This implies that the Kodaira dimension of
V_{\bullet}
is
d
, so

\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}{m^{d}}=\lim\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet}),m\to\infty}\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}{m^{d}}=\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(V_{\bullet})),

the first equality uses
Definition

3.5.2

(h)
. Hence (
26
) holds.
∎

We end this subsection by noting a result on the behavior of the Okounkov bodies under perturbation.

3.5.5
.

A
convex body
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a compact concave subset in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with non-empty interior. For example, by
3.5.3
, for
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)
,
D
is big if and only if
\Delta(D)
is a convex body in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
(i.e. the corresponding Kodaira dimension is
d
). We denote by
\mathscr{K}^{d}
the set of concave bodies in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Let
\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2}
be compact concave subsets in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
(not necessarily with interior points). We define the
Hausdorff distance
between
\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2}
as

d_{H}(\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2})\coloneq\inf\{\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\mid\Delta_{1}\subset\Delta_{2}+\varepsilon\mathbb{B},\Delta_{2}\subset\Delta_{1}+\varepsilon\mathbb{B}\}

where
\mathbb{B}
is the unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We also set

d_{S}(\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2})\coloneq\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}((\Delta_{1}\cup\Delta_{2})\setminus(\Delta_{1}\cap\Delta_{2})).

Then
d_{H}
and
d_{S}
are metrics, called the
Hausdorff metric
and the
symmetric difference metric
, respectively, on
\mathscr{K}^{d}
. Let
(\Delta_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}\subset\mathscr{K}^{d}
and
\Delta\in\mathscr{K}^{d}
. By
[
SW65
, Theorem 7]
,
\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}d_{H}(\Delta_{j},\Delta)=0
if and only if
\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}d_{S}(\Delta_{j},\Delta)=0
.

Lemma 3.5.6
.

Let
(\Delta_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
be a sequence of compact concave bodies in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
converging to
\Delta
, i.e.
\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}d_{H}(\Delta_{j},\Delta)=0
. Then for any
\lambda\in\Delta^{\circ}
, we have
\lambda\in\Delta^{\circ}_{j}
when
j
is large enough.

Proof.

Let
\lambda\in\Delta^{\circ}
and
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that
B\coloneq\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mid|x-\lambda|\leq r\}\subset\Delta
. We consider
B_{j}\coloneq B\cap\Delta_{j}
. Since
B\setminus B_{j}\subset(\Delta\cup\Delta_{j})\setminus(\Delta\cap\Delta_{j})
and
\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}d_{S}(\Delta_{j},\Delta)=0
by
3.5.5
, we have that
\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}d_{S}(B_{j},B)=0
, i.e.
B_{j}
converge to
B
. Since
B_{j}\subset B
, we have that

(27)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(B_{j})=\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(B)

and
B_{j}
is a convex body for
j
large enough. To show
\lambda\in\Delta_{j}^{\circ}
for
j
large, it suffices to show that
\lambda\in B_{j}^{\circ}
for
j
large. If
\lambda\not\in B_{j}^{\circ}
, since
B_{j}
is a convex body,

•

there is a hyperplane in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
separating
\lambda
and
B_{j}
when
\lambda\not\in B_{j}
;

•

there is a supporting hyperplane of
B_{j}
containing
\lambda
when
\lambda
is in the boundary of
B_{j}
by
[
ROC70
, Theorem 11.6]
.

In either case,
B_{j}
is contained in a semi-ball of
B
. By (
27
), we know that
\lambda\in B_{j}^{\circ}
for
j
large enough. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.5.7
.

Let
(D_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
be a Cauchy sequence in
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
converging to
D
. Assume that
D
is big. Then

\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}d_{H}(\Delta(D_{j}),\Delta(D))=0,

where
d_{H}(\cdot,\cdot)
is the usual Hausdorff metric on the space of compact convex bodies.
In particular, if
(D_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
is decreasing (resp. increasing) defining
D
, then

\Delta(D)=\bigcap\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\Delta(D_{j})

(resp.
\Delta(D)^{\circ}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{\infty}\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}
).

Proof.

The first claim is a consequence of
[
Bis23a
, Corollary 1.19]
. For the second claim, if
D_{j}
is decreasing, then
\Delta(D)\subset\bigcap\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\Delta(D_{j})
. By
3.5.5

\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}d_{H}(\Delta(D_{j}),\Delta(D))=0
implies that

\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}d_{S}(\Delta(D_{j}),\Delta(D))=\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(D_{j}))-\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(D))=0,

so
\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\bigcap\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\Delta(D_{j})\right)=\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(D))
. This implies that
\Delta(D)=\bigcap\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\Delta(D_{j})
since they are both concave bodies. If
D_{j}
is increasing, then
\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}\subset\Delta(D)^{\circ}
. By
Lemma

3.5.6
, the equality holds.
∎

4.
Arithmetic volumes and concave transforms of compactified
S
-metrized divisors

In this section, we will define the arithmetic volume, arithmetic
\chi
-volume and concave transform of a compactified
S
-metrized divisor, and use these notion to study essential minimal and give a height inequality. Throughout this section, we fix an adelic curve
S=(K,\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
satisfying the condition
(a)

(b)

(c)
in §
3.3
and the tensorial minimal slope property of level
\geq C_{0}
for some
C_{0}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
(see
2.5.1
). We also fix a
d
-dimensional normal quasi-projective variety
U
over
K
. We fix an admissible flag
Y_{\bullet}
of
U_{\overline{K}}
with
Y_{d}=p
, see
3.5.1
for the definition of admissible flag, then we have the algebra
\widehat{\mathcal{O}_{U_{\overline{K}},p}}=\overline{K}[[z_{1},\cdots,z_{d}]]
. By a graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundle, we mean a graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundle with respect to
R\coloneq\mathrm{Frac}(\overline{K}[[z_{1},\cdots,z_{d}]])
in the sense of
Definition

2.5.2
.

4.1.
Auxiliary graded linear series for compactified
S
-metrized divisors

In this subsection, we associate a graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles to any compactified
S
-metrized divisor. We need to show that this definition actually gives us a graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles. We derive the necessary results from the corresponding facts in the projective case.

4.1.1
.

For any compactified
S
-metrized divisor
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
introduced in
3.3.3
and any
\omega\in\Omega
, we can define the
sup-norm
on
H^{0}(U_{\omega},D_{\omega})
as follows: for any
s\in H^{0}(U_{\omega},D_{\omega})
, set

\log\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}\coloneq\sup\{(-g_{\omega}+\log|s|_{\omega})(x)\mid x\in U_{\omega}^{\mathrm{an}}\}.

Note that by the above definition,
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,\omega}
can take
\infty
as a value and hence it is not an honest norm.
Our idea will be to look at those sections in
H^{0}(U,D)
which satisfies an appropriate dominancy.

Definition 4.1.2
.

Let
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. The space of
auxiliary global sections
as

{H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D})}\coloneq\{s\in H^{0}(U,D)\mid\text{{$\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}<\infty$ for any $\omega\in\Omega$,}}\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=6.2611pt,depth=-5.8611pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=4.87221pt,depth=-4.47221pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}}\!\int_{\Omega}\log\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}\,\nu(d\omega)<\infty\}.

With the above definition we associate an
auxiliary graded
K
-algebra
to
\overline{D}
as
\{H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D})\otimes_{K}\overline{K}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}

which is a
\overline{K}
-subalgebra of
R[T]
, see
3.5.1
.
Each of the graded pieces
H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D})
equipped with the sup-norm
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,m,\omega}
for each
\omega\in\Omega
becomes an
S
-normed
K
-vector space.

Remark 4.1.3
.

Let
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}
, and
X
a projective
K
-model of
U
such that
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
. If
X
is integrally closed in
U
, then
H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D})=H^{0}(U,D)=H^{0}(X,D)
. Indeed, the second equality is from
3.1.3
. For the first equality, notice that for any
\omega\in\Omega
,
U^{\mathrm{an}}_{\omega}
is dense in
X^{\mathrm{an}}_{\omega}
, then the sup-norm on
H^{0}(U,D)
is exactly the one on
H^{0}(X,D)
via the identity, so
\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}<\infty
for any
s\in H^{0}(U,D)
. Moreover, by
[
CM20
, Theorem 6.1.13]
, we have that
\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=6.2611pt,depth=-5.8611pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=4.87221pt,depth=-4.47221pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}}\!\int_{\Omega}\log\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}\,\nu(d\omega)<\infty
for any
s\in H^{0}(X,D)
. This proves our claim.

We want to show next that
\{(H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is a graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles, so that we can study their arithmetic volumes. The main task is to prove that
(H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,m})
is an adelic vector bundle (see
2.2.1
) for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and we will use approximating projective models to deduce that. We need the following lemma.

Lemma 4.1.4
.

Let
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, and
(\overline{D_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}\subset\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
a sequence decreasingly converging to
\overline{D}
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology for some weak boundary divisor
\overline{B}
of
U
. Then for every
s\in H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D})\subset H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D_{j}})
and
\omega\in\Omega
, we have that

\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\log\|s\|_{j,\sup,\omega}=\log\|s\|_{\sup,\omega},

where
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,\omega}
(resp.
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{j,\sup,\omega}
) is the sup-norm on
H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D})
(resp.
H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D_{j}})
) at
\omega
.

Proof.

Write
\overline{D}=(D,g)
and
\overline{D_{j}}=(D_{j},g_{j})
with
D,D_{j}\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. Let
s\in H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D})\subseteq H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D_{j}})
and
\omega\in\Omega
. The effectivity relation
\overline{D_{j}}\geq\overline{D}
implies that for any
x\in U^{\mathrm{an}}_{\omega}
, we have

-g_{j,\omega}(x)+|s(x)|_{\omega}\leq-g_{\omega}(x)+|s(x)|_{\omega},

which implies
\log\|s\|_{j,\sup,\omega}\leq\log\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}
. Similarly, the monotonicity of the Cauchy sequence clearly implies that
\log\|s\|_{j,\sup,\omega}
is increasing. Then
\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\log\|s\|_{j,\sup,\omega}
exists and

(28)

\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\log\|s\|_{j,\sup,\omega}\leq\log\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}.

On the other hand, let
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
. By definition, there is a point
x\in U_{\omega}^{\mathrm{an}}
such that

-g_{\omega}(x)+\log|s(x)|_{\omega}\geq\log\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}-\varepsilon.

Since
\overline{D_{j}}
converges to
\overline{D}
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology for some weak boundary divisor
\overline{B}
of
U
, we have that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\log\|s\|_{j,\sup,\omega}

\displaystyle\geq\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}(-g_{j,\omega}(x)+\log|s(x)|_{\omega})

\displaystyle=-g_{\omega}(x)+\log|s(x)|_{\omega}

\displaystyle\geq\log\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}-\varepsilon.

Since
\varepsilon
is arbitrary, we have that
\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\log\|s\|_{j,\sup,\omega}\geq\log\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}
. Combining this with (
28
), we complete the proof.
∎

Proposition 4.1.5
.

Let
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. Then
(H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup})
is an adelic vector bundle. Moreover, the graded
K
-algebra
\{(H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
equipped with the sup-norms is a graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles in the sense of
Definition

2.5.2
.

Proof.

We prove the proposition in the following steps.

Step 1:
The
S
-normed space
(H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup})
is measurable.

Let
(\overline{D_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
be a sequence in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}
decreasingly converging to
\overline{D}
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology for some weak boundary divisor
\overline{B}
of
U
. Assume that
\overline{D_{j}}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X_{j})
for some projective
K
-model of
U
which is integrally closed in
U
. Then
H_{+}^{0}(U,\overline{D})=H^{0}(U,\overline{D_{j}})=H^{0}(X_{j},D_{j})
by Remark
4.1.3
. Let
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{j,\sup,\omega}
the sup-norm on
H^{0}(X,D_{j})
at
\omega\in\Omega
. By
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.1.26]
, for any
j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
and
s\in H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D})\subset H^{0}(X,{D}_{j})
, the function
\log\|s\|_{j,\sup,\omega}
is
\mathcal{A}
-measurable. By
Lemma

4.1.4
, this implies that
\log|s|_{\sup,\omega}
is
\mathcal{A}
-measurable. Hence
(H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup})
is measurable.

Step 2:
The
S
-normed space
(H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup})
is dominated.

Let
\overline{V}^{\vee}=(V^{\vee},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}^{\vee})
be the dual of
(H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup})
. By definition of
H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D})
in
Definition

4.1.2
, we have that

\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=6.2611pt,depth=-5.8611pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=4.87221pt,depth=-4.47221pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}}\!\int_{\Omega}\log\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}\,\nu(d\omega)<\infty

for any
s\in H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D})
. It remains to show that

\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=6.2611pt,depth=-5.8611pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=4.87221pt,depth=-4.47221pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}}\!\int_{\Omega}\log\|t\|_{\omega}^{\vee}\,\nu(d\omega)<\infty

for any
t\in V^{\vee}
. Let
\overline{D^{\prime}}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
for some projective
K
-model
X
of
U
such that
X
is integrally closed in
U
and
\overline{D^{\prime}}\geq\overline{D}
in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}
. By Remark
4.1.3
, we have that
H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D^{\prime}})=H^{0}(X,D^{\prime})
. We denote
{\overline{V^{\prime}}}^{\vee}=({V^{\prime}}^{\vee},{{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}^{\prime}}^{\vee})
the dual of
H^{0}(X,D^{\prime})
equipped with the sup-norm
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup}^{\prime}
.
Since
\overline{D}\leq\overline{D^{\prime}}
, we have a canonical injective map of
K
-vector spaces

f\colon H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D})\rightarrow H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D^{\prime}})=H^{0}(X,D^{\prime})

which induces a contractive map

f_{\omega}\colon(H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D})\otimes_{K}K_{\omega},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,\omega})\rightarrow(H^{0}(X,D^{\prime})\otimes_{K}K_{\omega},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,\omega}^{\prime}),

i.e. the operator norm
\|f_{\omega}\|\leq 1
, for any
\omega\in\Omega
. For the dual map

f_{\omega}^{\vee}\colon({V^{\prime}}^{\vee}\otimes_{K}K_{\omega},{{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}^{\prime}}^{\vee}_{\omega})\to(V^{\vee}\otimes_{K}K_{\omega},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}^{\vee}_{\omega})

which is surjective by
[
CM20
, Proposition 1.1.22]
, we have that
\|f_{\omega}^{\vee}\|\leq\|f_{\omega}\|\leq 1
. Then for any
t\in V^{\vee}
, there is
t^{\prime}\in{V^{\prime}}^{\vee}
such that
t=f^{\vee}(t^{\prime})
, and we have that

\log\|t\|_{\omega}^{\vee}=\log\|f^{\vee}(t^{\prime})\|_{\omega}^{\vee}\leq\log{\|t^{\prime}\|^{\prime}}^{\vee}_{\omega}

for any
\omega\in\Omega
.
Note that
(H^{0}(X,D^{\prime}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup}^{\prime})
is dominated by
[
CM20
, Theorem 6.1.13]
, then

\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=6.2611pt,depth=-5.8611pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=4.87221pt,depth=-4.47221pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}}\!\int_{\Omega}\log\|t\|_{\omega}^{\vee}\,\nu(d\omega)\leq\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=6.2611pt,depth=-5.8611pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=4.87221pt,depth=-4.47221pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}}\!\int_{\Omega}\log{\|t^{\prime}\|^{\prime}}_{\omega}^{\vee}\,\nu(d\omega)<\infty.

This completes the proof of Step 2.

Step 3:
The graded
K
-algebra
\{(H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is a graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundle.

For any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, by Step 1 and Step 2 the
S
-normed space
(H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,m})
is an adelic vector bundle on
S
. It remains to verify the three conditions in
Definition

2.5.2
. To verify condition
(3)
, note that we can choose a projective model
X
of
U
and a very ample divisor
A
in
X
such that
X
is integrally closed in
U
and
A\geq D
in
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, then there are inclusions

H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D})\xhookrightarrow{}H^{0}(U,mD)\xhookrightarrow{}S_{m}\coloneq H^{0}(U,mA)=H^{0}(X,mA).

Since
A
is very ample on
X
, Serre’s theorem implies that
\bigoplus\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}S_{m}T^{m}
is a
K
-algebra of finite type which clearly shows condition
(3)
(
\bigoplus\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}(S_{m}\otimes_{K}\overline{K})T^{m}
is
\overline{K}
-subalgebra of
R[T]
by restricting at
p
). Condition
(4)
is clear and condition
(5)
is clearly satisfied for the sup-norms
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,\omega}
due to how tensor product of two metrics are defined. This finishes the proof of Step 3.
∎

4.2.
Arithmetic volumes of compactified
S
-metrized divisors

In this subsection, we define the arithmetic volumes for compactified
S
-metrized divisors. We first consider the geometric volume. For
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, we use the graded linear series
\{H^{0}(U,mD)\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
to define the geometric volume
\mathrm{vol}(D)
and the Okounkov body
\Delta(D)
in
3.5.3
(see also
Definition

4.2.2
). On the other hand, we have the graded linear series
\{H_{+}^{0}(U,m\overline{D})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
defined in
Definition

4.1.2
, we denote the corresponding volume and Okounkov body by
\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})
and
\Delta_{+}(\overline{D})
(see
3.5.3
), respectively. We have the following result.

Lemma 4.2.1
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
with
D
big. Then the graded linear series
\{H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
contains an ample series (see
Definition

3.5.2
). Furthermore, we have that

\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})={\mathrm{vol}}(D)

In particular, we have that

\Delta_{+}(\overline{D})^{\circ}=\Delta(D)^{\circ}.

Proof.

Let
\overline{B}=(B,g_{B})
be a weak boundary divisor of
U
,
(\overline{D_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}=(D_{j},g_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}\subset\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}
and
(\varepsilon_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}\subset\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
such that
\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\varepsilon_{j}=0
and

-\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B}\leq\overline{D}-\overline{D_{j}}\leq\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B}.

For any
j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, let
X_{j}
be a projective model of
U
such that
\overline{D_{j}}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X_{j})
. We claim that there is an inclusion

(29)

\displaystyle H^{0}(U,mD_{j}^{\prime})\xhookrightarrow{}H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D})\subseteq H^{0}(U,mD)

for all
j
and
m
where
\overline{D_{j}^{\prime}}=(D_{j}^{\prime},g^{\prime}_{j})\coloneq\overline{D_{j}}-\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B}
. If the claim holds, then by
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.2.1]
we can deduce that
D_{j}^{\prime}
is big for large enough
j
since
D
is assumed to be big. We fix such a
j_{0}
. Since
D_{j_{0}}^{\prime}
is big, we have that
H^{0}(X_{j_{0}},mD_{j_{0}}^{\prime})\not=0
for large
m
, so
H^{0}(U,m\overline{D})\not=0
. By Kodaira lemma (
[
Laz04
, Proposition 2.2.6]
), we can find a positive integer
n
and an ample divisor
A
on
X_{j_{0}}
such that
nD_{j_{0}}^{\prime}-A\geq 0
which in turn will induce injections

H^{0}(U,mA)\xhookrightarrow{\otimes s^{\otimes m}}H^{0}(U,mnD_{j_{0}}^{\prime})\xhookrightarrow{}H^{0}_{+}(U,mn\overline{D})

for a non-zero section
s\in H^{0}(U,nD_{j_{0}}^{\prime}-A)
. This clearly shows that
\{H^{0}_{+}(U,n\overline{D})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
contains an ample series. Moreover from (
29
), we have inequalities

\mathrm{vol}(D_{j}^{\prime})\leq\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})\leq\mathrm{vol}(D)

which also shows the equality of volumes by
3.1.3
(7)
. Then we can deduce the claim about the interiors by noting that two compact convex subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, one contained in another, can have the same volume if and only if their difference has empty interior. Hence it is enough to show our claim. To show the claim, for any
j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, note that the effectivity
\overline{D_{j}^{\prime}}\leq\overline{D}
induces an inequality

(30)

-g_{\omega}+\log|s|_{\omega}\leq-g^{\prime}_{j,\omega}+\log|s|_{\omega}

on
U_{\omega}^{\mathrm{an}}
for all
s\in H^{0}(U,D_{j}^{\prime})
and
\omega\in\Omega
. Furthermore since
\overline{D_{j}^{\prime}}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X_{j})
, by
[
CM20
, Theorem 6.1.13]
we have that

\log\|s\|^{\prime}_{j,\sup,\omega}\coloneq\sup\{-g_{j,\omega}^{\prime}(x)+\log|s(x)|_{\omega}\mid x\in X_{j,\omega}^{\mathrm{an}}\}<\infty,

\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=8.34444pt,depth=-7.94444pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=6.2611pt,depth=-5.8611pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hfil\vrule width=4.49997pt,height=4.87221pt,depth=-4.47221pt\kern 0.50003pt}\hss}}}\!\int_{\Omega}\log\|s\|^{\prime}_{j,\sup,\omega}\,\nu(d\omega)<\infty

for all
s\in H^{0}(U,D_{j}^{\prime})
. Now we can easily deduce the claim noting the inequality (
30
) and
[
CM20
, Proposition A.4.2 (2)]
.
∎

Recall the arithmetic
\chi
-volume
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})
and arithmetic volume
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{E_{\bullet}})
of a graded algebra of adelic vector bundles
\overline{E_{\bullet}}=\{\overline{E_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
in
Definition

2.5.5
.

Definition 4.2.2
.

Let
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. We call

\overline{V_{\overline{D},\bullet}}=\left\{\overline{V_{\overline{D},m}}\right\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\coloneq\left\{(H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,m})\right\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}

the
graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles associated to
\overline{D}
and define the
arithmetic volume
and
arithmetic
\chi
-volume
of
\overline{D}
as

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})\coloneq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{V_{\overline{D},\bullet}})=\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg_{+}}(\overline{V_{\overline{D},m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}

and

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})\coloneq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{V_{\overline{D},\bullet}})=\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}(\overline{V_{\overline{D},m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!},

respectively.
We furthermore set

\widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\max}(\overline{D})\coloneq\widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\max}(\overline{V_{\overline{D},\bullet}})\ \text{ and }\ \widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\min}(\overline{D})\coloneq\widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\min}(\overline{V_{\overline{D},\bullet}}).

We say that
\overline{D}
is
big
if
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})>0
.

Remark 4.2.3
.

Even if we can define the arithmetic volume of any compactified
S
-metrized divisor with any assumption on the generic fiber thanks to the formalism of graded
K
-algebras, we will mostly stick to the case where
D
is big. In that case
Lemma

4.2.1
shows that the graded
K
-algebra
\{H_{+}^{0}(U,\overline{D})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
has Kodaira dimension
d
. Furthermore the construction of Okounkov bodies in
[
Bis23a
]
coincides with the more general construction sketched in
[
CM20
, Section 6.3.2]
. The main difference is that in the big case, all the Okounkov bodies live in the same ambient Euclidean space
\mathbb{R}^{d}
even if we perturb it in small enough directions whereas the Okounkov bodies in
[
CM20
]
may live in different ambient spaces of different dimension. Hence we almost always stick to the big case to be able to use properties shown in
[
Bis23a
]
.

Remark 4.2.4
.

When
U=X
is projective, and
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
a relatively nef element, recall
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
in
3.3.2
, then Chen-Moriwaki also define the asymptotic minimal slope for
\overline{D}
in
[
CM22
, Definition 6.4.2]
. Since
X
is normal, if
K
is perfect,
D
is ample and with
\mathbb{Z}
-coefficients, then our definition above is the same as their definition in
[
CM22
, Definition 6.4.2]
.

Lemma 4.2.5
.

Let
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. Then

\widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\max}(\overline{D})<\infty.

Proof.

The idea will be to deduce it from the projective case as usual. Let
\overline{D^{\prime}}=(D^{\prime},g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}
such that
\overline{D^{\prime}}\geq\overline{D}
. For any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, write
\overline{V_{m}}\coloneq(H^{0}(U,mD),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m,\sup})
(resp.
\overline{V_{m}^{\prime}}\coloneq(H^{0}(U,mD^{\prime}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m,\sup}^{\prime})
), where
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m,\sup}
(resp.
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m,\sup}^{\prime}
) is the corresponding sup-norm. Assume that
\overline{D^{\prime}}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
for some projective
K
-model of
U
which is integrally closed in
U
. Then
H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D^{\prime}})=H^{0}(X,mD^{\prime})
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
by Remark
4.1.3
. Moreover,
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.4.4]
and
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.2.7]
together show that
\widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\max}(\overline{D^{\prime}})<\infty
. Furthermore the effectivity relation
\overline{D^{\prime}}\geq\overline{D}
implies that there is an injective
K
-linear map

f_{m}\colon H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D})\xhookrightarrow{}H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D^{\prime}})

which is furthermore norm-contractive at every place
\omega
, i.e.
\|f_{m}\|_{\omega}\leq 1
for all
\omega\in\Omega
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Consequently we have that
h(f_{m})\leq 0
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Then
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.3.31 (1)]
shows that

\widehat{\mu}_{\max}(\overline{V_{m}})\leq\widehat{\mu}_{\max}(\overline{V_{m}^{\prime}})

for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
which clearly implies

\widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\max}(\overline{D})\leq\widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\max}(\overline{D^{\prime}})

and finishes the proof.
∎

4.3.
Concave transforms of compactified
S
-metrized divisors

4.3.1
.

Let
\overline{V_{\bullet}}=\{\overline{V_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}=\{(V_{m},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
be a graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles over
S
, and
\mathcal{H}_{\bullet}=\{\mathcal{H}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
be the the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
V_{\bullet}=\{V_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
defined in
2.4.1
. We assume that

\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet})\coloneq\{n\in\mathbb{N}\mid V_{n}\not=0\}\not=\{0\}.

Write
V_{\overline{K},\bullet}=\{V_{\overline{K},m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\coloneq\{V_{m}\otimes_{K}\overline{K}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}

\mathcal{H}_{\overline{K},\bullet}\coloneq\{\mathcal{H}_{m}\otimes_{K}\overline{K}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
, notice that
\mathcal{H}_{\overline{K},\bullet}
may not be the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
V_{\overline{K},\bullet}
. However, by
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.3.25]
(notice that the canonical extension
S_{\overline{K}}
of
S
to
\overline{K}
(see
[
CM20
, §3.4.2]
) satisfies the tensorial minimal slope property in sense of
2.5.1
),
\mathcal{H}_{\overline{K},\bullet}
is a
\delta
-superadditive
\mathbb{R}
-filtration on
V_{\overline{K},\bullet}
in the sense of
[
CM20
, Definition 6.3.19 (c)]
, where
\delta\colon\mathbb{N}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},\ \ n\mapsto C\log(\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{n}))
(although
\overline{V_{\bullet}}
may not be a graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles in the sense of
[
CM20
, Definition 6.4.24]
, but the proof of
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.3.25]
works without the assumption that
V_{\bullet}
is a
K
-subalgebra of
\mathrm{Frac}(K[[T_{1},\cdots,T_{d}]])[T]
). Notice that
\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{n})\leq C_{1}n^{\kappa}
for some
C_{1}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, where
\kappa
is the Kodaira dimension of
V_{\bullet}
, we can replace
\delta
by the map
n\mapsto C_{2}\log n
for some suitable
C_{2}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
. It is obvious that
\delta
is increasing, and

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{a\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\delta(2^{a})}{2^{a}}

\displaystyle=C_{2}\log 2\cdot\sum\limits_{a\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{a}{2^{a}}=C_{2}\log 2<\infty,

i.e. the condition in
[
CM20
, Theorem 6.3.20]
holds (similarly, the condition for
\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W_{n})
in
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.3.28]
automatically holds).
Then we can construct a
concave transform

G_{\mathcal{H}_{\bullet}}\colon\Delta(V_{\bullet})^{\circ}\to\mathbb{R}\cup\{+\infty\}
associated to
\mathcal{H}_{\bullet}
as follows (see
3.5.3
for the definitions of
\Gamma(V_{\bullet})
and
\Delta(V_{\bullet})
).
We set

\displaystyle\rho\colon\Gamma(V_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\},

\displaystyle(m,\gamma)

\displaystyle\mapsto\sup\{t\in\mathbb{R}\mid\text{there is $x\in\mathcal{H}_{\overline{K},m}^{t}(V_{\overline{K},m})$ such that $v_{Y}(x)=\gamma$}\},

and construct the auxiliary function
\widetilde{\rho}(m,\gamma)\coloneq\limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{\rho(nm,n\gamma)}{n}
, where
v_{Y}
is the valuation defined by the admissible flag
Y_{\bullet}
, see
3.5.1
.
We furthermore define

\Gamma^{t}(V_{\bullet})\coloneq\{(m,\gamma)\in\Gamma(V_{\bullet})\mid\widetilde{\rho}(m,\gamma)\geq mt\},

\Gamma^{t}(V_{\bullet})_{m}\coloneq\Gamma^{t}(V_{\bullet})\cap(\{m\}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}),

\Delta(\Gamma^{t}(V_{\bullet}))\coloneq\text{ the closure of $\bigcup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}\frac{1}{m}\Gamma^{t}(V_{\bullet})_{m}$ in $\{1\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}$ }.

For any
\lambda\in\Delta(V_{\bullet})^{\circ}
,

G_{\mathcal{H}_{\bullet}}(\lambda)\coloneq\sup\{t\in\mathbb{R}\mid\lambda\in\Delta(\Gamma^{t}(V_{\bullet}))\}.

Since
G_{\mathcal{H}_{\bullet}}
is convex, we have that
G_{\mathcal{H}_{\bullet}}\equiv+\infty
or
G_{\mathcal{H}_{\bullet}}(\Delta(V_{\bullet})^{\circ})\subset\mathbb{R}
.
On the other hand, for
m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet})
, let
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathcal{H}_{m}}
(resp.
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathcal{H}_{\overline{K},m}}
) be the norm on
V_{m}
(resp.
V_{\overline{K},m}
) associated to
\mathcal{H}_{m}
(resp.
\mathcal{H}_{\overline{K},m}
) in
[
CM20
, Remark 1.1.40]
, by
[
CM20
, Remark 4.3.63]
, we have that

(31)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}_{i}(V_{\overline{K},m},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathcal{H}_{\overline{K},m}})=\widehat{\mu}_{i}(V_{m},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathcal{H}_{m}})=\widehat{\mu}_{i}(\overline{V_{m}}),

where
\widehat{\mu}_{i}
is the
i
-th jumping number of the Harder-Narasimhan filtration defined in
2.4.1
(we view
(V_{m},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathcal{H}_{m}})
(resp.
(V_{\overline{K},m},{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\mathcal{H}_{\overline{K},m}})
) as adelic vector bundle over
K
(resp.
\overline{K}
) with the trivial absolute value). Write
\nu_{m}
the Borel probability measure on
\mathbb{R}
such that for any Borel function
f
on
\mathbb{R}
, we have that

\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(t)\ \nu_{m}(dt)\coloneq\frac{1}{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\sum_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}f\left(\frac{1}{m}\widehat{\mu}_{i}(\overline{V_{m}})\right).

From our discussion above and apply
[
CM20
, Theorem 6.3.20]
to
\mathcal{H}_{\overline{K},\bullet}
, we have that
(\nu_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet})}
vaguely converge to the zero measure or to the pushforward
\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{\kappa}}(\Delta(V_{\bullet}))}G_{\mathcal{H}_{\bullet},*}(d\lambda)
, where
d\lambda
is the standard Lebesgue measure on
\Delta(V_{\bullet})^{\circ}\subset\mathbb{R}^{\kappa}
. More precisely, from Step 6 of the proof of
[
CM20
, Theorem 6.3.12]
, if
G_{\mathcal{H}_{\bullet}}\equiv+\infty
, then
(\nu_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet})}
vaguely converge to the zero measure; if
G_{\mathcal{H}_{\bullet}}(\Delta(V_{\bullet})^{\circ})\subset\mathbb{R}
, then
(\nu_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet})}
weakly converge to
\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{\kappa}}(\Delta(V_{\bullet}))}G_{\mathcal{H}_{\bullet},*}(d\lambda)
. By
[
CM20
, Remark 4.3.48, Remark 6.3.21]
and (
31
), if
\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{V_{\bullet}})<+\infty
, then

(32)

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta(V_{\bullet})^{\circ}}G_{\mathcal{H}_{\bullet}}(\lambda)=\lim\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet}),m\to+\infty}\frac{1}{n}\widehat{\mu}_{1}(\overline{V_{m}})=\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{V_{\bullet}})<+\infty,

hence in this case,
(\nu_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet})}
converge weakly to
\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{\kappa}}(\Delta(V_{\bullet}))}G_{\mathcal{H}_{\bullet},*}(d\lambda)
.

Let
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
such that there is
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
with
\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D}))>0
. We consider the case where
\overline{V_{\bullet}}
is the graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles associated to
D
defined in
Definition

4.1.2
, then all above assumptions for
\overline{V_{\bullet}}
are satisfied (notice that
\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})<\infty
by
Lemma

4.2.5
). The corresponding concave transform
G_{\overline{D}}\colon\Delta_{+}(\overline{D})^{\circ}\to\mathbb{R}
is called the
concave transform
of
\overline{D}
.

We have the following theorem which generalizes a result of Chen-Moriwaki
[
CM20
, Theorem 6.4.6]
.

Theorem 4.3.2
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, and
\overline{V_{\bullet}}=\{\overline{V_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
its associated graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles defined in
Definition

4.1.2
. Assume that
\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet})\not=0
. Then the associated Borel probability measures
(\nu_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet})}
in
4.3.1
converge weakly to the probability measure
\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{\kappa}}(\Delta_{+}(\overline{D}))}G_{\overline{D},*}(d\lambda)
, where
d\lambda
is the standard Lebesgue measure on
\Delta(D)\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\kappa}
and
\kappa
is the Kodaira dimension
V_{\bullet}
(
\kappa=d
if
D
is big). In particular, if
\overline{D}
is big, then
D
is big. Moreover, in the case where
D
is big (note that
\Delta(D)^{\circ}=\Delta_{+}(\overline{D})^{\circ}
by
Lemma

4.2.1
), we have that

(33)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}={(d+1)!}\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}\max\{G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda),0\}\,d\lambda.

and

(34)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})=\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}\leq(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda.

with equality if
\widehat{\mu}_{\min}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>-\infty
(in this case the supremum limit in (
34
) is a limit).

Proof.

By our discussion in
4.3.1
,
(\nu_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet})}
converge weakly to the probability measure
\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{\kappa}}(\Delta_{+}(\overline{D}))}G_{\overline{D},*}(d\lambda)
. In particular, by
[
CM20
, Remark 6.3.27]
,

(35)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{\kappa}}(\Delta_{+}(\overline{D}))}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\max\{x,0\}\,((G_{\overline{D}})_{*}d\lambda)(dx)=&\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{\kappa}}(\Delta_{+}(\overline{D}))}\int_{\Delta_{+}(D)^{\circ}}\max\{G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda),0\}\,d\lambda\\
=&\lim_{m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet}),m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}.\end{split}

Since
\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet})\not=0
, there are infinitely many
m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
V_{m}\not=0
. So

(36)

\displaystyle\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})=\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}{m^{d}/d!}=\limsup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet}),m\to\infty}\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}{m^{d}/d!},

(37)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=&\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}=\limsup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet}),m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}\\
=&(d+1)\cdot\limsup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet}),m\to\infty}\left(\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}{m^{d}/d!}\cdot\frac{\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\right)\end{split}

Combine (
37
) (
35
) (
36
), we have that

(38)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})\cdot\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{\kappa}}(\Delta_{+}(\overline{D}))}\int_{\Delta_{+}(D)^{\circ}}\max\{G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda),0\}\,d\lambda.

If
\overline{D}
is big, i.e.
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})>0
, then by (
38
),
\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})>0
which implies that
D
is big since
\mathrm{vol}(D)\geq\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})>0
.

In the case where
D
is big, by
Lemma

4.2.1
,
V_{\bullet}
contains an ample series,
\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})=\mathrm{vol}(D)
,
\Delta_{+}(\overline{D})^{\circ}=\Delta(D)^{\circ}
and
\kappa=d
(see
3.5.3
). In particular,
V_{m}\not=0
for
m
large enough, so (
35
) becomes

(39)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta_{+}(\overline{D}))}\int_{\Delta_{+}(D)^{\circ}}\max\{G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda),0\}\,d\lambda=&\lim_{m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet}),m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\\
=&\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}.\end{split}

We can deduce from
Lemma

3.5.4
that

(40)

\displaystyle\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})=\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}{m^{d}/d!}=d!\cdot\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta_{+}(\overline{D})).

Combine (
38
) and (
40
), we have that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=

\displaystyle(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})\cdot\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta_{+}(\overline{D}))}\int_{\Delta_{+}(\overline{D})^{\circ}}\max\{G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda),0\}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}\max\{G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda),0\}\,d\lambda

On the other hand, combine (
38
) (
39
) (
40
), we have that

(41)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=(d+1)\cdot\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}{m^{d}/d!}\cdot\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}=\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!},

i.e. the supremum limit in the definition of
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})
is actually a limit when
D
is big.
Hence we have completed the proof of (
33
).

It remains to consider (
34
). For any large
m\in\mathbb{N}
(notice that
m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet})
),
\omega\in\Omega
, we recall the invariant
\delta_{\omega}(\overline{V_{m}})
of
\overline{V_{m}}
defined in
[
CM20
, Definition 4.3.9]
and set

\delta(\overline{V_{m}})\coloneq\int_{\omega\in\Omega}\log\delta_{\omega}(\overline{V_{m}})\,\nu(d\omega)

as in
[
CM20
, Definition 4.3.12]
. Then by
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.3.10]

0\leq\delta(\overline{V_{m}})\leq\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})\log(\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m}))\nu(\Omega_{\infty}),

i.e.

\displaystyle 0\leq\frac{\delta(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{\log(\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m}))}{m}\cdot\nu(\Omega_{\infty}).

By
Remark

2.5.4
, we have that
\log(\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m}))\leq C^{\prime}\log(m)
for some
C^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, then

(42)

\displaystyle 0\leq\frac{\delta(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{2}\cdot\frac{\log(m)}{m}\cdot\nu(\Omega_{\infty}).

By our assumption that
\nu(\Omega_{\infty})<\infty
, after taking the limit on (
42
), we have that

(43)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\delta(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}=0.

By
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.3.50, Proposition 4.3.51]
,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\widehat{\mu}_{i}(\overline{V_{m}})\leq\widehat{\deg}(\overline{V_{m}})\leq\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\widehat{\mu}_{i}(\overline{V_{m}})+\delta(\overline{V_{m}}).

i.e.

(44)

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\widehat{\mu}_{i}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\leq\frac{\widehat{\deg}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\leq\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\widehat{\mu}_{i}(\overline{V_{m}})+\delta(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}.

After taking the supremum limit on (
44
), by
Definition

4.2.2
and (
43
), we have that

(45)

\displaystyle\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}=\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\widehat{\mu}_{i}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}.

Then

(46)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})=&\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\deg}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}\\
=&(d+1)\cdot\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\left(\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}{m^{d}/d!}\cdot\frac{\widehat{\deg}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\right)\\
=&(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})\cdot\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\widehat{\mu}_{i}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}.\end{split}

where the last equality is from (
40
) and from (
45
), notice that the supremum limit in the definition of
\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})
(see (
36
)) is actually a limit in (
40
) when
D
is big.
On the other hand, notice that
\sup\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}}\{G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\}=\widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\max}(\overline{D})=\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\mu}_{\max}(V_{m})}{m}<\infty
, so there exists
c>\max\{0,\widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\max}(\overline{D})\}
such that
\frac{\widehat{\mu}_{i}(V_{m})}{m}\leq\frac{\widehat{\mu}_{\max}(V_{m})}{m}\leq c
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}(V_{\bullet})
and
1\leq i\leq\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})
. Hence for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta_{+}(\overline{D}))}\int_{-\alpha}^{\infty}x\,((G_{\overline{D}})_{*}d\lambda)(dx)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta_{+}(\overline{D}))}\int_{-\alpha}^{c}x\,((G_{\overline{D}})_{*}d\lambda)(dx)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\int_{-\alpha}^{c}x\,\nu_{m}(dx)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\int_{-\alpha}^{\infty}x\,\nu_{m}(dx)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}x\,\nu_{m}(dx)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\widehat{\mu}_{i}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}.

Let
\alpha\to\infty
in the above inequality, we get

(47)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta_{+}(\overline{D}))}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}x\,((G_{\overline{D}})_{*}d\lambda)(dx)=\frac{d!}{\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})}\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda\geq\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\widehat{\mu}_{i}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}.

Combine (
46
) and (
47
), we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})\leq(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})\cdot\frac{d!}{\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})}\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda=(d+1)!\cdot\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda.

If
\widehat{\mu}_{\min}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})=\liminf\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\mu}_{\min}(m\overline{D})}{m}>-\infty
, by
[
CM20
, Remark 6.3.27]
, we have that

\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta_{+}(\overline{D}))}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}x\,((G_{\overline{D}})_{*}d\lambda)(dx)=\frac{d!}{\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})}\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda=\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}\widehat{\mu}_{i}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})},

in particular, the supremum limits in (
46
) are limits. Combine (
46
) and (
47
), we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})=(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})\cdot\frac{d!}{\mathrm{vol}_{+}(\overline{D})}\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda=(d+1)!\cdot\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda.

This completed the proof of (
34
).
∎

For any
c=(c(\omega))_{\omega\in\Omega}\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
, we denote
(0,c)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
the compactified
S
-metrized divisor such that the underlying compactified divisor of
(0,c)
on a projective model of
U
is
0
, and the Green functions at
\omega\in\Omega
are
c(\omega)
. For any
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, we write
\overline{D}(c)\coloneq\overline{D}+(0,c)
.

We list the following properties of the concave transforms.

Proposition 4.3.3
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g),\overline{D^{\prime}}=(D^{\prime},g^{\prime})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
with
D,D^{\prime}
big. Then the following statements hold.

(10)

For any
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
, we have that
\Delta(\alpha\overline{D})=\alpha\Delta(\overline{D})
and

G_{\alpha\overline{D}}(\alpha\lambda)=\alpha\cdot G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)

for any
\lambda\in\Delta(\overline{D})^{\circ}
.

(11)

Let
c=(c(\omega))_{\omega\in\Omega}\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
. Then

G_{\overline{D}(c)}=G_{\overline{D}}+\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)

on
\Delta(\overline{D}(c))^{\circ}=\Delta(D)^{\circ}
.

(12)

If
\overline{D}\leq\overline{D^{\prime}}
, then
G_{\overline{D}}\leq G_{\overline{D^{\prime}}}
on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}\subseteq\Delta(D^{\prime})^{\circ}
.

(13)

We have that

\widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\min}(\overline{D})\leq G_{\overline{D}}\leq\widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\max}(\overline{D}).

Moreover,
\sup\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)=\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})
.

(14)

For any
\lambda\in\Delta(\overline{D})^{\circ}
and
\lambda^{\prime}\in\Delta(\overline{D^{\prime}})^{\circ}
, we have that
\lambda+\lambda^{\prime}\in\Delta(\overline{D}+\overline{D^{\prime}})^{\circ}
and

G_{\overline{D}+\overline{D^{\prime}}}(\lambda+\lambda^{\prime})\geq G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)+G_{\overline{D^{\prime}}}(\lambda^{\prime}).

(15)

Let
\varphi\colon V\to U
a birational morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties such that
\varphi^{*}Y_{\bullet}
is an admissible flag of
V
. Then
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\varphi^{*}\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})
,
\Delta(\varphi^{*}D)^{\circ}=\Delta(D)^{\circ}
and
G_{\varphi^{*}\overline{D}}=G_{\overline{D}}
on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
.

Proof.

Let
\overline{V}_{\bullet}=\{\overline{V}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\coloneq\{(H_{+}^{0}(U,m\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m,\sup})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
(resp.
\overline{V_{\bullet}^{\prime}}=\{\overline{V_{m}^{\prime}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\coloneq\{(H_{+}^{0}(U,m\overline{D^{\prime}}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m,\sup}^{\prime})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
) be the graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundles corresponding to
\overline{D}
(resp.
\overline{D^{\prime}}
),
\mathcal{H}
the Harder-Narasimhan filtration on
V_{\bullet}
(resp.
V_{\bullet}^{\prime}
) induced by
\overline{D}
(resp.
\overline{D}^{\prime}
) and
\rho,\widetilde{\rho},\Gamma^{t}(V_{\bullet})
(resp.
\rho^{\prime},\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}
,
\Gamma^{t}(V_{\bullet}^{\prime})
) the corresponding functions and semi-group given in
4.3.1
.

(10)
This is from
[
CM20
, Remark 6.3.21 (2)]
.

(11)
For any
m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, let
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m,c,\sup}
be the sup-norm on
H^{0}(U,m\overline{D}(c))
. Then

{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m,c,\sup,\omega}=e^{-c(\omega)}{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m,\sup,\omega}

on
H_{+}^{0}(U,m\overline{D})=H_{+}^{0}(U,m\overline{D}(c))
. This equality above of sup-norms now easily implies
(11)
by the construction of concave transforms in
4.3.1
.

(12)
The effectivity relation
\overline{D}\leq\overline{D^{\prime}}
implies that there is an injective
K
-linear map

f_{m}\colon V_{m}\hookrightarrow V_{m}^{\prime}

for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
which is furthermore norm-contractive at every
\omega\in\Omega
, i.e.
\|f_{m}\|_{\omega}\leq 1
for all
\omega\in\Omega
(see
2.2.3
for the notation). This implies that
h(f_{m})\leq 0
. By
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.3.49]
we have that
\mathcal{H}^{t}(V_{m})\subseteq\mathcal{H}^{t}(V_{m}^{\prime})
since
h(f_{m})\leq 0
and the filtrations are non-increasing. This implies that
\rho\leq\rho^{\prime},\widetilde{\rho}\leq\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}
, hence
\Gamma^{t}(V_{\bullet})\subset\Gamma^{t}(V_{\bullet}^{\prime})
, for any
t
. By the construction of
G_{\overline{D}},G_{\overline{D^{\prime}}}
, we have
G_{\overline{D}}\leq G_{\overline{D^{\prime}}}
, this completes the proof of
(12)
.

(13)
From the discussion in
4.3.1
, we have that
\sup\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)=\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})
. It remains to show that
\widehat{\mu}_{\min}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})\leq G_{\overline{D}}
. Notice that
\rho(m,\gamma)\geq\widehat{\mu}_{\min}(\overline{V_{m}})
for any
(m,\gamma)\in\Gamma(V_{\bullet})
(see
4.3.1
). For any
t<\widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\min}(\overline{D})
, by definition we have that
\widehat{\mu}_{\min}(\overline{V_{m}})>mt
for all large enough
m
. Then for any large enough
m
with
(m,\gamma)\in\Gamma(V_{\bullet})
, we conclude that
\rho(m,\gamma)\geq\widehat{\mu}_{\min}(\overline{V_{m}})>mt
which in turn implies
\widetilde{\rho}(m,\gamma)>mt
for all
(m,\gamma)\in\Gamma(V_{\bullet})
by definition. In particular, we conclude that for any
t<\widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\min}(\overline{D})
,
\Gamma^{t}(V_{\bullet})=\Gamma(V_{\bullet})
,
\Delta(\Gamma^{t}(V_{\bullet}))=\Delta(V_{\bullet})
which clearly shows that
G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\geq t
for all
\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}
. As
t<\widehat{\mu}^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\min}(\overline{D})
was arbitrary we deduce that
\widehat{\mu}_{\min}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})\leq G_{\overline{D}}
.

(14)
This is from
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.3.28]
, notice that the condition for
\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W_{n})
in
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.3.28]
holds, see
4.3.1
.

(15)

Since the sup-norm is stable under birational morphism, it suffices to show that
H^{0}(V,\varphi^{*}D)=H^{0}(U,D)
which will imply the corresponding graded algebras of adelic vector bundles associated to
\varphi^{*}\overline{D},\overline{D}
, respectively, are isomorphic, hence proves
(15)
. We have a homomorphism
H^{0}(U,{D})\to H^{0}(V,\varphi^{*}{D})
which is injective since
\varphi
is birational, it remains to show that if
f\in K(V)\simeq K(U)
such that
\widehat{\mathrm{div}}(f)+\varphi^{*}\overline{D}\geq 0
, then
\widehat{\mathrm{div}}(f)+D\geq 0
. It suffices to show that
\varphi^{*}\overline{D}\geq 0
implies
\overline{D}\geq 0
. If
\varphi^{*}\overline{D}\geq 0
, let
(\overline{D_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
be a sequence of
S
-metrized divisors in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}
decreasingly converging to
\overline{D}
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology for some weak boundary divisor
\overline{B}
of
U
. Let
X_{j}
be a normal projective
K
-model of
U
such that
\overline{D_{j}}=(D_{j},g_{j})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X_{j})
. Let
X_{j}^{\prime}
be a projective
K
-model of
V
such that the extension
\varphi_{j}\colon X_{j}^{\prime}\to X_{j}
of
\varphi
exists. Moreover, since
\varphi^{*}\overline{D_{j}}\geq\varphi^{*}\overline{D}\geq 0
in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, we can assume that
\varphi_{j}^{*}\overline{D_{j}}\geq 0
in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X_{j}^{\prime})
. This implies that
g_{j}\circ\varphi_{j}\geq 0
and the Weil divisor corresponding to
\varphi_{j}^{*}{D}_{j}
is non-negative, so
g_{j}\geq 0
and the Weil divisor corresponding to
\overline{D_{j}}
is non-negative. Since
X_{j}
is normal, so
\overline{D_{j}}\geq 0
. This completes the proof of
(15)
.
∎

Remark 4.3.4
.

Note that if
\overline{D}\leq\overline{D^{\prime}}
in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, as in the proof of
Proposition

4.3.3

(12)
, we have that
\widehat{\deg}_{+}(H_{+}^{0}(U,\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup})\leq\widehat{\deg}_{+}(H_{+}^{0}(U,\overline{D^{\prime}}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup})
. In particular,
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D^{\prime}})
.

Corollary 4.3.5
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. Then
\overline{D}
is big if and only if
D
is big and
\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>0
.

Proof.

As argued in the proof of
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.4.18]
, the corollary is from
Proposition

4.3.3

(13)
and
Theorem

4.3.2
. For the convenience of readers, we give a complete proof.
Let
\overline{V}_{\bullet}=\{\overline{V}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\coloneq\{(H_{+}^{0}(U,m\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m,\sup})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
be the graded
K
-algebra of adelic vector bundle corresponding to
\overline{D}
.

If
\overline{D}
is big, then for big enough
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{V_{m}})>0
. By
[
CM20
, Remark 4.3.53]
we have that

\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{V_{m}})\leq\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})\cdot\widehat{\mu}_{1}(\overline{V_{m}}).

Then we can see that

0<\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{\deg_{+}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m^{d+1}}\leq\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(V_{m})}{m^{d}}\cdot\frac{\widehat{\mu}_{1}(\overline{V_{m}})}{m}=\mathrm{vol}(D)\cdot\widehat{\mu}^{\text{asy}}_{\max}(\overline{D}).

This then clearly implies that
\mathrm{vol}(D)>0
and
\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>0
.

Conversely, assume that
D
is big and
\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>0
. Since
D
is big, we have that
\Delta(D)^{\circ}\not=\emptyset
. Since
\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>0
and
G_{\overline{D}}
is continuous on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
, there is a non-empty open subset
W
of
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
such that
G_{\overline{D}}>0
on
W
. By
Theorem

4.3.2
, we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}\max\{G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda),0\}\,d\lambda\geq(d+1)!\int_{W}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda>0,

which implies that
\overline{D}
is big.
∎

Corollary 4.3.6
.

Let
\overline{D_{1}}
,
\overline{D_{2}}\in\widetilde{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. If
\overline{D_{1}}
,
\overline{D_{2}}
are big. Then

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D_{1}}+\overline{D_{2}})^{\frac{1}{d+1}}\geq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D_{1}})^{\frac{1}{d+1}}+\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D_{2}})^{\frac{1}{d+1}}.

In particular,
\overline{D_{1}}+\overline{D_{2}}
is big.

Proof.

Arguing as in the proof of
[
CM20
, Theorem 6.4.7]
, we easily deduce the corollary from
Proposition

4.3.3

(14)
,
Theorem

4.3.2
and the classical Brunn-Minkowski inequality.
∎

Remark 4.3.7
.

An easy observation from the corollary is that, if
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
is big, then for any
\overline{M}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{M})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{M}+\overline{D}).

Indeed, it is trivial if
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{M})=0
, otherwise the inequality is from the corollary.

For further application, we give the following definition.

Definition 4.3.8
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
with
D
big and
G_{\overline{D}}
the concave transform of
\overline{D}
given in
4.3.1
. We define the
numerical
\chi
-volume
of
\overline{D}
as

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})\coloneq(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\ d\lambda.

By
Theorem

4.3.2
, we have that
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})\geq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})
with equality if
\widehat{\mu}_{\min}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>-\infty
.

Next we record an easy corollary of the previous theorem which will be useful later.

Corollary 4.3.9
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
with
D
big. If
G_{\overline{D}}\geq 0
on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
, then
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})
. More generally, if
\inf\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)>-\infty
, then there exists
c\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
such that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}(c))=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D}({c}))
.

Proof.

The proof is immediate from the two statements of
Theorem

4.3.2
and
Proposition

4.3.3

(11)
.
∎

4.4.
Continuity of arithmetic volumes

Lemma 4.4.1
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
with
D
big. Then there is a non-negative integrable function
c\colon\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that the twist
\overline{D}(c)\coloneqq\overline{D}+(0,c)
is big.

Proof.

Let
\alpha\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}
and
r\coloneq G_{\overline{D}}(\alpha)>-\infty
. There is a function
c\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)_{\geq 0}
such that
\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)>-r
. Then clearly from
Proposition

4.3.3

(11)
we have that

G_{\overline{D}(c)}(\alpha)=G_{\overline{D}}(\alpha)+\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)={r+\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)}>0

and thus we have
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}(c))>0
by
Theorem

4.3.2
.
∎

We next show that the big cone is open along arbitrary directions. This is the quasi-projective analogue of
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.4.23]
.

Lemma 4.4.2
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g),\overline{M}=(M,f)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. If
\overline{D}
is big, then there is an
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
such that
n\overline{D}+\overline{M}
is big for all
n\geq n_{0}
.

Proof.

For any
\overline{D^{\prime}}=(D^{\prime},g^{\prime})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
with
D^{\prime}
big, we set
\widetilde{G}_{\overline{D^{\prime}}}\coloneqq\max\{G_{\overline{D^{\prime}}},0\}
for simplicity. By bigness of
\overline{D}
, we know that the underlying compactified (geometric) divisor
D
is big from
Corollary

4.3.5
. Hence by continuity of geometric volumes, there is an integer
m
such that
mD+M
is big. On the other hand, by
Proposition

4.3.3

(11)
, for any integrable function
c\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, we have that

G_{\overline{D}(c)}=G_{\overline{D}}+\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)

which clearly implies

\widetilde{G}_{\overline{D}}(\alpha)\leq\widetilde{G}_{\overline{D}(c)}(\alpha)\leq\widetilde{G}_{\overline{D}}(\alpha)+\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)

for all
\alpha\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}
. Similarly we have

\widetilde{G}_{\overline{D}}(\alpha)\geq\widetilde{G}_{\overline{D}(-c)}(\alpha)\geq\widetilde{G}_{\overline{D}}(\alpha)-\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega),

for all
\alpha\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}
.
By
Theorem

4.3.2
the above then clearly implies

(48)

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}(c))\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})+(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}(D)\cdot\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega).

(49)

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})\geq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}(-c))\geq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})-(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}(D)\cdot\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega).

By
Lemma

4.4.1
, there is a non-negative integrable function
c\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
(m\overline{D}+\overline{M})(c)
is big. Applying our observation in (
49
) to the non-negative integrable function
c/N
for any positive integer
N\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, we get

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}\left(\overline{D}\left(c/N\right)\right)\geq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})-\frac{(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}(D)}{N}\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega).

Since
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})>0
and
c
is an integrable function, we can choose a large integer
N
such that
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}((\overline{D}(c/N))>0
. Now we write

(m+N)\overline{D}+\overline{M}=(m\overline{D}+\overline{M})(c)+N\overline{D}(-c/N)

which is clearly big by choice of
c
and
N
and
Corollary

4.3.6
. Now clearly
n_{0}=m+N
satisfies the claim again by
Corollary

4.3.6
.
∎

We now state the main theorem which is the quasi-projective analogue of
[
CM20
, Theorem 6.4.24]
.

Theorem 4.4.3
.

Let
(\overline{D_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
be a sequence in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{cpt}}
converging to
\overline{D}=(D,g)
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology for some weak boundary divisor
\overline{B}=(B,g_{B})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}
. Then

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=\lim_{j\to\infty}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D_{j}}).

Proof.

There is a sequence
(\varepsilon_{j})_{j\geq 0}\subset\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
converging to
0
such that

\overline{D}-\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B}\leq\overline{D_{j}}\leq\overline{D}+\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B},

Then by
Remark

4.3.4
,

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}-\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D_{j}})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}+\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B}).

So it suffices to show that

(50)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=\lim_{\mathbb{Q}\ni t\to 0}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}+t\overline{B}).

We firstly consider the case where
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
is not big, i.e.
{\mathrm{vol}}(D)=0
. Notice that in this case,
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=0
by
Corollary

4.3.5
.

•

For any
t\in\mathbb{Q}_{\leq 0}
,
\mathrm{vol}(D+tB)=0
, so
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}+t\overline{B})=0
by
Corollary

4.3.5
which implies that

(51)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=\lim_{\mathbb{Q}_{\leq 0}\ni t\to 0}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}+t\overline{B}).

•

If
{\mathrm{vol}}(D+tB)=0
for
t\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
small enough, then
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}+t\overline{B})=0
when
t\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
is small by
Corollary

4.3.5
, so

\lim\limits_{\mathbb{Q}_{>0}\ni t\to 0}=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}+t\overline{B})=0.

If
{\mathrm{vol}}(D+tB)>0
, i.e.
D+tB
is big, for any
t\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
, by
Proposition

4.3.3

(12)

(13)
,
Lemma

4.2.5
, we have that

G_{\overline{D}+t\overline{B}}\leq G_{\overline{D}+\overline{B}}\leq\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D}+\overline{B})<\infty

on
\Delta(D+tB)
for any
t\in\mathbb{Q}_{<1}
. By
Theorem

4.3.2
and
Lemma

3.5.4
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}+t\overline{B})=

\displaystyle(d+1)!\cdot\int_{\Delta(D+tB)}\max\{G_{\overline{D}+t\overline{B}}(\lambda),0\}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(d+1)!\cdot\max\{\mu^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\max}(\overline{D}+\overline{B}),0\}\cdot\frac{1}{d!}\cdot\mathrm{vol}(D+tB)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(d+1)\cdot\max\{\mu^{\mathrm{asy}}_{\max}(\overline{D}+\overline{B}),0\}\cdot\mathrm{vol}(D+tB)

for any
t\in\mathbb{Q}_{<1}
. So

\lim\limits_{\mathbb{Q}_{>0}\ni t\to 0}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}+t\overline{B})=0

by the continuity of geometric volumes, see
3.1.3

(7)
.

From our discussion above, (
51
) holds when
D
is not big.

If
D
is big, by
Lemma

4.4.1
, we can find a non-negative integrable function
c\colon\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that
\overline{D}(c)=(D,g+c)
is big. Then by
Lemma

4.4.2
, we can choose a positive integer
n
such that
n\overline{D}(c)\pm\overline{B}
is big. Let
t\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
. Then by
Remark

4.3.7
we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}-nt\cdot\overline{D}(c))\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}-t\overline{B})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}+nt\cdot\overline{D}(c))

and

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}-nt\cdot\overline{D}(c))\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}+t\overline{B})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}+nt\cdot\overline{D}(c)),

since
nt\overline{D}(c)\pm t\overline{B}
is big.
But

\overline{D}-nt\cdot\overline{D}(c)=(1-nt)\overline{D}-nt\cdot(0,c)=(1-nt)\cdot\left(\overline{D}-\frac{nt}{1-nt}(0,c)\right).

Similarly,

\overline{D}+nt\cdot\overline{D}(c)=(1+nt)\cdot\left(\overline{D}+\frac{nt}{1+nt}(0,c)\right).

Thus it reduces to showing

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=\lim_{\mathbb{Q}\ni t\to 0}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}\left((1+nt)\cdot\left(\overline{D}+\frac{nt}{1+nt}(0,c)\right)\right).

Using homogeneity of arithmetic volumes and the fact that
\lim\limits_{t\to 0}(1+nt)=1
, it is enough to show that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=\lim_{\mathbb{Q}\ni t\to 0}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}\left(\overline{D}+\frac{nt}{1+nt}(0,c)\right).

Since
\lim\limits_{t\to 0}\frac{nt}{1-nt}=0
, it amounts showing that for any non-negative integrable function
c\colon\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we have

\lim_{\mathbb{Q}\ni t\to 0}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}+t(0,c))=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})).

However from (
48
), we have that for
t>0
,

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}(tc))\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})+t(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}(D)\cdot\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega).

Similarly from (
49
) on the other side, we have

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})\geq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}(-tc))\geq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})-t(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}(D)\cdot\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega).

The claim now clearly follows by letting
t\to 0
as
c
is an integrable function. This completed the proof of
Theorem

4.4.3
.
∎

Corollary 4.4.4
.

Let
\overline{D},\overline{M_{1}},\dots,\overline{M_{r}}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. Then

\lim\limits_{\mathbb{Q}^{r}\ni(t_{1},\dots,t_{r})\to 0}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}+t_{1}\overline{M_{1}}+\cdots+t_{r}\overline{M_{r}})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}).

Proof.

For any
1\leq i\leq r
, let
\overline{M_{i}^{\prime}}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}
be an effective
S
-metric divisor on some projective model of
U
such that
