\overline{M_{i}^{\prime}}\pm\overline{M_{i}}\geq 0
. Denote
\overline{M}\coloneq\overline{M_{1}^{\prime}}+\cdots+\overline{M_{r}^{\prime}}
. It suffices to show that

(52)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=\lim_{\mathbb{Q}\ni t\to 0}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}+t\overline{M}).

By
Proposition

4.3.3

(15)
, after shrinking
U
, we may assume that
\overline{M}
is a weak boundary divisor of
U
. Then (
52
) is exactly (
50
) which is proved. This proved the corollary.
∎

4.5.
Essential minimum

Definition 4.5.1
.

Let
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. We define the
essential minimum
of
\overline{D}
as

\zeta_{\mathrm{ess}}(\overline{D})\coloneq\sup\limits_{\text{$V\subset U$ open}}\inf\limits_{x\in V(\overline{K})}h_{\overline{D}}(x).

We next show
Theorem

4.5.3
which gives an analogue for compactified divisors of the fundamental inequality of Zhang. To prove this theorem, we need the following definition from
[
CM20
, §1.1.10, Remark 1.1.67]
.

4.5.2
.

Let
\overline{V}=(V,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{V}),\overline{W}=(W,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{W})
be
S
-norm vector spaces and
\psi\colon[0,1]\to[0,1]
such that
\max\{t,1-t\}\leq\psi(t)
for any
t\in[0,1]
. For any
(x,y)\in V_{\omega}\oplus W_{\omega}
, set

\|(x,y)\|_{\psi,\omega}\coloneq(\|x\|_{V,\omega}+\|y\|_{W,\omega})\cdot\psi\left(\frac{\|x\|_{V,\omega}}{\|x\|_{V,\omega}+\|y\|_{W,\omega}}\right).

We call
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\psi}=\{{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\psi,\omega}\}_{\omega\in\Omega}
the
\psi
-direct sum
of
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{V}
and
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{W}
.

A basis
\{e_{1},\dots,e_{r}\}\subset V
is a
Hadamard basis
if

\|e_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge e_{r}\|_{V,\det,\omega}=\|e_{1}\|_{V,\omega}\cdots\|e_{r}\|_{V,\omega},

for every
\omega\in\Omega
where
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{V,\det}=\{{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{V,\det,\omega}\}_{\omega\in\Omega}
is the determinant norm of
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{V}
defined in
2.2.3
.

Theorem 4.5.3
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. Then

(53)

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathrm{ess}}(\overline{D})\geq\widehat{\mu}_{\max}(\overline{D})\ \text{ and }\ \zeta_{\mathrm{ess}}(\overline{D})\geq\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D}).

In particular, if
D
is big, then

(54)

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathrm{ess}}(\overline{D})\geq\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})\geq\frac{\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})}{(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}(D)}\geq\frac{\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})}{(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}(D)}.

Proof.

Notice that
\zeta_{\mathrm{ess}}(m\overline{D})=m\zeta_{\mathrm{ess}}(\overline{D})
, then the second inequality of (
53
) is a consequence of the first inequality of (
53
). If
D
is big, let
\Delta(D)
be the Okounkov body of
D
defined in
3.5.3
. By
Proposition

4.3.3

(13)
and
Lemma

3.5.4
,
we have that

\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})\geq\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(D))}\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\ d\lambda=\frac{d!}{\mathrm{vol}(D)}\cdot\frac{\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})}{(d+1)!}=\frac{\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})}{(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}(D)}.

This shows the second inequality of (
54
). Hence it suffices to show the first inequality of (
53
).

We follow the idea of the proof of
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.4.4]
to show that
\zeta_{\mathrm{ess}}(\overline{D})\geq\widehat{\mu}_{\max}(\overline{D})
. Let
S_{\overline{K}}=(\overline{K},\Omega_{\overline{K}},\mathcal{A}_{\overline{K}},\nu_{\overline{K}})
be the adelic curve associated to the algebraic closure
\overline{K}
induced by
S
and let
\overline{V}=(V,{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|})=(H_{+}^{0}(U,\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup})
be the adelic vector bundle of auxiliary global section associated to
\overline{D}
defined in
Definition

4.1.2
. Let
t\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\zeta_{\mathrm{ess}}(\overline{D})<t
. Then there is an infinite subset
\Lambda
of
U(\overline{K})
such that
\Lambda
is dense in
U
and
h_{\overline{D}}(x)<t
for all
x\in\Lambda
. Let
W\subset V
be an arbitrary subspace. Notice that
V
is a subspace of
H^{0}(U,D|_{U})
and hence a subspace of
K(U)
since
U
is integral. We consider the evaluation map

W\otimes_{K}\overline{K}\to\prod\limits_{x\in\Lambda}\kappa(x)

which is injective since
\Lambda
is dense in
U
, where
\kappa(x)
is the residue field of
x\in\Lambda
. Then there are points
x_{1},\dots,x_{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)}\in\Lambda
such that the evaluation map
\varphi\colon W\otimes_{K}\overline{K}\to\bigoplus\limits_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)}\kappa(x_{i})
is bijective. For any
\omega\in\Omega_{\overline{K}}
, let
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{i,\omega}
be a norm of
\kappa(x_{i})_{\omega}\coloneq\kappa(x_{i})\otimes_{\overline{K}}\overline{K}_{\omega}
given by
\|1\|_{i,\omega}\coloneq\exp(g_{\pi(\omega)}(\sigma(x_{i})))
where
\pi\colon\Omega_{\overline{K}}\to\Omega_{K}
is the canonical map and
\sigma\colon(U_{\overline{K}})^{\mathrm{an}}_{\omega}\to U_{\pi(\omega)}^{\mathrm{an}}
is the canonical morphism as analytic spaces. We equip
\bigoplus\limits_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)}\kappa(x_{i})
with the
\psi
-direct sum
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\psi}=\{{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\psi,\omega}\}_{\omega\in\Omega}
, where
\psi\colon[0,1]\to[0,1],\ \ t\mapsto\max\{t,1-t\}
, i.e. for any
(s_{1},\dots,s_{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)})\in\bigoplus\limits_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)}\kappa(x_{i})_{\omega}
, we have that

\|(s_{1},\dots,s_{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)})\|_{\psi,\omega}=\max\{\|s_{1}\|_{1,\omega},\dots,\|s_{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)}\|_{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W),\omega}\}.

If we denote
\{e_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)}
a basis of
\bigoplus\limits_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)}\kappa(x_{i})
such that
e_{i}\in\kappa(x_{i})
, then this basis is orthogonal with respect to
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\psi,\omega}
for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\overline{K}}
. By
[
CM20
, Proposition 1.2.23]
,
\{e_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)}
is a Hadamard basis for
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\psi,\omega}
for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\overline{K}}
. In particular,

\widehat{\deg}\left(\bigoplus\limits_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)}\left(\kappa(x_{i}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\psi}\right)\right)=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)}h_{\overline{D}}(x_{i})\leq\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)\cdot t.

Moreover, for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\overline{K}}
the operator norm
\|\varphi\|_{\omega}\leq 1
. By
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.3.18]
, one has

\widehat{\mu}(\overline{W})=\widehat{\mu}(\overline{W}\otimes_{K}\overline{K})\leq\frac{1}{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)}\widehat{\deg}\left(\bigoplus\limits_{i=1}^{\operatorname{dim}\nolimits_{K}(W)}\left(\kappa(x_{i}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\psi}\right)\right)\leq t.

This completes the proof of (
53
).
∎

4.6.
Height Inequality

We end this section by obtaining a height inequality which is a generalization of the height inequalities obtained in
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.3.5 (1)(3)]
. Note that this height inequality has been used later as one of the tools to obtain a uniform Bogomolov type result, see
[
Yua21
, §4.5]
. We generalize this in the setting of adelic curves.

Theorem 4.6.1
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g),\overline{M}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
.

(16)

If
\overline{D}
is big, then there exists
\epsilon>0
and a non-empty Zariski open subset
V
of
U
such that

h_{\overline{D}}(x)\geq\epsilon\cdot h_{\overline{M}}(x).

(17)

If
D
is big, then there exists
\epsilon>0
,
c\in\mathbb{R}
and a non-empty Zariski open subset
V
of
U
such that

h_{\overline{D}}(x)\geq\epsilon\cdot h_{\overline{M}}(x)-c\text{ for all }x\in V(\overline{K}).

.

Proof.

We see that
(16)
implies that
(17)
. In fact, if
D
is big, then there exists
c_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}
such that
G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)>-c_{0}
. We take a function
{c}\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
with
c_{0}=\int_{\Omega}\widetilde{c}(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)
. By
Proposition

4.3.3

(11)

(13)
,

\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D}(c))\geq G_{\overline{D}({c})}(\lambda)=G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)+c_{0}>0.

Then
\overline{D}({c})
is big by
Corollary

4.3.5
. We can apply
(16)
to
\overline{D}({c})
. This gives
(17)
by the simple relation

h_{\overline{D}({c})}(x)=h_{\overline{D}}(x)+\int_{\Omega}{c}(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)=h_{\overline{D}}(x)+c_{0}.

It remains to show
(16)
. By
Corollary

4.4.4
, there is
\epsilon\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
such that
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}-\epsilon\overline{M})>0
, i.e.
\overline{D}-\epsilon\overline{M}
is big. By
Theorem

4.5.3
and
Corollary

4.3.5
, we then have that

\zeta_{\mathrm{ess}}(\overline{D}-\epsilon\cdot\overline{M})\geq\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D}-\epsilon\cdot\overline{M})>0.

By definition of the essential minima, we then have a non-empty Zariski open subset
V
of
U
such that

h_{\overline{D}-\epsilon\cdot\overline{M}}(x)=h_{\overline{D}}(x)-\epsilon\cdot h_{\overline{M}}(x)>0\text{ for all }x\in V(\overline{K})

which readily proves
(16)
.
∎

The generalization of
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.3.5 (2)]
will be seen in
Proposition

5.2.5
as an application of arithmetic Hilbert-Samuel formula.

5.
Arithmetic Hilbert-Samuel for singular metrics

In this section, we will build the relation between the numerical
\chi
-volume and arithmetic auto-intersection number of a given relatively nef compactified
S
-metrized divisor, i.e. the arithmetic Hilbert-Samuel formula. We fix an adelic curve
S=(K,\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
satisfying the condition
(a)

(b)

(c)
in §
3.3
, and assume that
S
has either the strong tensorial minimal slope property of level
\geq C_{0}
or the Minkowski property of level
\geq C_{0}
for some
C\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
(see
2.5.1
). We also fix a
d
-dimensional normal quasi-projective variety
U
over
K
and an admissible flag
Y_{\bullet}
of
U_{\overline{K}}
, see
3.5.1
.

5.1.
Behaviour of the concave transform under perturbations

Recall that for
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
with
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
big, we denote by
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
the topological interior of the Okounkov body of
D
calculated with respect to the fixed flag
Y_{\bullet}
in
U
, see
3.5.3
. Furthermore we have the concave transform
G_{\overline{D}}
on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
constructed in
4.3.1
corresponding to
\overline{D}
.

5.1.1
.

Let
(\overline{D_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}=(D_{j},g_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
be a Cauchy sequence in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{cpt}}
converging to
\overline{D}=(D,g)
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology for some weak boundary divisor
\overline{B}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}
. If
{D}
is big, by the continuity of (geometric) volume, see
3.1.3

(7)
,
D_{j}
is big for
j
large enough. Hence
G_{\overline{D_{j}}}
is well-defined for
j
large enough. By
Lemma

3.5.6
, we can conclude that every
\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}
is in
\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}
for
j
large enough. Hence for any
\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}
, it makes sense to consider the limits
\limsup\limits_{j\to\infty}G_{\overline{D_{j}}}(\lambda)
and
\liminf\limits_{j\to\infty}G_{\overline{D_{j}}}(\lambda)
.

Theorem 5.1.2
.

Let
(\overline{D_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
be a sequence in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{cpt}}
converging to
\overline{D}=(D,g)
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology for some weak boundary divisor
\overline{B}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}
. If
D
is big, then

(55)

\displaystyle\lim_{j\to\infty}G_{\overline{D_{j}}}=G_{\overline{D}}

pointwise on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
(as we discussed in
5.1.1
, the limit makes sense for
j
large enough).

Proof.

We prove the theorem in the following steps.

Step 1:
If the theorem holds when
\overline{D_{j}}
is decreasing or
\overline{D_{j}}
is increasing, then it holds in general.

Let
\overline{B}
be a weak boundary divisor in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}
, and
(\varepsilon_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
a sequence in
\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
such that
\overline{D}-\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B}\leq\overline{D_{j}}\leq\overline{D}+\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B}
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
and
\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\varepsilon_{j}=0
. Let
\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}
. By
Proposition

4.3.3

(12)
, we have that

(56)

\displaystyle G_{\overline{D}-\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B}}(\lambda)\leq G_{\overline{D_{j}}}(\lambda)\leq G_{\overline{D}+\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B}}(\lambda).

It suffices to show that

(57)

\displaystyle G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)=\limsup\limits_{j\to\infty}G_{\overline{D}+\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B}}(\lambda)=\liminf\limits_{j\to\infty}G_{\overline{D}-\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B}}(\lambda).

We only consider the the sequence
(\overline{D}+\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
, it is similar for
(\overline{D}-\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
.
Let
(\varepsilon_{j_{i}})_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
be a subsequence of
(\varepsilon_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
such that

\lim\limits_{i\to\infty}G_{\overline{D}+\varepsilon_{j_{i}}\overline{B}}(\lambda)=\limsup\limits_{j\to\infty}G_{\overline{D}+\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B}}(\lambda).

Furthermore, we can assume that
\varepsilon_{j_{i}}\leq 1/i
for any
i\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
. Since the theorem holds for
(\overline{D}+\frac{1}{i}\overline{B})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
by our assumption, from
Proposition

4.3.3

(12)
, we have that

G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\leq\limsup\limits_{j\to\infty}G_{\overline{D}+\varepsilon_{j}\overline{B}}(\lambda)=\lim\limits_{i\to\infty}G_{\overline{D}+\varepsilon_{j_{i}}\overline{B}}(\lambda)\leq\lim\limits_{i\to\infty}G_{\overline{D}+\frac{1}{i}\cdot\overline{B}}(\lambda)=G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda).

This shows the first equality of (
57
). The proof for the second equality of (
57
) is similar. Since
\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}
is arbitrary, we have that Step 1 holds.

In the following, we fix an integrable function
c\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
such that
\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)=1
. For any
\alpha>0
, we set

\Delta(D)^{\alpha}\coloneq\{\lambda\in\Delta(D)\mid G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\geq-\alpha\},

and denote
\overline{D}(\alpha)\coloneq\overline{D}+(0,\alpha c)
, similarly for
\overline{D_{j}}(\alpha)
.

Step 2:
If
\overline{D_{j}}
is decreasing, then the theorem holds.

If
\overline{D_{j}}
is decreasing, then for any
\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}
,
G_{\overline{D_{j}}}(\lambda)\geq G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)
and
\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}G_{\overline{D_{j}}}(\lambda)
exists by
Proposition

4.3.3

(12)
. So it suffices to show that
\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}G_{\overline{D_{j}}}\leq G_{\overline{D}}
almost everywhere on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
. For any
r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, we set

{K_{\mathrm{Sing},r}^{+}}\coloneq\{\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}\mid\lim_{j\to\infty}G_{\overline{D_{j}}}(\lambda)>G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)+r\},

\Delta(D)^{\alpha,r}_{+}\coloneq\Delta(D)^{\alpha}\cap K_{\mathrm{Sing},r}^{+}\subset\Delta(D)^{\circ}.

We claim that the measure
\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(D)^{\alpha,r}_{+})
of
\Delta(D)^{\alpha,r}_{+}
is
0
for any
r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0},\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
. Indeed, given
r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0},\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, for any
\lambda\in\Delta(D)_{+}^{\alpha,r}
we have

(58)

G_{\overline{D_{j}}(\alpha)}(\lambda)\geq\lim_{i\to\infty}G_{\overline{D_{i}}(\alpha)}(\lambda)>G_{\overline{D}(\alpha)}(\lambda)+r\geq r>0

thanks to
Proposition

4.3.3

(11)
. Write
f_{\overline{D}(\alpha)}\coloneq\max\{G_{\overline{D}(\alpha)},0\}
on
\Delta(D)
(resp.
f_{\overline{D}_{j}(\alpha)}\coloneq\max\{G_{\overline{D_{j}}(\alpha)},0\}
on
\Delta(D_{j})
), then
f_{{\overline{D_{j}}}(\alpha)}\geq f_{\overline{D}(\alpha)}
on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
.
For all
\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\alpha,r}_{+}\subseteq\Delta(D)^{\circ}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, by (
58
) we have

f_{\overline{D_{j}}(\alpha)}(\lambda)=G_{\overline{D_{j}}(\alpha)}(\lambda),\ \ f_{\overline{D}(\alpha)}(\lambda)=G_{\overline{D}(\alpha)}(\lambda),

then

(59)

f_{\overline{D_{j}}(\alpha)}(\lambda)-f_{\overline{D}(\alpha)}(\lambda)=G_{\overline{D_{j}}(\alpha)}(\lambda)-G_{\overline{D}(\alpha)}(\lambda)=G_{\overline{D_{j}}}(\lambda)-G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)>r,

the last inequality holds since
\lambda\in{K_{\mathrm{Sing},r}^{+}}
.
Using the first claim of
Theorem

4.3.2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d+1)!}(\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D_{j}}(\alpha))-\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}(\alpha)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}\left(f_{\overline{D_{j}}(\alpha)}(\lambda)-f_{\overline{D}(\alpha)}(\lambda)\right)\,d\lambda+\int_{\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}\backslash\Delta(D)^{\circ}}f_{\overline{D_{j}}(\alpha)}(\lambda)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta(D)^{\alpha,r}_{+}}\left(f_{\overline{D_{j}}(\alpha)}(\lambda)-f_{\overline{D}(\alpha)}(\lambda)\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle r\cdot\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(D)^{\alpha,r}_{+})\geq 0.

By the continuity of arithmetic volumes, see
Theorem

4.4.3
, we have that the left-hand side of the equation above goes to
0
as
j\to\infty
. Hence the
\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(D)^{\alpha,r}_{+})=0
. This proves our claim. So
K_{\mathrm{Sing},r}^{+}=\bigcup\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}}\Delta(D)^{\alpha,r}_{+}
has measure
0
. Note the set of points where the mentioned pointwise convergence fails to happen is exactly given by
\bigcup\limits_{r\in\mathbb{Q}}K_{\mathrm{Sing},r}^{+}
. So (
55
) holds almost everywhere on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
. Since
\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}G_{\overline{D_{j}}}
and
G_{\overline{D}}
are concave, hence continuous on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
, we have that (
55
) holds pointwise on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
, which proves Step 2.

Step 3:
If
\overline{D_{j}}
is increasing, then the theorem holds.

If
\overline{D_{j}}
is increasing, as before, for any
\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}
, the limit
\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}G_{\overline{D_{j}}}(\lambda)
exists. For any
r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0},\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, we set

K_{\mathrm{Sing},r}^{-}\coloneq\{\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}\mid\lim_{j\to\infty}G_{\overline{D_{j}}}(\lambda)<G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)-r\},

\Delta(D)^{\alpha,r}_{-}\coloneq\Delta(D)^{\alpha}\cap K_{\mathrm{Sing},r}^{-}\subset\Delta(D)^{\circ},

\Delta(D_{j})^{\alpha,r}_{-}\coloneq\Delta(D)^{\alpha,r}_{-}\cap\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}\subset\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}.

As before, to show the theorem in this case, it suffices to show that the measure
\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(D)^{\alpha,r}_{-})=0
for any
r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0},\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.
Given
r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0},\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, we can assume that
\alpha\geq r
(notice that
\Delta(D)_{-}^{\alpha,r}
is increasing at
\alpha
). By
Lemma

3.5.7
we have that
\Delta(D)^{\alpha,r}_{-}=\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\Delta(D_{j})^{\alpha,r}_{-}
. Notice that
\Delta(D_{i})^{\alpha,r}\subset\Delta(D_{j})^{\alpha,r}
if
i\leq j
, then

(60)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(D_{j})^{\alpha,r}_{-})=\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(D)^{\alpha,r}_{-}).

For any
\lambda\in\Delta(D_{j})_{-}^{\alpha,r}
we have

(61)

G_{\overline{D_{j}}(2\alpha)}(\lambda)\leq\lim_{i\to\infty}G_{\overline{D_{i}}(2\alpha)}(\lambda)<G_{\overline{D}(2\alpha)}(\lambda)-r,

(62)

\displaystyle G_{\overline{D}(2\alpha)}(\lambda)-r\geq\alpha-r\geq 0

thanks to
Proposition

4.3.3

(11)
. Write
f_{\overline{D}(2\alpha)}\coloneq\max\{G_{\overline{D}(2\alpha)},0\}
on
\Delta(D)
(resp.
f_{\overline{D_{j}}(2\alpha)}\coloneq\max\{G_{\overline{D_{j}}(2\alpha)},0\}
on
\Delta(D_{j})
), then
f_{\overline{D_{j}}(2\alpha)}\leq f_{\overline{D}(2\alpha)}
on
\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}
.
For all
\lambda\in\Delta(D_{j})^{\alpha,r}_{-}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, by (
61
) and (
62
), we have

(63)

f_{\overline{D_{j}}(2\alpha)}(\lambda)=\max\{G_{\overline{D_{j}}(2\alpha)}(\lambda),0\}\leq G_{\overline{D}(2\alpha)}(\lambda)-r\leq f_{\overline{D}(2\alpha)}(\lambda)-r

Now we will apply a similar argument as the decreasing case, more precisely using
Theorem

4.3.2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d+1)!}(\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D_{j}}(2\alpha))-\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}(2\alpha))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}}(f_{\overline{D_{j}}(2\alpha)}(\lambda)-f_{\overline{D}(2\alpha)}(\lambda))\,d\lambda-\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}\setminus\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}}f_{\overline{D}(2\alpha)}(\lambda)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}}(f_{\overline{D_{j}}(2\alpha)}(\lambda)-f_{\overline{D}(2\alpha)}(\lambda))\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta(D_{j})^{\alpha,r}_{-}}(f_{\overline{D_{j}}(2\alpha)}(\lambda)-f_{\overline{D}(2\alpha)}(\lambda))\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-r\cdot\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(D_{j})^{\alpha,r}_{-})\leq 0.

By the continuity of arithmetic volumes, see
Theorem

4.4.3
, and (
60
), taking
j\to\infty
, we have that
\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta(D)^{\alpha,r}_{-})=0
. This implies Step 3. Hence we complete the proof.
∎

The following proposition is based on the fact that
S
has the strong tensorial minimal slope property or the Minkowski property (of level
\geq C_{0}
for some
C_{0}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
).

Proposition 5.1.3
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
. Then the following statements hold:

(10)

we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}.

(11)

if
D
is big,
G_{\overline{D}}\geq 0
on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
.

Proof.

We prove the proposition in the following steps.

Step 1:
If
S
has the strong tensorial minimal slope property of level
\geq C_{0}
for some
C_{0}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,
U=X
is projective and
\overline{D}
is
S
-ample in sense of
3.3.2
, then
\widehat{\mu}_{\min}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>0
.

Notice that
[
CM22
, Theorem 8.8.3]
holds for adelic curves with the strong tensorial property. Indeed, in the proof of
[
CM22
, Theorem 8.8.3]
, the condition that
K
is perfect is only required in the application of
[
CM20
, Theorem 7.2.4]
which holds for adelic curves with the strong tensorial property. Since
[
CM22
, Theorem 8.8.3]
implies that
[
CM22
, Proposition 9.1.2]
, Step 1 holds.

Step 2:
If
S
has the Minkowsi property of level
C_{0}
for some
C_{0}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,
U=X
is projective and
\overline{D}
is
S
-ample, then then
\widehat{\mu}_{\min}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>0
.

If
S
has the Minkowski property for level
C_{0}
for some
C_{0}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, by
[
CM20
, Theorem 7.2.1]
and the fact that restriction of
S
-ample on a closed subvariety is again
S
-ample, to show Step 2, it suffices to show the claim: there is
n\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
and
0\not\in s\in H^{0}(X,D)
such that
\widehat{\deg}(s)>0
, where
H^{0}(X,nD)
is endowed with the sup-norm, see
4.1.1
. By definition of
S
-ampleness, there is
\varepsilon
such that

(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid X)_{S}\geq\varepsilon D^{d}(\operatorname{dim}\nolimits(X)+1)>0.

Then
[
CM22
, Theorem B]
implies that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})\geq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid X)_{S}>0.

So
\overline{D}
is big. Hence
\overline{D}
is
strongly big
in the sense of
[
CM20
, Definition 6.4.19]
, i.e. our claim holds. This completed the proof of Step 2.

Step 3:
If
U=X
is projective and
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
, then
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid X)_{S}
. If, furthermore,
D
is big, then
G_{\overline{D}}\geq 0
.

We first consider the case where
D
is not big. Then
\overline{D}
is not big, i.e.
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=0
, by
[
CM20
, Proposition 6.4.18]
. On the other hand, by
[
CG24
, Proposition 7.10 (v), Proposition 6.34 (1)]
, we have that
(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid X)_{S}\geq 0
.
Hence we have the inequalities

0=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})\geq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid X)_{S}\geq 0

which implies that
\overline{D}=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid X)_{S}=0.

Assume that
D
is big. Let
\overline{A}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{+}(X)
be an
S
-ample divisor on
X
such that the point corresponding to the admissible flag
Y_{\bullet}
is not in
|A|
. Then for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
,
\overline{D}+\frac{1}{n}\overline{A}
is
S
-ample. This implies that
G_{\overline{D}+\frac{1}{n}\overline{A}}\geq 0
by Step 1 and Step 2. Let
V=X\setminus|A|
, and
i\colon V\to X
the corresponding open immersion. Notice that
i^{*}\left(\overline{D}+\frac{1}{n}\overline{A}\right)
converges to
\overline{D}
in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(V)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
with respect to the
\overline{A}
-boundary topology, by
Proposition

4.3.3

(15)
and
Theorem

5.1.2
, we have that

G_{\overline{D}}=G_{i^{*}\overline{D}}=\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}G_{i^{*}\left(\overline{D}+\frac{1}{n}\overline{A}\right)}=\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}G_{\overline{D}+\frac{1}{n}\overline{A}}\geq 0

almost everywhere on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
. Since
G_{\overline{D}}
is continuous on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
, so the statement holds in this case.

Step 4:
If
U
is quasi-projective and
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
, then
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}
. If, furthermore,
D
is big, then
G_{\overline{D}}\geq 0
.

For general case where
U
is quasi-projective and
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
, by
Remark

3.2.4
and
Proposition

4.3.3

(15)
, after shrinking
U
, we can assume that
\overline{D}
is the limit of a sequence
(\overline{D_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}=(D_{j},g_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}\subset N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(U)
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology for some weakly boundary divisor
\overline{B}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(U)
. Since
\overline{D_{j}}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(U)
, we can deduce from Step 3 and
Proposition

4.3.3

(15)
that
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D_{j}})=(\overline{D_{j}}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}
and
G_{\overline{D_{j}}}\geq 0
on
\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}
. By
Theorem

4.4.3
, we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D_{j}})=\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}(\overline{D_{j}}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}.

It
D
is big, then
D_{j}
is big for
j
large enough. So
G_{\overline{D_{j}}}\geq 0
on
\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}
by Step 3. By
Theorem

5.1.2
, we have that
G_{\overline{D}}\geq 0
on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
. ∎

5.2.
Arithmetic Hilbert-Samuel formula

Proposition 5.2.1
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{cpt}}
and
(\overline{D_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}=(D_{j},g_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
a sequence in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{cpt}}
converging to
\overline{{D}}
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology for some weak boundary divisor
\overline{B}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{CM}}
of
U
. Assume that
D_{j},D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
are big and one of the following condition holds:

(12)

there is
c_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\inf\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D_{j}}}(\lambda)
,
\inf\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)>c_{0}
for any
j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
;

(13)

\overline{D_{j}}
is decreasing and
D_{j}=D
in
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
for arbitrary large
j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
.

Then

\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{num}}_{\chi}(\overline{D_{j}})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{num}}_{\chi}(\overline{D}).

Proof.

(12)
Let
c_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\inf\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D_{j}}}(\lambda)
,
\inf\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)>c_{0}
for any
n\geq 1
. We fix an integrable function
c\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
such that
c_{0}=\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)
. By
Proposition

4.3.3

(11)
, we have that
G_{\overline{D_{j}}(-{c})}>0
on
\Delta(D_{j})^{\circ}
(resp.
G_{\overline{D}(-{c})}>0
on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
). By
Corollary

4.3.9
and
Lemma

3.5.4
, we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D_{j}})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D_{j}}(-{c}))+c_{0}(d+1)\mathrm{vol}(D_{j})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D_{j}}(-{c}))+c_{0}(d+1)\mathrm{vol}(D_{j}).

Similarly, we have
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}(-{c}))+c_{0}(d+1)\mathrm{vol}(D)
. By
3.1.3

(7)
and
Theorem

4.4.3
, we have that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D_{j}})

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D_{j}}(-c))+c_{0}(d+1)\mathrm{vol}(D_{j})\right)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}(-c))+c_{0}(d+1)\mathrm{vol}(D)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{{D}}).

(13)
By
Theorem

5.1.2
, the concave transforms
G_{\overline{D_{j}}}
decreasingly converges to
G_{\overline{D}}
pointwise on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
. Hence, by the monotone convergence theorem, we have that

\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D_{j}})=\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D_{j}}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda=(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D}).

∎

We are ready to prove the main result of this section.

Theorem 5.2.2
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
with
D
big. Then

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}.

In particular, if
\widehat{\mu}_{\min}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>-\infty
, then by
Theorem

4.3.2
, we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}.

Proof.

We prove the theorem in the following steps.

Step 1:
If
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
, then
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}
.

By
Proposition

5.1.3

(11)
and
Theorem

4.3.2
, we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})=(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(\overline{D})^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\,d\lambda=(d+1)!\int_{\Delta(\overline{D})^{\circ}}\max\{G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda),0\}\,d\lambda=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}).

By
Proposition

5.1.3

(10)
, we have that
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}
. This proves Step 1.

Step 2:
If there is
{c}\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
such that
\overline{D}(c)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
, then
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})=\overline{D}^{d+1}
.

Let
{c}\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
be the one in the assumption of Step 2. By
Proposition

4.3.3

(11)
and
Lemma

3.5.4
, we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D}(c))=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})+(d+1)\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)\cdot\mathrm{vol}(D).

On the other hand, by the properties of arithmetic intersection number on quasi-projective varieties (see
[
CG24
, Theorem 7.22]
), we can induce that

(\overline{D}({c})^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}+(d+1)\int_{\Omega}c(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)\cdot D^{d}.

By
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.2.2]
, we have
\mathrm{vol}(D)=D^{d}
. Combining these with Step 1, we easily see that Step 2 holds.

Step 3:
Theorem holds.

Let
\overline{D^{\prime}}=({D},g^{\prime})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
. By
Remark

6.3.2
and
Proposition

4.3.3

(15)
, we can shrink
U
and assume that there is a
\overline{B}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)
such that
\overline{D}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{d_{\overline{B}}}
(resp.
\overline{D^{\prime}}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(U)^{d_{\overline{B}}}
). We can assume that
g^{\prime}\geq g
, otherwise, by
[
CG24
, Lemma 10.3 (ii)]
, we can replace
g^{\prime}
by
\max\{g^{\prime},g\}
. By
[
CG24
, Lemma 10.6]
, there is an increasing sequence of
\nu
-integrable functions
(c_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}\subset\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
such that
\overline{D_{m}}=(D,g_{m}^{\prime})\coloneq(D,\max\{g,g^{\prime}-c_{m}\})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}
and
\overline{D_{m}}
converges decreasingly to
\overline{D}
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology. By
[
CG24
, Theorem 10.9 (v)]
, we have that

(64)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}(\overline{D_{m}}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}.

On the other hand, by
Proposition

5.2.1

(13)
, we have that

(65)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D_{m}})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D}).

Notice that
\overline{D_{m}}(c_{m})\geq\overline{D^{\prime}}
and
\overline{D^{\prime}}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
, so
\overline{D_{m}}(c_{m})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
by
[
CG24
, Lemma 10.3]
.
By Step 2, we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D_{m}})=(\overline{D_{m}}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}.

Take
m\to\infty
on both sides and by (
64
), (
65
), we prove the theorem.
∎

Remark 5.2.3
.

Theorem

5.2.2
gives another definition of arithmetic auto-intersection of relatively nef compactified line bundle in
[
CG24
]
when the underlying geometry line bundles are big by realising it as the integral of a concave function in analogy to the toric setting. In particular, the Hodge bundles on Shimura varieties and the Jacobi line bundles on the universal elliptic surface are
relatively nef compactified line bundles
.

The following corollary follows directly.

Corollary 5.2.4
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
with
D
big. Then

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})\geq(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}.

In particular, if
\overline{D}^{d+1}>0
, then
\overline{D}
is big.

We have the following height inequality generalizing
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.3.5 (2)]
.

Proposition 5.2.5
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g),\overline{M}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. If
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
and
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
is big, then for any
c>0
, there is
\epsilon\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
and a Zariski open, dense subset
V
of
U
such that

h_{\overline{D}}(x)\geq\epsilon h_{\overline{M}}(x)+\frac{(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}}{(d+1)D^{d}}-c,\ \ \text{ for any $x\in V(\overline{K})$.}

Proof.

We can assume that
(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}>-\infty
. Let
c
be an arbitrary positive number and
\widetilde{c}\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
such that

\int_{\Omega}\widetilde{c}(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)=-\frac{(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}}{(d+1)D^{d}}+c.

Since
D
is nef and big, we have that
\mathrm{vol}(D)=D^{d}>0
by
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.2.2]
and

(\overline{D}(\widetilde{c})^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}=(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}+(d+1)\cdot\int_{\Omega}\widetilde{c}(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)\cdot D^{d}=(d+1)cD^{d}>0.

Then
\overline{D}(\widetilde{c})
is big by
Corollary

5.2.4
. By
Theorem

4.6.1

(16)
, there is
\epsilon>0
and a Zariski open, dense subvariety
V
of
U
such that

h_{\overline{D}(\widetilde{c})}=h_{\overline{D}}(x)+\int_{\Omega}\widetilde{c}(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega)\geq\epsilon h_{\overline{M}}(x),\ \ \text{ for any $x\in V(\overline{K})$.}

This completes the proof.
∎

6.
Equidistribution on quasi-projective varieties over classical adelic curves and comparison with classical volumes

In our final section, we obtain an equidistribution result for compactified metrized divisors on quasi-projective varieties over the classical global fields. We fix
C
to be the spectrum of the ring of integers of a number field
K
or a regular projective curve over a field
k
with function field
K
, and denote the corresponding adelic curve given in
2.6.1
by
S=(K,\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
. Notice that
S
has the strong tensorial minimal slope property of level
\geq C_{0}
for some
C_{0}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
when
K
is a number field;
S
has the Minkowski property of level
\geq C_{0}
for some
C_{0}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
when
K
is the function field of a curve, see
2.5.1
. We also fix a
d
-dimensional normal quasi-projective variety
U
over
K
and an admissible flag
Y_{\bullet}
of
U_{\overline{K}}
.

6.1.
Compactified
S
-metrized
\mathrm{YZ}
-divisors

Recall the space of compactified
S
-metrized divisors
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, the relatively nef cone
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}
and the arithmetically nef cone
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
defined in
3.3.3
.

We will prove the arithmetic Hilbert-Samuel formula for the relatively nef compactified
S
-metrized
\mathrm{YZ}
-divisors which form a subset
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
of
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}
. To introduce such divisors, we follow the process similar as
3.3.3
.

6.1.1
.

Let
X
be a projective variety over
K
. A
projective
C
-model
of
X
is a flat, integral scheme
\mathcal{X}
projective over
C
with generic fiber
X
. We say
(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
is a
model
S
-metrized
(
\mathbb{Q}
-
)
divisor
if it is induced by a pairing
(\mathcal{D},g_{\infty})
on a projective
C
-model
\mathcal{X}
of
X
where
\mathcal{D}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier divisor on
\mathcal{X}
and
g_{\infty}=\{g_{\omega}\}_{\omega\in\Omega_{\infty}}
is a family of smooth Green functions for
D
on
X^{\mathrm{an}}_{\omega}
for each
\omega\in\Omega_{\infty}
. Set
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({X})_{\mathrm{mo}}
to be the subspace of model
S
-metrized divisors in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({X})
.
We denote

N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}\coloneq\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({X})_{\mathrm{mo}}\cap N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X),\ \ N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}\coloneq\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({X})_{\mathrm{mo}}\cap N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(X)

(see
3.3.2
for
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X),N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(X)
).

6.1.2
.

The space of
model
S
-metrized
(
\mathbb{Q}
-
)
divisors
on
U
is defined as the limit

\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}\coloneq\varinjlim_{X}\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}},

where
X
runs through all projective
K
-models of
U
. Similarly, we also denote

N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}\coloneq\varinjlim_{X}N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}},\ \ N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}\coloneq\varinjlim_{X}N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}

Let
T
be the subset of weak boundary divisors in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
. For a weak boundary divisor
\overline{B}\in T
, we have a
\overline{B}
-boundary topology
on
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
, see
3.3.3
,
and denote by
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{d_{\overline{B}}}_{\mathrm{mo}}
(resp.
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}^{d_{\overline{B}}}
, resp.
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}^{d_{\overline{B}}}
) the completion of
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
(resp.
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
, resp.
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
) with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology. The space of
compactified
S
-metrized
\mathrm{YZ}
-divisors
is defined as

\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}\coloneq\varinjlim_{\overline{B}\in T}\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{d_{\overline{B}}}_{\mathrm{mo}}.

We also set

\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}\coloneq\varinjlim_{\overline{B}\in T}N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}^{d_{\overline{B}}},\ \ \widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}snef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}\coloneq\varinjlim_{\overline{B}\in T}N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}^{d_{\overline{B}}}

\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}int}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}\coloneq\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}-\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}},

\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}int}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}\coloneq\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}snef}}-\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}snef}},

and denote by
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
(resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
)
the closure of
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
(resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}snef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
) in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}int}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
(resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}int}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
) with respect to the finite subspace topology. The elements of
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}int}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
(resp.
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
,
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
,
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}int}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
,
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}snef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
,
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
) are called
relatively integrable
(resp.
strongly relatively nef
,
relatively nef
,
arithmetically integrable
,
strongly arithmetically nef
,
arithmetically nef
) compactified
S
-metrized
\mathrm{YZ}
-divisors of
U
.
We have that

\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{cpt}}\subset\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}\subset\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)

and a forgetting homomorphism

\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}\to\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}},

where
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
is the space of compactified (geometric) divisors of
U
defined in
3.1.1
. As
3.3.3
, from now on we write an element
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
as
(D,g)
with
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
and
g
an
S
-measurable, locally
S
-bounded
S
-Green function for
D|_{U}
.

Remark 6.1.3
.

Our definition above is slightly different from Yuan-Zhang’s definition given in
[
YZ21
, §2.4, §2.5.3]
when
K
is a number field, their space of adelic (compactified) divisors on
U
is a subspace of
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
, see
[
CG24
, Section 8]
. However, it is not hard to show that the results for (classical) arithmetic volumes (see
Definition

6.2.1
below) in
[
YZ21
, Section 5]
can be generalized to
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
.

Remark 6.1.4
.

Obviously, we have that
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}\subset\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}\cap\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}
, it is natural to ask if the converse holds or not, i.e. if
\overline{D}
is a relative nef compactified
S
-metrized divisor which is also a compactified
S
-metrized
\mathrm{YZ}
-divisor, is
\overline{D}
a compactified
S
-metrized
\mathrm{YZ}
-divisor?

6.2.
Classical arithmetic volume

In this subsection, we compare the arithmetic volume of a compactified
S
-metrized
\mathrm{YZ}
-divisor
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)
defined in
Definition

4.2.2
and the classical one defined in
[
YZ21
, Definition 5.1.3]
.
The comparison relies on the results given in §
2.6
. Recall the definition of purification in
2.3.1
, and the notion of generically trivial
S
-normed vector spaces, coherent
S
-normed vector space in
Definition

2.6.3
.
The space of global sections of an adelic line bundle over a projective variety with sup-norms in
[
CM20
]
is not necessarily generically trivial. It is similar for quasi-projective case. Recall the space of auxiliary sections
H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D})
of a compactified
S
-metrized divisor defined in
Definition

4.2.2
, it is an adelic vector bundle on
S
by
Proposition

4.1.5
. We introduce a subspace of
H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D})
which will naturally give rise to coherent adelic vector bundles.

Definition 6.2.1
.

Let
\overline{D}\in{\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}}
. We define

H^{0}_{\mathrm{YZ}}(U,\overline{D})\coloneq\{s\in H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D})\mid\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}\leq 1\ \text{for almost all}\ \omega\in\Omega\},

where
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup}=({\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,\omega})_{\omega\in\Omega}
is the family of sup-norms on
H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D})
, see
4.1.1
.
Since
(H^{0}_{+}(U,\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup})
is an adelic vector bundle, we have that
(H^{0}_{\mathrm{YZ}}(U,\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup})
is a coherent adelic vector bundle. Set
\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},m}}\coloneq(H^{0}_{\mathrm{YZ}}(U,m\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,m})
, where
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,m}
is the family of sup-norms on
H^{0}_{\mathrm{YZ}}(U,m\overline{D})
. We get a graded
K
-algebra of coherent adelic vector bundles
\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\bullet}}=\{\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
. We define the
classical arithmetic volume
of
\overline{D}
as

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})\coloneq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\bullet}})=\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{h}^{0}(\overline{V_{m,\mathrm{YZ}}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}

(see
Definition

2.6.10
for the notion
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\cdot)
).
By
Lemma

2.6.4

(9)
, the purification
\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\mathrm{pur},m}}
of
\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},m}}
is a pure generically trivial adelic vector bundle. Set
\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\mathrm{pur},\bullet}}\coloneq\{\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\mathrm{pur},m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
. We define the
classical arithmetic
\chi
-volume
of
\overline{D}
as

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})\coloneq\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{\chi(\overline{V_{m,\mathrm{YZ},\mathrm{pur}}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}

(see
Definition

2.6.10
for the notion
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\chi}(\cdot)
).
Notice that
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\mathrm{pur},\bullet}})
by
Remark

2.6.9
.

Remark 6.2.2
.

Let
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
. Notice that our definition of
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})
coincide with the one in
[
YZ21
, Definition 5.1.3 (2)]
. Following a similar statement as
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.2.1]
, we know that the limit
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})=\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{h}^{0}(\overline{V_{m,\mathrm{YZ}}})}{m^{d+1}/(d+1)!}
exists and if a sequence of model
S
-metrized divisor
(\overline{D_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}\subset\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
converges to
\overline{D}
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology for some weak boundary divisor
\overline{B}
in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
, then

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})=\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D_{j}}).

Example 6.2.3
.

Assume that
K
is a number field. Let
X
be a projective variety over
K
,
\mathcal{X}
a projective
C
-model of
X
with the structure morphism
\varphi\colon\mathcal{X}\to C
, and
\overline{\mathcal{D}}=(\mathcal{D},g_{\infty})
a metrized divisor on
\mathcal{X}
corresponding to a line bundle with smooth metrics. We can assume that
\mathcal{X}
is integrally closed in
X
. Let
H^{0}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})

be the space of global sections of
\mathcal{L}\coloneq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{D})
which is an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-module. Then
H^{0}(X,D)=H^{0}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}K
. We consider an
S
-normed
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\overline{\mathcal{D}}}
on
H^{0}(X,D)
defined as follows:

•

For any
\omega\in\Omega_{\infty}
and
s\in H^{0}(X,D)\otimes_{K}K_{\omega}
, we set

\|s\|_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\omega}\coloneq\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}=\sup\limits_{x\in X_{\omega}^{\mathrm{an}}}\|s(x)\|_{\omega}.

•

For any
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
and
s\in H^{0}(X,D)\otimes_{K}K_{\omega}
, we set

\|s\|_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\omega}\coloneq\inf\{|\alpha|_{\omega}\mid\alpha\in K_{\omega}^{\times},s\in\alpha(H^{0}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}K_{\omega}^{\circ})\}.

On the other hand,
\overline{\mathcal{D}}
determines an element
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}
, and we have a family of sup-norms
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup}=\{{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,\omega}\}_{\omega\in\Omega}
on
H^{0}(X,D)
, see
4.1.1
. Then the following statements hold:

(18)

The
S
-norm vector space
(H^{0}(X,D),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\overline{\mathcal{D}}})
is a pure generically trivial adelic vector bundle.

(19)

The purification of
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup}
is
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\overline{\mathcal{D}}}
, and
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,\omega}={\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\omega}
for all but finitely many
\omega\in\Omega
(in fact,
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,\omega}={\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\omega}
if the special fiber of
\mathcal{X}
over
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
is reduced). In particular,
H^{0}_{\mathrm{YZ}}(X,\overline{D})=H^{0}_{+}(X,\overline{D})=H^{0}(X,D)
, and
(H^{0}(X,D),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup})
is a generically trivial adelic vector bundle.

(20)

Recall the arithmetic volume
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})
and arithmetic
\chi
-volume
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})
of
\overline{D}
defined in
Definition

2.5.5
. Write
\overline{V_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\bullet}}=\{(H^{0}(X,mD),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m\overline{\mathcal{D}}})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
. Then

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{V_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\bullet}})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{V_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\bullet}}),

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\chi}(\overline{V_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\bullet}})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{V_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\bullet}}).

Notice that
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{V_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\bullet}})
(resp.
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\chi}(\overline{V_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\bullet}})
) is exactly the arithmetic volume (resp. arithmetic
\chi
-volume) of
\overline{\mathcal{D}}
defined
[
Mor08
, Section 4]
(resp.
[
Yua09
, §1.3]
), see also
[
BMPS16
, Definition 3.14]
.

When
K
is a function field of a curve over a field
k
, with the same notation, the module
H^{0}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})
is replaced by
\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{D})
on
C
and we can define
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\overline{D}}
on
H^{0}(X,D)
similarly. Then the statements
(18)
,
(19)
,
(20)
also hold.

We will show
(18)
,
(19)
,
(20)
. For
(18)
, notice that
H^{0}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}K_{\omega}^{\circ}
is a lattice of
H^{0}({X},{D})\otimes_{K}K_{\omega}
in the sense of
[
CM20
, Definition 1.1.23]
, then
(H^{0}(X,D),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\overline{\mathcal{D}}})
is pure by
Remark

2.3.3
.
Write
\varphi\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow C
the structure morphism. Since
\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{D})
is free locally around the generic point of
C
, by choosing an open subset
W\subset C
and an
\mathcal{O}_{W}
-basis
\mathbf{e}
of
\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{D})
on
W
, it can be seen easily that
(H^{0}(X,D),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\overline{\mathcal{D}}})
is generically trivial given by the basis
\mathbf{e}
.

For
(19)
, by
[
BE21
, Proposition 6.3]
, for any
\omega\in\Omega
, if the special fiber of
\mathcal{X}
over
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
is reduced, then
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\omega}={\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,\omega}
. Hence
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\omega}={\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,\omega}
for all but finitely many
\omega\in\Omega
, this implies that
H^{0}_{\mathrm{YZ}}(X,\overline{D})=H^{0}_{+}(X,\overline{D})=H^{0}(X,D)
, and
(H^{0}(X,D),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup})
is a generically trivial adelic vector bundle. Moreover, since
\mathcal{X}
is assumed to be integrally closed in
X
, by
[
YZ21
, Lemma 3.3.3]
, we have that

H^{0}(\mathcal{X},{\mathcal{D}})=\{s\in H^{0}(X,D)\mid\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}\leq 1\text{ for any $\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}$}\}.

Since
(H^{0}(X,D),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup})
is coherent, by
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.4.2]
, for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
, we have that

H^{0}(\mathcal{X},{\mathcal{D}})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}K^{\circ}_{\omega}=\{s\in H^{0}(X,D)\otimes_{K}K_{\omega}\mid\|s\|_{\sup,\omega}\leq 1\},

this implies that the purification of
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup}
is
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\overline{\mathcal{D}}}
by the definitions.

For
(20)
, by
(19)
and
Remark

2.6.9
, we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{V_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\bullet}}),\ \ \widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{V_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\bullet}}).

By
(18)
and
Proposition

2.6.12
, we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{V_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\bullet}})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{V_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\bullet}}),\ \ \widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\chi}(\overline{V_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\bullet}})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{V_{\overline{\mathcal{D}},\bullet}}).

By
[
CM20
, Theorem 7.5.9]
, the graded
K
-algebra of generically trivial adelic vector bundle
\{(H^{0}(X,m\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m,\sup})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is asymptotically pure, where
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{m,\sup}
is the sup-norm on
H^{0}(X,m\overline{D})
. By
(19)
and
Proposition

2.6.12
again, we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D}),\ \ \widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\chi}(\overline{D}).

This completes the proof of
(20)
.

Finally we can state the main result of this subsection which shows that the arithmetic volumes and the classical arithmetic volumes coincide.

Proposition 6.2.4
.

Let
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
. Then

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}).

The equality holds if
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{cpt}}
.

Proof.

Let
\overline{V_{\bullet}}=\{\overline{V_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\coloneq\{(H^{0}_{+}(X,\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
(resp.
\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\bullet}}=\{\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\coloneq\{(H^{0}_{\mathrm{YZ}}(X,\overline{D}),{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
), where
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\sup,m}
is the family of sup-norms on
H^{0}_{+}(U,m\overline{D})
. Let
\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\mathrm{pur},m}}
be the purification of
\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},m}}
, and
\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\mathrm{pur},\bullet}}\coloneq\{\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\mathrm{pur},m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
. By Remark
2.6.9
and
Proposition

2.6.12
, we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\mathrm{pur},\bullet}})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\mathrm{pur},\bullet}}).

Note that there is a canonical norm-contractive injection of adelic vector bundles

f_{m}\colon\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\mathrm{pur},m}}\xhookrightarrow{}\overline{V_{m}},

i.e. the operator norms
\|f_{m}\|_{\omega}\leq 1
at all
\omega\in\Omega
since
{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega}\leq{\|\hskip 4.30554pt\|}_{\omega,\mathrm{pur}}
. It is easy to deduce then from
[
CM20
, Proposition 4.3.18]
that

\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\mathrm{pur},m}})\leq\widehat{\deg}_{+}(\overline{V_{m}})

which clearly shows that
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{V_{\mathrm{YZ},\mathrm{pur},\bullet}})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})
. This proves the inequality.

If
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, we choose any model
S
-metrized divisor
\overline{D^{\prime}}
on some projective model of
U
with
\overline{D}\leq\overline{D^{\prime}}
. Then by
Example

6.2.3

(20)
, we have that
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D^{\prime}})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(´\overline{D^{\prime}})
. However the effectivity relation
\overline{D}\leq\overline{D^{\prime}}
shows by arguments just like before that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D^{\prime}})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D^{\prime}}).

Now choosing a sequence of such model
S
-metrized divisors
\overline{D^{\prime}}
converging to
\overline{D}
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology for some weak boundary divisor
\overline{B}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
, by the generalization of
[
YZ21
, Theorem 5.2.1]
, see Remark
6.2.2
, one has
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})
.
∎

Remark 6.2.5
.

Similarly, for
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, we have that
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{YZ}}(\overline{D})\leq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})
. We may expect that the equality holds if
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, but it is unknown.

Proposition

6.2.4
allows us to translate the results for in
[
YZ21
, § 5.2]
in the language of our paper (although
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
is slightly large than the group of adelic divisors defined in
[
YZ21
]
).

6.3.
Suitably approximating sequences

In this subsection, we construct suitable Cauchy sequences of compactified
S
-metrized divisors which will be crucial for obtaining arithmetic Hilbert-Samuel formula later.

Lemma 6.3.1
.

Let
\overline{{D}}=(D,g),\overline{D^{\prime}}=(D^{\prime},g^{\prime})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}
be given as a limit of a sequence in
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology for some boundary divisor
\overline{B}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)
. Assume that
{D}\geq{D}^{\prime}
in
\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
and
write

h\coloneq\max\{g,g^{\prime}\}
.

(21)

If we can choose
\overline{B}
in
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
, then
h
is an
S
-Green function for
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, and
(D,h)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
.

(22)

If we can choose
\overline{B}
in
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
and if additionally
(D,g)\in N^{\prime}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}^{d_{\overline{B}}}
, then
(D,h)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}snef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar as the one of
[
CG24
, Lemma 10.3]
. By
[
CG24
, Lemma 10.3]
,
h
is a
S
-Green function for
D\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
.

Step 1:
If
U=X
is projective and
\overline{D},\overline{D^{\prime}}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}
, let
c\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
such that
c_{\omega}=0
for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
and
c_{\omega}>0
for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\infty}
, then
(D,h)
is the limit of a decreasing sequence
(D,h_{n})
in
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}
with respect to
(0,c)
-boundary topology with
h_{n,\omega}=h_{\omega}
for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{D}}=(\mathcal{D},g_{\infty}),\overline{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}=(\mathcal{D}^{\prime},g_{\infty}^{\prime})
be pairs of Cartier divisors with Green functions on a projective
C
-model
\mathcal{X}
of
X
inducing
\overline{D},\overline{D^{\prime}}
, respectively. Since
D\geq D^{\prime}
, there is a finite subset
T
of
C
such that
\mathcal{D}|_{C\setminus T}\geq\mathcal{D}^{\prime}|_{C\setminus T}
, where
\mathcal{D}|_{C\setminus T}
(resp.
\mathcal{D}|_{C\setminus T})
is the restriction of
\mathcal{D}
(resp.
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
) on the
\mathcal{X}\times_{C}(C\setminus T)
. Then when
\omega\not\in T
, we have that
h_{\omega}=g_{\omega}
and when
\omega\in T\cap\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
, we have that
(D_{\omega},h_{\omega})\in N_{\mathrm{mo},\mathbb{Q}}(X_{\omega})
(see proof of
[
CG24
, Lemma 9.3]
). Then after replacing
\mathcal{X}
by its blowing up along a subscheme of
\coprod\limits_{\omega\in T\setminus\Omega_{\infty}}\mathcal{X}_{\omega}
where
\mathcal{X}_{\omega}
is the special fiber of
\mathcal{X}
over
\omega\in T\setminus\Omega_{\infty}
, we can find a divisor
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}
on
\mathcal{X}
inducing
(D_{\omega},h_{\omega})
for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\mathrm{fin}}
. When
\omega\in T\cap\Omega_{\infty}
, we have that
(D_{\omega},g_{\omega})\in\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(X_{\omega})_{\mathrm{nef}}
by
[
CG24
, Lemma 9.3]
. Moreover, it is the limit of a decreasing sequence in
N_{\mathrm{mo},\mathbb{Q}}(X_{\omega})
.
Then
(D,h)
is the limit of some decreasing sequence in
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}
with respect to
(0,c)
-boundary topology. This proves Step 1.

Step 2:
Under the assumptions from Step 1 and if
(D,g)\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}
, then
(D,h)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
.

By
[
CG24
, Lemma 10.3, Proposition 7.10 (iv)]
, we have that
(D,h)\in N^{\prime}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
, i.e.
S
-nef on
X
. From Step 1,
(D,h)
is the limit of a decreasing sequence
(D,h_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
in
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}
. Since
(D,h_{j})\geq(D,h)
and
(D,h)\in N^{\prime}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)
, we have that
(D,h_{j})\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}
. This proves Step 2.

Step 3:
(21)
holds.

Let
\overline{B}=(B,g_{B})\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)
be a weak boundary divisor, and
(\overline{D_{j}})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}},(\overline{D_{j}^{\prime}})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
sequences in
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
converging to
\overline{D}
,
\overline{D^{\prime}}
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology, respectively. We can assume that there is a positive constant
c_{0}
such that
g_{B,\omega}>c_{0}
for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\infty}
. Let
X_{j}
be a projective model of
U
such that
\overline{D}_{j}=(D_{j},g_{j}),\overline{D_{j}^{\prime}}=(D_{j}^{\prime},g_{j}^{\prime})\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X_{j})_{\mathrm{mo}}
. Since
D\geq D^{\prime}
, after adding small multiples of
\overline{B}
to
\overline{D}_{j}
, we can assume that
D_{j}\geq D_{j}^{\prime}
. By Step 1, we have that
(D_{j},\max\{g_{j},g_{j}^{\prime}\})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X_{j})_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
and
(D_{j},\max\{g_{j},g_{j}^{\prime}\})
converges to
(D,h)
in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
with respect to
\overline{B}
-boundary topology. By Step 1, we can choose an element
\overline{D_{j}^{\prime}}
in
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X_{j})_{\mathrm{mo}}
closed to
(D_{j},\max\{g_{j},g_{j}^{\prime}\})
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology (notice that
g_{B,\omega}>c_{0}>0
on
U^{\mathrm{an}}_{\omega}
for any
\omega\in\Omega_{\infty}
). Then
\overline{D_{j}^{\prime}}
converges to
(D,h)
in
\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
with respect to
\overline{B}
-boundary topology. This proves Step 3.

Step 4:
(22)
holds .

We can twist a boundary divisor
\overline{B}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
with a positive constant at some place
\omega\in\Omega
, and assume that
\overline{B}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(U)
. Then Step 4 follows from the same proof as in Step 3 relying now on Step 2 instead of Step 1.
∎

Remark 6.3.2
.

We can obtain a weak boundary divisor
\overline{B}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
by shrinking
U
. The shrinking procedure is useful in the following situation: as
Remark

3.2.4
, by shrinking
U
we may assume that finitely many given relatively nef (resp. arithmetically nef)
\mathrm{YZ}
-divisors become strongly relatively nef (resp. strongly arithmetically nef).

Lemma 6.3.3
.

Let
\overline{D_{0}}=(D_{0},g_{0}),\dots,\overline{D_{d}}=({D}_{d},g_{d})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}
and
\overline{D_{0}^{\prime}}=({D}_{0},g_{0}^{\prime}),\penalty\dots,\overline{D_{d}^{\prime}}=({D}_{d},g_{d}^{\prime})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}snef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
. Assume that for any
j=0,\dots,d
,

•

there is a boundary divisor
\overline{B}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
and a sequence in
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
(resp.
N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
) converging to
\overline{D_{j}}
(resp.
\overline{D_{j}^{\prime}}
);

•

we have that
g_{j,\omega}=g_{j,\omega}^{\prime}
for all but finitely many
\omega\in\Omega
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, set

h_{j,m}\coloneq\max\{g_{j},g_{j}^{\prime}-m\}.

Then the following statements hold.

(23)

For each
j=0,\dots,d
, the function
h_{j,m}
is an
S
-Green function for
{D}_{j}\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{cpt}}
and
\overline{D_{j,m}}\coloneq({D}_{j},h_{j,m})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}
.

(24)

For each
j=0,\dots,d
, the sequence
(\overline{D_{j,m}})_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
decreasingly converges to
\overline{D_{j}}
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology for some weak boundary divisor
\overline{B}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
.

(25)

We have that

\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}(\overline{D_{0,m}}\cdots\overline{D_{d,m}}\mid U)_{S}=(\overline{D_{0}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}}\mid U)_{S}.

(26)

For any
\overline{E}=(E,f)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}int}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
with underlying compactified (geometric) divisor
E=0\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, we have that

(66)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}(\overline{D_{1,m}}\cdots\overline{D_{d,m}}\cdot\overline{E}\mid U)_{S}=\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}\nu(\omega)\int_{U_{\omega}}f_{\omega}\,c_{1}(\overline{D_{1,\omega}})\wedge\cdots\wedge c_{1}(\overline{D_{d,\omega}}).

Proof.

(23)
Let
T\subset\Omega
be a finite subset such that for any
1\leq j\leq d
and
\omega\in\Omega
,
g_{j,\omega}\neq g_{j,\omega}
implies that
\omega\in T
. Then for any
m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
and
\omega\not\in\Omega\setminus T
, we have that
g_{j,\omega}\geq g_{j,\omega}^{\prime}-m
, and
h_{j,m,\omega}=g_{j,\omega}
. By
Lemma

6.3.1

(21)
, we know that
h_{j,m}
is an
S
-Green function for
{D}_{j}\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}({U})_{\mathrm{cpt}}
, and
\overline{D_{j,m}}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
.

(24)
Obviously, we have that
(\overline{D_{j,m}})_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
is decreasing, as the proof of
[
CG24
, Lemma 10.6]
, it is not hard to show
(24)
holds similarly.

(25)

Recall the definition of
E(\mathbf{g}^{\prime},\mathbf{g})
in
3.4.2
. By
(24)
and
[
CG24
, Theorem 10.9 (v)]
, we have that

\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}E(\mathbf{g}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}_{m})=E(\mathbf{g}^{\prime},\mathbf{g}).

Hence

\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}(\overline{D_{0,m}}\cdots\overline{D_{d,m}}\mid U)_{S}=(\overline{D_{0}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}}\mid U)_{S}.

This proves
(25)
.

(26)
Write
\overline{E}=\overline{E_{1}}-\overline{E_{2}}
with
\overline{E_{1}}=(E_{1},f_{1}),\overline{E_{2}}=(E_{2},f_{2})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
. Since
E=0
, we have that
E_{1}=E_{2}\in\widetilde{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{nef}}
. We may assume that
[f_{1}]\leq[f_{2}]
in the sense of
3.4.1
. Otherwise, we consider
f_{3}\coloneq\max\{f_{1},f_{2}\}
which is still a Green function for
E_{1}=E_{2}
and
(E_{1},f_{3})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}
(see
Lemma

6.3.1

(22)
),
[f_{1}]\leq[f_{3}],[f_{2}]\leq[f_{3}]
, then (
66
) for
\overline{E_{1}}-\overline{E_{3}}
and for
\overline{E_{2}}-\overline{E_{3}}
implies (
66
) for
\overline{E}=\overline{E_{1}}-\overline{E_{2}}
, i.e.
(26)
holds. After assigning
\overline{D_{0}}=\overline{E_{1}}
and
\overline{D_{0}}=\overline{E_{2}}
, notice that
f_{1}=\max\{f_{1},f_{1}-m\},f_{2}=\max\{f_{2},f_{2}-m\}
, by
(25)
, we have that

(67)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}(\overline{D_{1,m}}\cdots\overline{D_{d,m}}\cdot\overline{E_{1}}\mid U)_{S}=(\overline{D_{1}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}}\cdot\overline{E_{1}}\mid U)_{S}.

By
3.4.2

(68)

\displaystyle(\overline{D_{1}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}}\cdot\overline{E_{1}}\mid U)_{S}=(\overline{D_{1}^{\prime}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}^{\prime}}\cdot\overline{E_{1}}\mid U)_{S}+E((g_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,g_{d}^{\prime},f_{1}),(g_{1},\cdots,g_{d},f_{1})).

Similarly, we have that

(69)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}(\overline{D_{1,m}}\cdots\overline{D_{d,m}}\cdot\overline{E_{2}}\mid U)_{S}=(\overline{D_{1}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}}\cdot\overline{E_{2}}\mid U)_{S}.

(70)

\displaystyle(\overline{D_{1}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}}\cdot\overline{E_{2}}\mid U)_{S}=(\overline{D_{1}^{\prime}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}^{\prime}}\cdot\overline{E_{2}}\mid U)_{S}+E((g_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,g_{d}^{\prime},f_{2}),(g_{1},\cdots,g_{d},f_{2})).

We take (
68
)
-
(
70
) and substitute it into (
67
)
-
(
69
), then

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}(\overline{D_{1,m}}\cdots\overline{D_{d,m}}\cdot\overline{E}\mid U)_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\overline{D_{1}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}}\cdot\overline{E_{1}}\mid U)_{S}-(\overline{D_{1}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}}\cdot\overline{E_{2}}\mid U)_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\overline{D_{1}^{\prime}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}^{\prime}}\cdot\overline{E_{1}}\mid U)_{S}-(\overline{D_{1}^{\prime}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}^{\prime}}\cdot\overline{E_{2}}\mid U)_{S}+E((g_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,g_{d}^{\prime},f_{1}),(g_{1},\cdots,g_{d},f_{1}))

\displaystyle-E((g_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,g_{d}^{\prime},f_{2}),(g_{1},\cdots,g_{d},f_{2})).

By
[
CG24
, Theorem 11.2]
and our assumption that
[f_{1}]\leq[f_{2}]
, we have that

(\overline{D_{1}^{\prime}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}^{\prime}}\cdot\overline{E_{1}}\mid U)_{S}-(\overline{D_{1}^{\prime}}\cdots\overline{D_{d}^{\prime}}\cdot\overline{E_{2}}\mid U)_{S}+E((g_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,g_{d}^{\prime},f_{2}),(g_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,g_{d}^{\prime},f_{1})).

Then

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}(\overline{D_{1,m}}\cdots\overline{D_{d,m}}\cdot\overline{E}\mid U)_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E((g_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,g_{d}^{\prime},f_{2}),(g_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,g_{d}^{\prime},f_{1}))+E((g_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,g_{d}^{\prime},f_{1}),(g_{1},\cdots,g_{d},f_{1}))

\displaystyle-E((g_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,g_{d}^{\prime},f_{2}),(g_{1},\cdots,g_{d},f_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E((g_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,g_{d}^{\prime},f_{2}),(g_{1},\cdots,g_{d},f_{1}))-E((g_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,g_{d}^{\prime},f_{2}),(g_{1},\cdots,g_{d},f_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E((g_{1},\cdots,g_{d},f_{2}),(g_{1},\cdots,g_{d},f_{1}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}\nu(\omega)\int_{U_{\omega}}f_{\omega}\,c_{1}(\overline{D_{1,\omega}})\wedge\cdots\wedge c_{1}(\overline{D_{d,\omega}}),

the second and third equality are from
[
CG24
, Proposition 10.10]
. This completed the proof of
(26)
.
∎

6.4.
Positive intersection product

We recall the
positive intersection product
defined in
[
Bis23b
, Definition 3.2]
.

6.4.1
.

Let
X
be a projective variety over
K
. We say a model
S
-metrized divisor
\overline{A}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}
is
free
if
\overline{A}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}
(see
6.1.1
) and there is
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
such that
n\overline{A}
is with
\mathbb{Z}
-coefficients induced by some semiample divisor
\mathcal{A}
on some projective
C
-model of
X
.

Let
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
and
\overline{E}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
. If
\overline{D}
is big, the
positive intersection product
of
\overline{D}
and
\overline{E}
is defined as

\langle\overline{D}^{d}\rangle\cdot\overline{E}\coloneq\sup\limits_{(\pi,\overline{A})}\{\overline{A}^{d}\cdot\pi^{*}\overline{E}\},

where
(\pi,\overline{A})
runs over all tuples such that
\pi\colon U^{\prime}\to U
is a birational morphism, and
\overline{A}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U^{\prime})_{\mathrm{mo}}
is induced by a free model
S
-metrized divisor on some projective model of
U^{\prime}
such that
\pi^{*}\overline{D}-\overline{A}\geq 0
. By linearity, if
\overline{E}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}int}}
,
\langle\overline{D}^{d}\rangle\cdot\overline{E}
is well-defined, see
[
Bis23b
, Lemma 3.10]
. Notice that the positive intersection product is stable under birational pull-back, see
[
Bis23b
, Lemma 3.3]
.

Lemma 6.4.2
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S}(U)^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef},\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}
with
D
is big, and
(0,f)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}int}}^{\mathrm{YZ}}
. If
\inf\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)>0
, then

(71)

\langle\overline{D}^{d}\rangle\cdot(0,f)=\sum\limits_{\omega\in\Omega}\nu(\omega)\int_{U^{\mathrm{an}}_{\omega}}f_{\omega}\,c_{1}(\overline{D}_{\omega})^{d}.

Proof.

Write
\overline{E}=(0,f)
. As the proof of Step 3 in the proof of
Theorem

5.2.2
, after shrinking
U
(notice that the both sides of (
71
) are stable under birational base change), there is a sequence
(\overline{D_{m}})_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}\subset\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}snef}}
and a sequence
(c_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}\subset\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)_{\geq 0}
satisfying the following properties:

•

(\overline{D_{m}})_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}}
is decreasing converging to
\overline{D}
with respect to the
\overline{B}
-boundary topology for some weak boundary divisor
\overline{B}\in N_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{mo}}
and

(72)

\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}(\overline{D_{m}}^{d}\cdot\overline{E}\mid U)_{S}=\sum\limits_{\omega\in\Omega}\nu(\omega)\int_{U_{\omega}^{\mathrm{an}}}f_{\omega}\,c_{1}(\overline{D})^{d}

by
Lemma

6.3.3
;

•

for any
m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
,
c_{m}(\omega)=0
for all but finitely many
\omega\in\Omega
;

•

\overline{D_{m}}(c_{m})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{ar\text{-}snef}}
, then

\langle\overline{D_{m}}(c_{m})^{d}\rangle\cdot\overline{E}=(\overline{D_{m}}(c_{m})^{d}\cdot\overline{E}\mid U)_{S}

by the linearity and
[
Bis23b
, Corollary 3.8]
.

We claim that

\langle\overline{D_{m}}(c_{m})^{d}\rangle\cdot\overline{E}=\langle\overline{D_{m}}^{d}\rangle\cdot\overline{E}.

If this holds, notice that

(\overline{D_{m}}(c_{m})^{d}\cdot\overline{E}\mid U)_{S}=(\overline{D_{m}}^{d}\cdot\overline{E}\mid U)_{S}

(see
[
CG24
, Theorem 11.3]
), then

\langle\overline{D_{m}}^{d}\rangle\cdot\overline{E}=(\overline{D_{m}}^{d}\cdot\overline{E}\mid U)_{S}.

Taking
m\to\infty
on both sides, by
[
Bis23b
, Lemma 3.6]
and (
72
), the lemma holds. It remains to show the claim. To show the claim, we consider the functions
\varphi,\psi
on
\mathbb{R}
as follows:

\varphi(t)\coloneq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D_{m}}(c_{m})+t\overline{E}),\ \ \psi(t)\coloneq\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D_{m}}+t\overline{E}).

Since
\overline{D_{m}}
is decreasing converging to
\overline{D}
, then

G_{\overline{D_{m}}(c_{m})}\geq G_{\overline{D_{m}}}\geq G_{\overline{D}}\geq\inf\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)>0.

Notice that there is
b\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)_{\geq 0}
such that
b(\omega)=0
for all but finitely many
\omega\in\Omega
and
\sup\limits_{x\in U^{\mathrm{an}}_{\omega}}\{|f_{\omega}(x)|\}\leq b(\omega)
for any
\omega\in\Omega
.
Then for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\overline{D_{m}}-|t|(0,b)\leq\overline{D_{m}}+t\overline{E}\leq\overline{D_{m}}+|t|(0,b),

G_{\overline{D_{m}}+t\overline{E}}\geq G_{\overline{D_{m}}-|t|(0,b)}=G_{\overline{D_{m}}}-|t|\int_{\Omega}b(\omega)\,\nu(d\omega).

Then by
Theorem

4.3.2
, when
|t|
is small enough, we have that

\displaystyle\varphi(t)=

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D_{m}}(c_{m})+t\overline{E})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D_{m}}(c_{m})+t\overline{E})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D_{m}}+t\overline{E})+\sum\limits_{\omega\in\Omega}c_{m}(\omega)\nu(\omega)\cdot D^{d}_{m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi(t)+\sum\limits_{\omega\in\Omega}c_{m}(\omega)\nu(\omega)\cdot D^{d}_{m}.

Taking the derivatives on both sides of
\varphi(t)=\psi(t)+\sum\limits_{\omega\in\Omega}c_{m}(\omega)\nu(\omega)\cdot D^{d}_{m}
, then
[
Bis23b
, Theorem 3.13]
implies our claim. This finishes the proof of the lemma.
∎

6.5.
Equidistribution

We quickly recall the setting in which we prove the equidistribution.

6.5.1
.

Let
(x_{m})_{m\in I}\subset U(\overline{K})
a net of geometric points.

•

We say that
(x_{m})_{m\in I}\subset U(\overline{K})
is
generic
if it is Zariski dense in the Zariski topology of
U
.

•

We say that
(x_{m})_{m\in I}\subset U(\overline{K})
is
small with respect to
\overline{D}
if

\lim_{m\in I}h_{\overline{D}}(x_{m})=\frac{\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})}{(d+1)\mathrm{vol}(D)}.

•

Let
v\in\Omega
, and
\mu
a measure on
U^{\mathrm{an}}_{v}
.
We say that the Galois orbit of
(x_{m})_{m\in I}

equidistributes in
U_{v}^{\mathrm{an}}
with respect to
\mu
if

\lim_{m\in I}\frac{1}{\#O(x_{m})}\sum_{\sigma\in\text{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)}f(\sigma(x_{m}))=\int_{U^{\mathrm{an}}_{v}}f\,d\mu

for every compactly supported function
f
on
U_{v}^{\mathrm{an}}
, where
O(x_{m})
denotes the Galois orbit of
x_{m}
under the action of
\text{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)
and
U^{\mathrm{an}}_{v}
denotes the Berkovich analytification of
U\times_{K}K_{v}
. See
[
BPRS19
, Section 2]
for more details.

Remark 6.5.2
.

Let
(x_{m})_{m\in I}\subset U(\overline{K})
a net of geometric points, and
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)_{\mathrm{cpt}}
with
D
big. By
Theorem

4.5.3
, we have that

\limsup_{m\in I}h_{\overline{D}}(x_{m})\geq\zeta_{\mathrm{ess}}(\overline{D})\geq\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})\geq\frac{\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})}{(d+1)\mathrm{vol}(D)}.

If
(x_{m})_{m\in I}
is small with respect to
\overline{D}
, then
G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)=\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})
on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
. In particular, if this happen, then
\inf\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G(\lambda)=\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>-\infty
and
