\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})=\zeta_{\mathrm{ess}}(\overline{D}).

Furthermore, let us consider the following statements which often appear as hypothesis in equidistribution theorem:

(n)

(x_{m})_{m\in I}
is small with respect to
\overline{D}
;

(o)

\widehat{\mu}_{\min}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>-\infty
and

\lim\limits_{m\in I}h_{\overline{D}}(x_{m})=\frac{\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}(\overline{D})}{(d+1)\mathrm{vol}(D)};

(p)

\overline{D}
is big and

\lim\limits_{m\in I}h_{\overline{D}}(x_{m})=\frac{\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})}{(d+1)\mathrm{vol}(D)};

(q)

\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef},\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}
and

\lim\limits_{m\in I}h_{\overline{D}}(x_{m})=\frac{(\overline{D}^{d+1}\mid U)_{S}^{d+1}}{(d+1)D^{d}}.

If
\widehat{\mu}_{\min}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>-\infty
, by
Theorem

4.3.2
, then
(n)

(o)
are equivalent. If
\overline{D}
is big, by
Corollary

4.3.5
, then
\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>0
. By
Lemma

3.5.4
,
Proposition

4.3.3

(13)
and
Theorem

4.3.2
, we have that

\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})=\frac{d!}{\mathrm{vol}(D)}\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})\,d\lambda\geq\frac{d!}{\mathrm{vol}(D)}\int_{\Delta(D)^{\circ}}\max\{G_{\overline{D})},0\}\,d\lambda=\frac{\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})}{(d+1)\mathrm{vol}(D)},

and the equality holds if and only if
G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)=\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})
on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
. Hence in this case,
\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D})
, then
(n)

(p)
are equivalent. If
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef},\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}
, by
Theorem

5.2.2
, then
(n)

(q)
are equivalent.

For any
v\in\Omega
, and
f_{v}\in C_{c}(U_{v}^{\mathrm{an}})
, we simply denote by
(0,f_{v})
the compactified metrized divisor
\overline{D}=(D,g)
of
U
such that
D
is trivial,
g_{v}=f_{v}
, and
g_{\omega}=0
if
\omega\in\Omega\setminus v
.

Theorem 6.5.3
.

Let
\overline{D}=(D,g)\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{cpt}}
with
D
big. Let
(x_{m})_{m\in I}
be a generic net of points which is small with respect to
\overline{D}
. Then for any
c\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
such that
\overline{D}(c)
is big, for any
v\in\Omega
and for any
f_{v}\in C_{c}(U_{v}^{\mathrm{an}}),
we have that

(73)

\displaystyle\lim_{m\in I}\frac{\nu(v)}{\#O(x_{m})}\sum_{\sigma\in\text{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)}f_{v}(\sigma(x_{m}))=\frac{\langle\overline{D}(c)^{d}\rangle\cdot(0,f_{v})}{\mathrm{vol}(D)}.

In particular, if
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef},\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}
, then
the Galois orbit of
(x_{m})_{m\in I}
equidistributes in
U^{\mathrm{an}}_{v}
with respect to to
\frac{1}{D^{d}}\cdot c_{1}(\overline{D}_{v})^{d}
.

Proof.

By
Remark

6.5.2
, we have that
G_{\overline{D}}(\lambda)\equiv\widehat{\mu}_{\max}^{\mathrm{asy}}(\overline{D})>-\infty
on
\Delta(D)^{\circ}
. Let
c\in\mathscr{L}^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\nu)
such that
\overline{D}(c)
is big. Then
\inf\limits_{\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}(c)}(\lambda)>0
.

Let
X
be a projective
K
-model of
U
. Recall the space
\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(X_{v})_{\mathrm{mo}}
in
3.2.2
. Set
\overline{E}\coloneq(0,f_{v})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}
. We consider functions on
\mathbb{R}
: for any
m\in I
,

(74)

\displaystyle\varphi_{m}(t)\coloneq h_{\overline{D}(c)+t\overline{E}}(x_{m})=h_{\overline{D}(c)}(x_{m})+\frac{\nu(v)\cdot t}{\#O(x_{m})}\sum_{\sigma\in\text{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)}f_{v}(\sigma(x_{m})),

\psi(t)\coloneq\frac{\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D}(c)+t\overline{E})}{(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}(D)}

(the last equality of (
74
) is from
[
BPRS19
, Proposition 2.3]
).
We prove the theorem in the following steps.

Step 1:
The following statements hold:

(18)

\varphi_{m}(t)
is concave and differentiable with derivative
\frac{\nu(v)}{\#O(x_{m})}\sum\limits_{\sigma\in\text{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)}f_{v}(\sigma(x_{m}))
at
t=0
;

(19)

\liminf\limits_{m\in I}\varphi_{m}(t)\geq\psi(t)
;

(20)

\lim\limits_{m\in I}\varphi_{m}(0)=\psi(0)
.

(18)
is from the definition. For
(19)
, since
(x_{m})_{m\in I}
is generic, we have that

\liminf\limits_{m\in I}h_{\overline{D}(c)+t\overline{E}}(x_{m})\geq\zeta_{\mathrm{ess}}(\overline{D}(c)+t\overline{E})\geq\frac{\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D}(c)+t\overline{E})}{(d+1)\cdot\mathrm{vol}(D)^{d}}

which proves
(19)
, here the last equality is from
Theorem

4.5.3
.
(20)
is from
Proposition

4.3.3

(11)
the assumption that
(x_{m})_{m\in I}
is small.

Step 2:
If
(0,f_{v})\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(X)_{\mathrm{mo}}
(i.e. locally
(0,f_{v})\in\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(X_{v})_{\mathrm{mo}}
), then
\psi(t)
is differentiable at
t=0
with derivative given by

\frac{d\psi}{dt}(0)=\frac{\langle\overline{D}(c)^{d}\rangle\cdot(0,f_{v})}{\mathrm{vol}(D)}.

Hence, by Step 2 and
[
BB10
, Lemma 7.6]
, we have that

(75)

\lim\limits_{m\in I}\frac{\nu(v)}{\#O(x_{m})}\sum_{\sigma\in\text{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)}f_{v}(\sigma(x_{m}))=\frac{\langle\overline{D}(c)^{d}\rangle\cdot(0,f_{v})}{\mathrm{vol}(D)}.

Let
b_{v}\coloneq\sup\limits_{x\in X_{v}^{\mathrm{an}}}\{|f_{v}(x)|\}<\infty
. For any
\omega\in\Omega\setminus\{v\}
, set
b_{\omega}=0

Write
b=(b_{\omega})_{\omega\in\Omega}
.
Let
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Then

\overline{D}(c)+t\overline{E}\geq\overline{D}(c)-|t|(0,b)

which implies that

G_{\overline{D}(c)+t\overline{E}}\geq G_{\overline{D}(c)}-|t|b_{v}\geq\inf_{\lambda\in\Delta(D)^{\circ}}G_{\overline{D}(c)}(\lambda)-|t|b_{v}

by
Proposition

4.3.3

(12)
. By
Theorem

4.3.2
, we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{num}}(\overline{D}(c)+t\overline{E})+c_{v}=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\overline{D}(c)+t\overline{E})

when
|t|
is small enough. By
[
Bis23b
, Theorem 3.13]
, we have that

\frac{d\psi}{dt}(0)=\frac{\langle\overline{D}(c)^{d}\rangle\cdot\overline{E}}{\mathrm{vol}(D)}.

Step 3:
(
73
) holds for any
f_{v}\in C_{c}(U_{v}^{\mathrm{an}})
.

To show the theorem is equivalent to show (
75
) holds for any
f_{v}\in C_{c}(U_{v}^{\mathrm{an}})
. Notice that
C_{c}(U_{v}^{\mathrm{an}})\subset C(X_{v}^{\mathrm{an}})
. By
[
Gub98
, Theorem 7.12]
and
[
Yua08
, Lemma 3.5]
, the set

\{f_{v}\in C(X_{v}^{\mathrm{an}})\mid(0,f_{v})\in\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(X_{v})_{\mathrm{mo}}\}

is dense in
C(X_{v}^{\mathrm{an}})
under the topology of uniform convergence. Hence it suffices to show (
75
) for
f_{v}\in C(X_{v}^{\mathrm{an}})
such that
(0,f_{v})\in\widehat{\Div}_{\mathbb{Q}}(X_{v})_{\mathrm{mo}}
. This is Step 2, so the theorem holds.

Step 4:
If
\overline{D}\in\widehat{\Div}_{S,\mathbb{Q}}(U)^{\mathrm{ar\text{-}nef},\mathrm{YZ}}_{\mathrm{rel\text{-}nef}}
, then
the Galois orbit of
(x_{m})_{m\in I}
equidistributes in
U^{\mathrm{an}}_{v}
with respect to to
\frac{1}{D^{d}}\cdot c_{1}(\overline{D}_{v})^{d}
.

This is from (
73
) and
Lemma

6.4.2
. Notice that
c_{1}(\overline{D}(c)_{v})^{d}=c_{1}(\overline{D}_{v})^{d}
.
∎
